# Test Catalog

Every automated test in the suite with a one-line description of what it verifies. When you add or change a test, update the matching entry here. The per-file sections below are authoritative; the counts are indicative.

## How to run

- **Unit** (no server): `composer test:unit` - protocol encode/parse, connection state/handshake/reconnect, subscriptions & backpressure, and JetStream/KV/ObjectStore/Services logic exercised against in-memory transport doubles (`tests/Support`).
- **Integration** (live server): `RUN_INTEGRATION=1 composer test:integration`, or `composer test:e2e` for the full Dockerised stack (TLS/auth/WebSocket variants). Real connect/auth/TLS/WebSocket, JetStream/KV/ObjectStore/Services round-trips, reconnect, heartbeat soak, multi-consumer concurrency, and `nats` CLI interop.
- **Behat** (live server): `composer test:bdd` - behaviour specs.

Indicative totals: 3249 unit tests, 152 integration tests, 48 Behat scenarios.

## Unit Tests (`tests/Unit/`)

### tests/Unit/AmpSocketTransportTest.php
- `testWriteWithoutSocketThrowsWhileReadAndCloseStaySafe` - Asserts write() without a connected socket throws TransportClosedException (a silent no-op would confirm frames that never reached any socket, #124), while readLine() still returns '' and close() stays idempotent.
- `testUpgradeTlsIsNoOpWhenTlsNotConfigured` - Asserts upgradeTls() short-circuits (no handshake) when no socket and no TLS context are configured, leaving readLine() returning ''.
- `testWithTlsContextReturnsOriginalContextWhenTlsNotRequired` - Asserts withTlsContext() returns the same ConnectContext unchanged for a non-TLS nats:// DSN with tlsRequired=false.
- `testWithTlsContextBuildsTlsContextFromTlsScheme` - Asserts a tls:// DSN makes withTlsContext() return a new (different) ConnectContext with TLS enabled.
- `testWithTlsContextUsesExplicitTlsOptions` - With explicit TLS options (peer override, no peer verify, CA/cert/key files, passphrase) asserts withTlsContext() returns a new ConnectContext instance, exercising the explicit-options path.
- `testConnectThrowsOnInvalidDsn` - Asserts connect() propagates a Throwable for an invalid DSN.
- `testNormalizeSocketUriRewritesTlsScheme` - Asserts normalizeSocketUri() rewrites tls:// to tcp:// and leaves tcp:// unchanged, while accepting nats:// directly as tcp://.
- `testReadLineThrowsTransportClosedOnPeerEof` - With a server that writes "hello\r\n" then closes, asserts readLine() returns the data and the subsequent read throws TransportClosedException on EOF (not collapsing to '').
- `testReadLineSurfacesReadTimeoutAsCancelledExceptionNotEof` - With a peer held open but silent, asserts a bounded readLine() whose TimeoutCancellation fires surfaces CancelledException through the returned future - not EOF/TransportClosedException and not '' - guarding the #163 inline readLine() (no async() wrapper) so the passed Cancellation still reaches the underlying read and a bounded read stays distinguishable from a peer close.
- `testUpgradeTlsThrowsWhenConnectedWithoutTlsContext` - With a held-open plaintext connection, asserts upgradeTls() fails fast with TlsRequiredException ("TLS upgrade requested but no TLS context") rather than leaving the socket plaintext.
- `testReadChunkSizeControlsMaxBytesPerRead` - Over a real loopback socket, writes a 256 KiB payload and asserts the configured `readChunkSizeBytes` bounds each read: a 128 KiB chunk size lets a single readLine() return more than 8 KiB (and uses fewer total reads), while an 8 KiB chunk size caps every read at 8 KiB; both deliver the full payload (#119). Falsifiable: without the setChunkSize() call the transport stays at Amp's 8 KiB default and the >8 KiB assertion fails.
- `testDialStoppedDuringTheRetryPauseEndsAtOnceAndLeavesNoSocket` - A dial to a refused port, stopped while Amp's retry connector pauses for 2 s after the refused first attempt, fails with CancelledException less than 1 s after the stop; after the pause, with the port listening again, write() still finds no socket. No timer places the stop: the test installs Amp's retry connector over `tests/Support/StopsDialOnRefusalConnector.php`, which stops the dial from a microtask queued when the attempt is refused, so the stop lands at the start of the pause however late timers fire and however slowly the attempt is refused (#176). A dial held by the pause ends about 2 s or more after the stop, and one that honours the stop ends in the same event-loop turn. The default connector is restored afterwards. The pause used to hold the dial, and a close waiting for it, for its full length.
- `testSocketOpenedAfterTheDialWasStoppedIsClosedNotInstalled` - With a global Amp connector that ignores the cancellation for 0.2 s (standing in for Amp's retry pause), a dial stopped at 0.05 s throws CancelledException; the socket the connector opens afterwards is closed at once (the server reads EOF) and never installed (write() finds no socket). The default connector is restored afterwards.

### tests/Unit/BasicJsonSchemaValidatorTest.php
- `testObjectTypeRejectsNonEmptyJsonList` - Asserts a non-empty JSON list (`[1,2,3]`) is rejected by a `type: object` schema even though PHP's assoc decoding turns both maps and lists into arrays, while a real map (`{"id":5}`) still validates and the empty `{}` stays accepted because `{}` and `[]` are indistinguishable after assoc decoding.
- `testRejectsInvalidJsonPayload` - Asserts validating a message with a malformed JSON body (`{invalid`) against an object schema returns the error `payload is not valid JSON`.
- `testRejectsMissingRequiredField` - Asserts a payload missing a required `id` field returns `$.id is required`.
- `testRejectsWrongPropertyType` - Asserts a payload where `id` is a string but the schema requires integer returns `$.id must be integer, got string`.
- `testAcceptsValidPayload` - Asserts a payload satisfying all required fields and property types validates successfully (returns null).
- `testValidatesAdditionalPrimitiveTypes` - Asserts boolean/number/null payloads validate against their matching primitive type schemas (null), and that a JSON object validated against `array` type yields `$ must be array, got array`.
- `testUnknownTypeIsIgnored` - Asserts a schema with an unrecognized `type` value (`custom-type`) is ignored and validation passes (returns null).
- `testRejectsObjectTypeWhenPayloadIsNotObject` - Asserts a plain JSON string payload validated against an `object` schema returns `$ must be object, got string`.
- `testIgnoresMalformedRequiredAndPropertiesSchemaNodes` - Asserts malformed schema nodes (a scalar `required`, a non-array property definition, a non-string property key) are tolerated and validation passes (returns null).
- `testSkipsPropertyNotPresentInObject` - Asserts an optional property declared in `properties` but absent from the payload and not in `required` is skipped without a type error (returns null).

### tests/Unit/BatchPublisherTest.php
- `testCommitPreflightRejectsPre212ServerBeforeAnyWrite` - With INFO advertising version 2.11.4, `commit()` on a 3-message batch throws UnsupportedFeatureException (feature allow_atomic, requiredVersion 2.12, serverVersion 2.11.4) from the version pre-flight with ZERO batch frames written - the scripted plain PubAck reply stays unconsumed, so no orphan start message can be stored (#152).
- `testCommitPreflightRejectsTwoSegmentPre212Version` - A two-segment INFO version ("2.11") parses as below 2.12 and the pre-flight rejects even a single-message batch before any write - previously its plain PubAck would have been silently accepted as a normal publish (#152).
- `testCommitProceedsOnPrereleaseOf212` - INFO version "2.12.0-beta.1" passes the pre-flight (numeric-prefix comparison, not semver precedence where a pre-release orders below its release) and a 2-message batch commits normally (batchCount=2, batchId="b-beta") (#152).
- `testCommitUnparseableVersionFallsThroughToReplyShapeDetection` - An unparseable INFO version ("synadia-custom") does not trip the pre-flight; the plain-PubAck start reply is caught by the reply-shape detection instead: UnsupportedFeatureException with serverVersion "synadia-custom" after exactly 1 batch write (#152 keeping #130 as defense in depth).
- `testCommitBeforeConnectSkipsVersionPreflightNullSafely` - Asserts `commit()` on a never-connected client (serverInfo() is null) skips the version pre-flight null-safely rather than dereferencing the missing ServerInfo, so the failure surfaces as the request layer's NatsException and not an UnsupportedFeatureException (#152).
- `testReplyShapeAbortMessageOmitsMissingServerVersionCleanly` - Asserts that when INFO omits `version`, the reply-shape abort's UnsupportedFeatureException message reads exactly "Atomic batch publish requires NATS server 2.12+ (connected server treated the batch as plain publishes)" - an unknown version leaves no blank token or dangling space in the wording (#152).
- `testCommitAbortsWhenBatchStartIsAcknowledgedAsPlainPublish` - Mixed-version cluster: the connected server advertises 2.12.1 (pre-flight passes) but the batch START is acknowledged with a normal PubAck (older JS leader storing it as a plain publish) - aborts with UnsupportedFeatureException (feature allow_atomic, server version reported) before any further batch message is published (#130).
- `testCommitRejectsMultiMessageAckWithoutBatchFields` - A multi-message commit acknowledged by a PubAck without batch id/count (nothing was committed as a batch) throws UnsupportedFeatureException instead of reporting success, with the INFO version at 2.12.1 and both batch writes asserted on the wire so the commit-leg guard (not the #152 pre-flight) is what fires (#130).
- `testSingleMessageBatchAcceptsPlainPubAck` - A single-message batch (trivially atomic) accepts a plain PubAck commit ack without batch fields (#130).
- `testCommitNormalizesNoRespondersTo503` - A no-responders reply to the commit request (single-message batch) surfaces as JetStreamException(503) ("No JetStream responder"), not a bare NatsException, matching jsRequest()'s taxonomy (#161).
- `testStartRequestNormalizesNoRespondersTo503` - A no-responders reply to the batch-START request (multi-message batch) likewise surfaces as JetStreamException(503); the batch aborts at start so no intermediate/commit frame is sent (#161).
- `testCommitSendsBatchHeadersAndParsesAck` - Commits a 3-message batch and asserts the parsed ack (batchCount=3, batchId="batch-xyz"), that exactly 3 writes carry `Nats-Batch-Id:batch-xyz`, the START (seq 1) is a request with no commit marker, the intermediate (seq 2) is fire-and-forget, and the commit (seq 3) is a request carrying `Nats-Batch-Commit:1`.
- `testCommitCoalescesIntermediatesIntoSingleWrite` - A 5-message commit performs exactly 3 batch transport writes: the start request, ONE coalesced write whose bytes are the exact concatenation of the intermediate HPUB frames (sequences 2-4, no reply inbox, staging order), and the commit request - instead of one awaited write per intermediate (#138).
- `testOversizedIntermediateThrowsBeforeAnyIntermediateWrite` - With the server advertising `max_payload:256`, an oversized intermediate (300-byte payload + batch headers) throws `ProtocolException` ("exceeds server max_payload") before ANY intermediate reaches the wire - only the start frame is written, no seq-2 frame and no commit marker appear in any write (#138).
- `testCommitRejectedAtStart` - When the batch-start request gets an error JSON reply, `commit()` throws `JetStreamException` ("atomic publish not enabled") and no commit marker nor seq-3 message is written (publish aborts at start).
- `testCommitEmptyBatchThrows` - Committing a batch with no staged messages throws `JetStreamException` ("Cannot commit an empty batch").
- `testBatchRejectsOversizedId` - Constructing a batch with a 65-character id throws `JetStreamException` ("Batch id must be between 1 and 64 characters").
- `testAddAfterCommitThrows` - Calling `add()` after a successful `commit()` throws `JetStreamException` ("Cannot add to an already-committed batch").
- `testCommitAbortSurfacesError` - A commit ack containing an error object surfaces as `JetStreamException` ("batch consistency check failed").
- `testAddExceedingMaxMessagesThrows` - Pre-filling the batch to `BatchPublisher::MAX_MESSAGES` (via reflection) then calling `add()` throws `JetStreamException` ("Atomic batch is limited to").
- `testCountReturnsNumberOfStagedMessages` - `count()` returns 0 initially and increments to 1 then 2 as messages are added.
- `testBatchIdReturnsConstructedId` - `batchId()` returns the id passed to the constructor ("my-explicit-id").
- `testDoubleCommitThrows` - First `commit()` succeeds (batchCount=1); a second `commit()` throws `JetStreamException` ("Batch already committed").
- `testCommitReleasesStagedPayloads` - `commit()` releases the staged payloads: the internal `messages` array is empty afterwards (reflection) and `count()` returns 0, while the full 3-message wire exchange still happens (#133).
- `testNonJsonStartReplyTreatedAsAccepted` - A non-empty, non-JSON start reply ("OK") is treated as accepted so publish continues, and the commit ack parses correctly (batchCount=2, batchId="batch-nonjson").
- `testMalformedCommitAckThrows` - A non-JSON commit ack reply throws `JetStreamException` ("Malformed atomic batch commit ack").

### tests/Unit/CloseIntentTest.php
A close wins over what is still in flight on the connection: a connect() racing it, a reconnect backing off, failing or writing its log line, and a read, write or heartbeat that fails on a connection the application has since closed and reopened. Uses the scripted server in `tests/Support/ReconnectingTransport.php`.
- `testErrorListenerThatReopensTheConnectionIsNotUndoneByTheFailedRead` - An error listener disconnects and reconnects when a read fails; the failed read's recovery then leaves the new connection alone: still epoch 1, no reconnect, events Connected, Closed, Connected.
- `testWriteFailingOnAReplacedConnectionDoesNotTearDownTheNewOne` - Over a transport that fails a write still pending on the old socket only after the reopen (TransportInterface allows it), a publish whose write was stalled across a disconnect() and connect() resolves: the stalled write fails only after the reopen, the new connection is not reconnected, and the frame goes out on the new one.
- `testHeartbeatFailingOnAReplacedConnectionLeavesTheNewOneAlone` - Over the same kind of transport, a heartbeat PING write stalled across a disconnect() and connect() (checked to be still pending after the reopen) fails on the old socket without reconnecting the new connection or stopping its heartbeat, which keeps sending PINGs.
- `testDisconnectDuringTheInitialDialWins` - disconnect() while connect() is dialling: the dial that completes afterwards is closed again, connect() fails with "Connect was aborted before the connection opened", the connection stays Closed with the one Closed event, and a later connect() works.
- `testDisconnectDuringTheInitialDialWinsOverAnAuthenticationFailure` - The same when the server then refuses the credentials: connect() fails with the abort, and the close is announced once.
- `testDisconnectDuringTheInitialConnectRetriesWinsAtOnce` - disconnect() while connect() waits to retry a refused first dial (retryOnFailedInitialConnect, reconnect disabled, 10 s backoff) ends it at once, in under 2 s rather than after the backoff, with the abort, without another dial, announced once.
- `testConnectAfterDisconnectDialsAfreshWhileTheReconnectItStoppedWasDialling` - disconnect() while a reconnect is held mid-dial returns in under 2 s (the held dial never ends by itself, so a disconnect() that waited for it would return only at the 10 s connect timeout that bounds that wait), having stopped the dial (dialsCancelled 1), and a connect() issued after it opens the connection. It used to fail with "Recovery was aborted before the connection opened" (in a synchronous application, every time).
- `testDisconnectWaitsForTheDialOfATransportThatCannotStopIt` - Over `tests/Support/UncancellableDialTransport.php` (a transport without CancellableDialTransportInterface), disconnect() waits for the held dial, released after 0.1 s, and a connect() issued after it opens the connection.
- `testDisconnectStopsWaitingForADialThatCannotBeStoppedAtTheConnectTimeout` - The same with the dial never released: disconnect() returns after the 500 ms connect timeout (0.45 to 1.5 s), Closed.
- `testDisconnectLeavesNoTimerOfItsWaitBehind` - After disconnect() has waited for the reconnect it stopped, the event loop has no more referenced watchers than before the reconnect: the bound on the wait is cancelled, not left to keep a finished script running until the connect timeout.
- `testConnectRacingADisconnectThatWaitsForTheStoppedReconnectFailsAtOnce` - A connect() issued while disconnect() still waits for the reconnect it stopped (its dial held, a 10 s connect timeout bounding the wait) fails in under 2 s with "Recovery was aborted before the connection opened" (#145); once disconnect() has returned, connect() dials afresh.
- `testDisconnectFromAListenerInsideTheReconnectDoesNotWaitForIt` - disconnect() awaited from a Disconnected listener, inside the reconnect it stops, returns in under 2 s instead of waiting for that reconnect (until the 10 s connect timeout, or the listener's own 5 s bound), and the connection ends Closed.
- `testDisconnectFromAnotherFiberWaitsForTheReconnectItStopped` - disconnect() awaited from an application fiber of its own (not the reconnect's) still waits for the reconnect it stopped (a dial it cannot stop, released after 0.1 s), and connect() then dials afresh.
- `testConnectAfterADisconnectThatOvertookADiallingConnectDialsAfresh` - disconnect() while a first connect() is held mid-dial stops that dial; that connect() fails with "Connect was aborted before the connection opened", and the next connect() opens the connection instead of joining it.
- `testDisconnectWaitsForAConnectWhoseDialItCannotStop` - The same over a transport that cannot stop its dial: disconnect() waits for the held connect() (released after 0.1 s) before it returns, and the next connect() dials afresh instead of joining it.
- `testConnectAfterADrainThatStoppedADiallingReconnectDialsAfresh` - A drain() whose 0.2 s budget runs out with the reconnect held mid-dial stops the dial and ends Closed; a connect() issued after it opens the connection.
- `testDrainWaitsForTheDialOfATransportThatCannotStopIt` - drain() over a transport that cannot stop its dial waits for the dial (released at 0.2 s, after the drain's 0.1 s budget) before it returns, and a connect() issued after it opens the connection.
- `testReconnectDoesNotDialAfterACloseThatCameWhileItClosedThePreviousSocket` - With each transport close taking 0.1 s, disconnect() while the reconnect's first attempt closes the dead socket: no dial happens after the close, and the connection ends Closed.
- `testReconnectStoppedByDisconnectDoesNotDeliverWhatIsQueued` - o2 and o3 stay queued behind a handler failure on o1; a reconnect backing off between refused dials is stopped by disconnect(), and neither is delivered (it used to deliver them on its way out, after disconnect()). The drop is noticed by an operation's read, which leaves the remainder queued (`tests/Support/ReconnectScenarios.php`; your own read would continue it first, #186).
- `testAuthenticationFailureOfAStoppedReconnectIsNotAnnouncedAgain` - A reconnect that disconnect() stopped mid-dial and whose handshake is then refused ends quietly: the reader resolves, one Closed event, no authentication error reported.
- `testCloseWhileTheReconnectLogIsWrittenStillStopsTheReconnectAtOnce` - disconnect() while a suspending logger writes the reconnect's "attempt failed" line stops the reconnect in under 2 s instead of after its 10 s backoff.
- `testReconnectOutlivesALoggerThatThrowsOnEveryAttempt` - With a logger throwing on every "reconnect attempt" line the reconnect keeps backing off and reopens the connection once dials are accepted.
- `testDisconnectDuringAFailingInitialDialWinsWithReconnectDisabled` - With reconnect disabled, a dial that disconnect() overtook and that then fails: connect() fails with the abort, the connection stays Closed, announced once.
- `testConcurrentDisconnectsAnnounceTheCloseOnce` - Two disconnect() calls at once (a slow close) emit Connected, Closed.
- `testInitialConnectRetriesUntilADialSucceeds` - With retryOnFailedInitialConnect, connect() keeps retrying refused dials until one succeeds: dials are let through once three have been refused, and more than two dials are made in all.
- `testDisconnectDuringAnInitialConnectRetryDialWins` - disconnect() while a retry of the first dial is parked mid-dial, which then succeeds: connect() fails with the abort, the socket is closed again, announced once.
- `testDisconnectDuringAnInitialConnectRetryDialWinsOverAnAuthenticationFailure` - The same when the server refuses the retry's credentials: the abort, announced once.
- `testPublishInFlightWhenTheConnectionIsClosedFailsAndLeavesTheReopenedConnectionAlone` - Over a plain socket, whose pending write fails at the close: a publish stalled across disconnect() and connect() fails, nothing reconnects, and no PUB reaches either connection.
- `testPublishWhoseReconnectADisconnectStopsFailsWithAnErrorOfThisLibrary` - A publish whose write fails with a raw Amp\ByteStream\ClosedException, as a built-in transport's does, runs the reconnect, which backs off between refused dials until disconnect() stops it: the publish fails with TransportClosedException "Transport is not connected" carrying the ClosedException, the connection is Closed and no PUB is written. Thrown as it was, the raw error would escape a catch (NatsThrowable).
- `testDisconnectWhileAPathThatGaveUpClosesTheTransportAnnouncesTheCloseOnce` - (reconnect attempts exhausted / reconnect refused authentication / connection lost with reconnect disabled / first connect failed / first connect refused authentication / initial connect retries ran out / initial connect retry refused authentication) A disconnect() issued from the transport's close hook while the path that gave up is closing the transport (a slow close) closes quietly: one Closed event, carrying the path's error (with reconnect disabled, the dropped socket's EOF).
- `testDisconnectDuringTheLastInitialConnectRetryWinsWhenThatDialFails` - disconnect() while the last retry of a failed first dial is parked mid-dial, which then fails too: connect() fails with the abort instead of that dial's error, and the close is announced once, by the disconnect().
- `testDisconnectWhileTheInitialConnectRetriesCloseTheirLastSocketWins` - disconnect() from the close hook of the socket close that follows the last failed retry: connect() fails with the abort, announced once, by the disconnect().
- `testErrorListenerThatReopensTheConnectionOnACorruptStreamIsNotUndone` - An error listener disconnects and reconnects when the stream turns out corrupt (an unknown control line); the failed parse's recovery then leaves the new connection alone: still epoch 1, no reconnect, events Connected, Closed, Connected.
- `testServingReadDuringADisconnectLeavesWhatAnEarlierReadLeftQueuedToTheClose` - Data provider (left queued before the serving read starts / left queued by the read it waits for): the application's read is held up in a handler that awaits (`tests/Support/HeldUpDelivery.php`) with another subscription's message queued behind it, and a serving loop's read (readIncomingForOperation with alwaysReport, as a service's run() reads) during a 0.2 s disconnect() close does not deliver that message, which the close discards; the held-up read ends with its 2 frames once its handler returns after the close. (Built on a throwing handler before #177, which now leaves only its own subscription's remainder queued.)
- `testYourReadDuringADisconnectLeavesTheRemainderOfAFailingSubscriptionToTheClose` - Guard, passes on 2.19.0 as well: o1's handler throws and leaves o2 queued; a processIncoming() started as a 0.2 s disconnect() close begins does not continue o2, which the close discards (#134, #186), and the connection ends Closed. Fails when your read continues a remainder whatever the close.
- `testAPassUnderWayWhenADisconnectBeginsGoesOnToTheOtherStoppedSubscriptions` - One read stops a at x1 and b at y1 (b's failure reported), with x2 and y2 queued; your next read continues a with x2, whose handler holds up (`tests/Support/HeldUpDelivery.php`); a disconnect() begins, and the handler returns as the transport's close begins, the close waiting for it: the pass goes on to b and delivers y2 during the close (still queued when the close began), and the connection ends Closed (#186). A pass that stopped at a close between subscriptions leaves y2 to the close; on 2.19.0 your read continued nothing and took the socket at once.

### tests/Unit/ConcurrentReadTest.php
The application's own read - processIncoming() or readIncoming() - finding the socket read held by another fiber, over the scripted server in `tests/Support/ReconnectingTransport.php`: it waits for that read to finish, bounded by its cancellation, and returns without reading. It used to return 0 at once, and a loop of such calls spun at 100% CPU with no timer or socket read ever running again. The loops are capped, so the old behaviour fails them instead of hanging; each of these tests fails with the wait reverted. The #174 tests cover the other way round: the application's read delivers what an operation waits for while that operation is between its reads (a queue polled with a timeout, pausing, or the pipelined pull engine, writing its next pull request), and the operation takes it before it reads again; they fail with that check reverted. A delivery that reaches the operation while its read already waits ends that read: OperationReadWakeupTest, ReadWakeupTest and ReadWakeupLifecycleTest.
- `testReadLoopLetsTheReadAnotherFiberHoldsFinish` - (a request waiting for its reply / the heartbeat's read) While the other read waits for the server, which answers 30 ms after that read is known to wait, a loop of processIncoming() calls lets it finish within a few calls (it used to spin 1,000 calls without the reply's timer ever running).
- `testReadReturnsOnceTheReadAnotherFiberHoldsIsDone` - (processIncoming() / readIncoming()) Called while the heartbeat's read waits for a message, it returns as soon as that read is done - 0 frames, or no frames and no bytes consumed - with the message delivered by the heartbeat's read, and does not go on to wait for more.
- `testReadWaitingForAnotherFibersReadGivesUpAtItsCancellation` - While a request holds the read (the server answers it only once the test has checked the wait), processIncoming() with a 50 ms cancellation throws CancelledException in under 2 s, where waiting for that read would last until the request's 5 s timeout; the request is still waiting, and then gets its reply.
- `testQueuePollingLoopLetsTheReadAnotherFiberHoldsFinish` - (fetch() / next() without a timeout) While a request's read waits 30 ms for a reply that brings a job for the queue, a loop polling the queue gets that job and the request its reply (each poll used to return at once, and the loop spun 1,000 polls without the reply's timer ever running).
- `testPollReturnsAMessageAnotherReadDeliversDuringItsPause` - (next() with a 5 s timeout / fetchAll(1) with a 5 s timeout) The application's processIncoming() holds the read when the poll starts, so the poll's first read waits and finds nothing; the message that ends that read is for another subscription, whose handler defers the queue's message and a second application read, which run while the poll pauses for 1 ms. The poll returns the message at once (about 0.05 s, bound 2.5 s). It used to start another read before it looked at the queue and return the message only when its 5 s timeout ran out (#174).
- `testFetchAllTakesWhatAnotherReadDeliveredDuringItsPauseBeforeReadingOn` - (DropOldest / DropNewest / Error) A queue holding at most 2 messages is filled by the application's second read during fetchAll(4)'s pause, as in the test above, and the poll's own read then brings 2 more: fetchAll() returns all 4 and drops none. It used to read with the buffer still full, so the 2 more overflowed it: DropOldest returned j3 and j4, DropNewest j1 and j2, and Error threw a SlowConsumerException.
- `testPipelinedPullEngineHandsOverABatchAnotherReadDeliveredWhileItIssued` - (the second pull request written at once / held up 100 ms) A depth-2 pull consumer runs beside the application's processIncoming() loop; the server answers the first pull at once and holds the second, and the application's read delivers the first pull's message while the engine writes the second pull's request. The handler gets the message at once (about 0.001 s / 0.1 s, bound 3 s), with both pulls issued. The engine used to read on with the batch already there and hand it over only at the first pull's deadline, 6 s (#174's shape).
- `testReadFromInsideAReconnectDoesNotWaitForTheReadThatStartedIt` - (a Reconnected listener / a handler the reconnect delivers to / the error listener told of the failure) The application's read holds the socket when the connection drops; a processIncoming() made from there while that read recovers the connection returns (the listener's and the handler's with the message the server then sends, the error listener's after running the reconnect itself), and the connection ends Open after exactly one reconnect. The failed read used to hold the socket read through the whole reconnect, and such a read waited for it forever.
- `testRequestFromAHandlerTheReconnectDeliversToGetsItsReply` - A handler of a message that arrived during the reconnect makes a request and gets its reply ("reply:from-the-handler"); in 2.8.0 it timed out, the socket read still held by the read that failed.
- `testDrainFromAReconnectedListenerDoesNotWaitOutItsBudget` - drain() called from a Reconnected listener finishes in under 2 s, well within its 10 s budget, and the connection ends Closed; in 2.8.0 it waited out the whole budget.
- `testReadParkedBehindAFailingReadDoesNotWaitOutTheReconnect` - With waitForReconnect off, fiber B's read parked behind fiber A's returns in under 2 s of the drop although A's reconnect is still backing off (dials refused until the test lets them through); A's reconnect then completes. B used to wait for the whole reconnect.
- `testSingleProcessIncomingLoopKeepsRunningWhileTheHeartbeatReads` - The reported freeze: a single processIncoming() loop, a handler awaiting while the heartbeat sends its PING and reads, the server answering 20 ms later. Both jobs are handled (the loop used to freeze after the first, spinning 100,000 calls).

### tests/Unit/ConfigurationBuildersTest.php
- `testStreamConfigurationMapsEverySetter` - Chains every StreamConfiguration setter and asserts toArray() emits the correct wire keys, including seconds->ns conversions for max_age (60->60e9) and duplicate_window (120->120e9), boolean flags, compression, metadata, and a raw set('first_seq', 42).
- `testStreamConfigurationDefaultsToEmptySubjects` - Asserts a freshly created StreamConfiguration('EMPTY') serializes with its name and an empty subjects array by default.
- `testConsumerConfigurationMapsEverySetter` - Chains every ConsumerConfiguration setter and asserts toArray() maps to wire keys with ms->ns conversions for ack_wait (5000->5e9), inactive_threshold (30000->30e9), and each backoff element ([1000,2000]->[1e9,2e9]), plus filter_subjects, policies, metadata, and raw set('rate_limit_bps').
- `testConsumerConfigurationEphemeralHasNoDurableName` - Asserts an ephemeral consumer (ackPolicy None, no durable) has null name, omits the durable_name key from toArray(), and emits ack_policy 'none'.

### tests/Unit/ConnectionEndingFrameTest.php
- `testFatalErrWithReconnectOffClosesTheConnection` - A fatal -ERR ('Stale Connection') read by processIncoming() with reconnect off closes the connection for good, with one Closed event, and releases the socket; the read fails with the server's error ("Server sent error frame: 'Stale Connection'"), not "Reconnect is disabled". Before #171 the connection stayed Open.
- `testOperationAfterTheFatalErrWithReconnectOffFailsWithoutWritingToTheClosedSocket` - After that -ERR, a publish fails at once on the Closed connection and nothing more is written to the old socket.
- `testFatalErrWithReconnectOnReconnectsSoTheNextRequestSucceeds` - With reconnect on, a request answered by the -ERR (after which the server answers nothing) waits for the reconnect it starts, fails with the server's error, and leaves the connection Open on a new session, so the next request succeeds. Before, the next request went to the dead socket and timed out.
- `testRequestThatMeetsAFatalErrWaitsForTheReconnectOnlyWithinItsTimeout` - With the reconnect's dial held until the test releases it (the scripted transport ignores the connect timeout), the request waits for it no longer than its own 300 ms timeout (over 0.2 s, under 2 s in all), fails with the server's error, and the reconnect carries on (Connecting) and completes once the dial is released. A request that waited for the reconnect past its timeout would never end, so the test stops waiting for it after 5 s and fails with that cause.
- `testRequestThatMeetsAFatalErrWithWaitingDisabledFailsAtOnce` - With `waitForReconnect: false` the request fails at once (under 2 s, where waiting for the held reconnect would take its whole 10 s request timeout) with the server's error, the reconnect has already left Open, and the next request runs on the new connection.
- `testReconnectThatNothingWaitsForGivesUpWithoutAnUnhandledError` - With `waitForReconnect: false` and dials refused, the reconnect the -ERR started gives up on its own fiber ("Reconnect attempts exhausted") without an unhandled future error reaching the event loop, and the connection ends Closed. Found by mutation testing: removing the recovery future's ignore() was not caught before.
- `testFatalErrReadByTheHeartbeatClosesTheConnectionWithReconnectOff` - A fatal -ERR read by the heartbeat's own read closes the connection with reconnect off, and is still reported through the error listener.
- `testServerPingWhosePongWriteFailsClosesTheConnectionWithReconnectOff` - A server PING whose PONG write finds the socket dead fails processIncoming() with the socket's error, as before, and the connection no longer stays Open on that socket.
- `testFullSubscriptionQueueDoesNotEndTheConnection` - Guard: a full subscription queue under the Error policy still fails the read with SlowConsumerException, and the connection stays Open with its socket.
- `testFatalErrDuringDrainDoesNotReconnect` - Guard: a fatal -ERR answering drain()'s flush PING does not reconnect (no second dial); drain() reports it and closes.

### tests/Unit/CredentialsParserTest.php
- `testParseExtractsJwtAndNkeySeed` - Asserts `parse()` extracts the JWT and NKey seed from a standard five-dash BEGIN/END `.creds` block.
- `testParseAcceptsCanonicalNscMarkersWithSixDashEnd` - Asserts `parse()` accepts real nsc output with five-dash BEGIN and six-dash END markers, extracting both JWT and seed (regression for the five-dash-only regex).
- `testFromFileParsesRealNscFixtureWhenPresent` - Asserts `fromFile()` on the real `build/nats/jwt/user.creds` fixture yields a JWT starting `ey` and a seed starting `S`; skips when the fixture is absent.
- `testParseRejectsMissingJwtBlock` - Asserts `parse()` throws NatsException ('NATS USER JWT block') when only the NKey seed block is present.
- `testParseRejectsMissingNkeySeedBlock` - Asserts `parse()` throws NatsException ('USER NKEY SEED block') when only the JWT block is present.
- `testFromFileRejectsNonExistentPath` - Asserts `fromFile()` throws NatsException ('not found or not readable') for a missing path.
- `testExtractBlockTreatsBlockTypeLiterallyOnBothMarkers` - Asserts the private `extractBlock()` matches the block type literally rather than as a regex: a type containing a metacharacter ('A.B') returns null when either the BEGIN or the END marker only regex-matches it ('AXB'), and returns the block body only when both markers carry the literal text.
- `testFromFileReadsValidCredsFile` - Asserts `fromFile()` reads a valid creds file written to a temp file, returning the expected jwt and nkeySeed values (cleaning up the temp file afterward).

### tests/Unit/DrainFlushBoundedWriteTest.php
- `testDrainCompletesWithinBudgetWhenWritesWedge` - Asserts a wedged drain-phase UNSUB write cannot hang drain(): the write wait is bounded by the drain budget, the flush phase is skipped, and the connection still reaches Closed well inside its ~requestTimeoutMs budget (the #149 write-phase twin, where cancelling the heartbeat first removed the only escalation out of the wedge).
- `testFlushTimesOutWhenPingWriteWedges` - Asserts a wedged flush() PING write fails inside the request-timeout budget with the documented TimeoutException naming backpressure, instead of parking forever before the read phase even starts, and that a following disconnect() still releases the wedged write and reaches Closed.
- `testDrainSkipsFlushPhaseWhenPingWriteWedges` - Asserts a wedged drain-phase flush PING (the first drain write when no subscriptions exist) surfaces as a cancellation through the PING write's own catch, skipping the flush phase, with drain still reaching Closed within its budget.
- `testDrainReportsDeadSocketPingWriteFailureAndStillCloses` - Asserts the disclosed #150 contract that drain() no longer throws on a dead-socket write failure: a flush-phase PING write that throws lets drain()->await() resolve, surfaces the TransportClosedException via the error listener rather than swallowing it, and still reaches Closed with the socket torn down.
- `testDrainReportsDeadSocketUnsubWriteFailureAndStillCloses` - Asserts the same #150 contract for the per-sid UNSUB writes: a dead-socket UNSUB write is reported through the error listener and drain still reaches Closed, instead of throwing out of drain() and stranding the connection in Draining with the socket open.
- `testDrainStillClosesWhenLoggerThrowsWhileReportingWriteFailure` - Asserts a user-supplied PSR logger that throws while the drain write failure is being reported cannot escape emitErrorSafely and break the teardown (#158): drain()->await() still resolves, the connection reaches Closed, and the transport is closed.
- `testPublishDuringDrainTimesOutWhenWriteWedges` - Asserts a handler ack published while drain delivers backlog has its write wait bounded like drain's own writes: the backlog message is still delivered, the PUB is attempted on the wire, the wedged publish fails with the documented TimeoutException ("Publish during drain timed out") instead of parking forever, and drain still reaches Closed.
- `testDrainBudgetBoundsSerialHandlerPublishesAcrossBacklog` - Asserts mid-drain handler publishes share drain()'s single budget rather than each re-arming a fresh requestTimeoutMs: with a wedged transport and six backlog messages acked twice each, drain completes in roughly one budget instead of the pre-fix ~K x 2 budgets, delivery stops at the first inter-message boundary past the deadline, the first of a new pass included since the deadline seals the delivery phase (#213; only m1 arrives, where 2.24.3 also handed m2 over), the five dropped messages are reported loudly once with their count, and the wedged acks fail into the handler with TimeoutException.
- `testDrainStillClosesWhenTransportCloseThrows` - Asserts drain()'s teardown closes the transport best-effort like every other terminal path: a throwing close() on the deliberately broken socket does not rethrow out of drain()->await(), the close attempt still runs, and the connection reaches Closed rather than being stranded in Draining (#150).
- `testDrainDeadlineWithOnlySuspendedHandlerReportsNoZeroCountDiscard` - Asserts a drain whose only remaining backlog is a dispatch suspended mid-handler, with nothing queued behind it, drops no message and therefore emits no "0 buffered message(s)" deadline-exceeded discard error (#149), while still reaching Closed past the suspended handler.

### tests/Unit/DrainLifecycleTest.php
drain() and drainSubscription() where they meet reconnects and closes, over the scripted server in `tests/Support/ReconnectingTransport.php`: every drain ends Closed with one Closed event, connect() is refused while a drain runs, a reconnect that a drain or disconnect() stops really stops, nothing a drain discards goes unreported, and a subscription the client removed is never left subscribed on the server by a reconnect. Each regression test fails without the fix it covers.
- `testDrainOfAnOpenConnectionEndsWithAClosedEvent` - drain() of an open connection emits Connected then Closed; it used to close without any event.
- `testDrainThatWaitedForAReconnectEndsWithOneClosedEvent` - A drain that waited for a reconnect ends with Connected, Disconnected, Reconnected, Closed.
- `testDrainThatRanOutOfTimeWaitingForTheReconnectEndsWithOneClosedEvent` - A drain whose budget ran out waiting for the reconnect ends with one Closed instead of a dangling Disconnected.
- `testDrainThatCouldNotWaitForTheReconnectEndsWithOneClosedEvent` - A drain that cannot wait (waiting disabled) closes instead, throws, and the close is announced once.
- `testDrainWaitingForAReconnectThatGivesUpEndsWithOneClosedEvent` - When the reconnect a drain waits for gives up, its own Closed is the only one; the drain adds none.
- `testDrainWaitingForAReconnectThatGivesUpEndsClosedEvenWhenTheLoggerThrows` - A logger that throws while the exhausted reconnect reports its discarded publishes cannot skip the cleanup: the connection ends Closed with one Closed event, the reader gets "Reconnect attempts exhausted", and the waiting drain resolves.
- `testDisconnectDuringADrainEndsWithOneClosedEvent` - disconnect() while drain() is flushing: the drain resolves, the connection is Closed, and the close is announced once, not by both.
- `testClosedListenerCanReconnectStraightAfterADrain` - The drain's Closed event comes once the drain is over, so a listener's connect() dials afresh and the connection ends Open on a new session.
- `testDrainFromAListenerOfAReconnectThatGaveUpFailsWithoutClosingAgain` - (error listener on the exhaustion report / connection listener on Closed) A drain() from a listener of a reconnect that just gave up fails with "Connection is not open" and does not close or announce the connection a second time.
- `testSecondDrainOfAnOpenConnectionFails` - A second drain() while the first flushes fails with "Connection is not open"; the first still closes the connection.
- `testDrainBudgetIsAtLeastOneHundredMilliseconds` - With requestTimeoutMs 1, drain() still gets its 100 ms floor: the deadline it sets, read off the connection, lies 100 ms after it starts, and it waits for an unanswered flush PONG that long.
- `testConnectIsRefusedWhileADrainWaitsForAReconnect` - connect() while a drain waits for the reconnect is refused with "Cannot connect: drain in progress" instead of joining the reconnect; the drain then drains the reopened connection.
- `testConnectIsRefusedWhileADrainThatRanOutOfTimeWindsDown` - After a drain's budget runs out, a supervisor triggered from the handler the drain's backlog pass is running - so it always lands in that window, after the stopped reconnect has ended (state Closed) - is refused with "Cannot connect: drain in progress" instead of dialling a connection the teardown would destroy; after the drain, connect() opens a connection that stays open. The drop is noticed by an operation's read, which leaves the remainder queued (#186).
- `testPublishWhileATimedOutDrainClosesTheTransportFailsInsteadOfOutlivingTheDrain` - A publish issued from the transport's close hook - so it always lands while a timed-out drain closes the transport, with the stopped reconnect still mid-dial - fails with "Connection is not open" instead of being buffered, and no stale PUB reaches any later connection.
- `testDrainThatRanOutOfTimeStopsItsBacklogPassAtTheDeadline` - With 20 queued messages at 150 ms each and the budget spent waiting for the reconnect, the backlog pass delivers only its head message and reports "drain deadline exceeded: 19 buffered message(s)", ending in under 2 s against a 150 ms budget (a pass that went on would take 3 s). The drop is noticed by an operation's read, which leaves the remainder queued (#186).
- `testDrainDoesNotWaitForASlowReconnectedListener` - A Reconnected listener that stays busy until the drain is over (polling a flag the test clears afterwards) does not hold up a drain waiting for the reconnect: the drain unsubscribes on the new connection and ends Closed in under 2 s, within its 5 s budget. It used to wait for the listener, and then for the reconnect to end, until its budget ran out.
- `testConnectRightAfterADrainThatRanOutOfTimeSucceeds` - With a 10 s backoff, a drain whose budget ran out stops the reconnect at once, so connect() right after it succeeds straight away (under 2 s) instead of joining the winding-down reconnect.
- `testReconnectAttemptFailingAfterADisconnectStopsWithoutBackingOffOrReportingExhaustion` - An attempt that fails after disconnect() ends the reconnect at once (under 2 s): no 10 s backoff, no "Reconnect attempts exhausted", one Closed.
- `testDisconnectDuringTheLastReconnectBackoffDoesNotReportExhaustion` - disconnect() during the 10 s backoff after the last attempt stops the reconnect at once (under 2 s), without reporting exhaustion or a second Closed.
- `testDrainThatCannotWaitReportsTheMessagesItDiscards` - A drain that cannot wait reports "Connection closed: 2 parsed inbound message(s) were discarded undelivered", and nothing about buffered publishes when none were buffered. The drop is noticed by an operation's read, which leaves the remainder queued (#186).
- `testPublishWhileADrainThatCannotWaitClosesTheTransportFails` - A publish while a drain that cannot wait closes the transport fails instead of being accepted and dropped; only the earlier buffered publish is reported as discarded.
- `testDrainWaitingForAReconnectThatFailsAuthenticationEndsClosedAndReportsTheDiscardedPublishes` - A reconnect the server refuses to authenticate ends the drain Closed with one Closed event and reports "Reconnect failed authentication: N bytes of buffered publishes were discarded".
- `testDrainFromAConnectedListenerWaitsForAReconnectRunningElsewhere` - A drain() from a Connected listener while a reconnect runs on another fiber waits for it and drains, instead of closing the connection as if it ran inside the reconnect.
- `testDrainSubscriptionIsNotReplayedByAReconnectDuringItsFlush` - The heartbeat reconnects while drainSubscription()'s flush waits on a hung server: the new connection re-subscribes only the other subscription, so the drained queue-group member is not left alive on the server.
- `testDrainSubscriptionIsNotReplayedWhenItsFlushLosesTheConnection` - The same when the flush's own read finds the connection gone and starts the reconnect itself: the flush fails as soon as the reconnect's first attempt ends the lost connection's PONGs, the call reports the EOF and "Connection lost before the server answered the PING" and resolves with the subscription gone while the reconnect runs on (#178), and once the connection is Open again only the other subscription was replayed.
- `testDrainSubscriptionWhoseUnsubscribeFailsStillDeliversAndIsNotReplayed` - An UNSUB write that finds the connection dead (failNextWriteContaining): drainSubscription() still delivers the queued message, removes the subscription without throwing, no UNSUB reached the old connection, and the reconnect does not bring the subscription back.
- `testDrainSubscriptionDuringAReconnectWaitsForItThenDeliversWhatAlreadyArrived` - drainSubscription() while reconnecting is still pending 50 ms later - it waits for the reconnect - and once dials are accepted it resolves with the connection Open, the two queued messages delivered, nothing reported as discarded, and the subscription not re-subscribed.
- `testDrainSubscriptionThatRunsOutOfTimeWaitingForAReconnectDeliversWhatArrived` - With dials refused throughout, drainSubscription() waits out its 200 ms budget, then delivers the queued message and removes the subscription with the reconnect still in flight; the reconnect then re-subscribes only the other subscription.
- `testDrainSubscriptionDuringADrainLeavesTheSubscriptionToTheDrain` - drainSubscription() while drain() waits in its flush resolves at once, leaving the subscription registered; a message the server sent before processing the drain's UNSUB, read by that flush, is then delivered after the queued one, and the drain removes the subscription (it used to be dropped as an unknown sid).
- `testDrainSubscriptionHandsTheRestToADeliveryUnderWayOnAnotherFiber` - With a delivery for the sid under way on another fiber (a handler parked on the first message), drainSubscription() writes the UNSUB and resolves; that delivery then hands over the message queued behind it and removes the subscription, and nothing is reported as discarded.
- `testSecondDrainSubscriptionForTheSameSidResolvesAtOnce` - A second drainSubscription() for a sid being drained resolves while the first is still waiting for its flush; only one UNSUB is written and the first call removes the subscription.
- `testSubscriptionRemovedWhileAReconnectReplaysIsUnsubscribedBeforeTheConnectionOpens` - A subscription unsubscribe() removes while the reconnect is between re-subscribing and going live is unsubscribed on the new connection before it opens, so the server no longer has it.
- `testDrainSubscriptionWhileAReconnectReplaysDeliversWhatTheServerSendsBeforeItsUnsubscribe` - drainSubscription() of a queue-group member the reconnect has already re-subscribed, with the reconnect held before going live (its buffered publish held up until the test lets it through): a message the server routes to the re-subscribed sid meanwhile is delivered (it used to be dropped as an unknown sid), the subscription is removed, and the server no longer has it.
- `testSubscriptionRemovedWhileAReconnectUnsubscribesAnotherIsUnsubscribedToo` - A subscription removed while the reconnect is already sending such an UNSUB is unsubscribed too, before the connection opens.
- `testDrainSubscriptionReportsAHandlerThatThrowsDuringItsFlushAndDeliversTheRest` - A handler that throws while drainSubscription()'s flush delivers is reported through the error listener (it used to be swallowed with the flush failure), the message behind it is still delivered, nothing is reported as discarded, and the reconnect does not bring the subscription back.
- `testDrainFlushReportsAThrowingHandlerAndReadsOnToTheMessagesStillInFlight` - (drain() / drainSubscription()) The flush's PING is answered by one message, whose handler throws, then by another message and the PONG in a later read: the failure is reported once and the flush reads on, so the handler gets both messages (the flush used to end with the failure, and the second message was lost).
- `testDrainFlushReportsAnErrTheServerKeepsTheConnectionOpenForAndReadsOnToTheMessagesStillInFlight` - Data provider (drain() / drainSubscription(); 'maximum subscriptions exceeded', 'Permissions Violation for Publish with Reply of ...', a bare 'Permissions Violation', 'Invalid Publish Subject'; the -ERR alone ahead of the messages, or behind a message in the same read): the flush reports the -ERR once and reads on to its PONG, so the handler gets both messages and nothing else is reported; drain() ends Closed with one Closed event, drainSubscription() leaves the connection Open and the subscription removed. The flush used to end at the -ERR and lose what followed it, also in 2.10.0.
- `testDrainFlushReadsOnPastANonClosingErrButEndsAtAFatalOneAfterIt` - drain()'s flush meets 'maximum subscriptions exceeded', then a message and 'Stale Connection', then another message and the PONG: the first message is delivered, both -ERRs are reported in order, the drain ends Closed, and nothing reconnects. The flush used to end at the first -ERR.
- `testDrainFlushReadsOnPastAnErrReadAheadOfALineThatDoesNotParse` - The same -ERR followed by a line that does not parse in one read: the -ERR is reported, then 'Unsupported control frame: BOGUS', and the flush reads on, so both messages after them are delivered. The read used to throw the -ERR after dealing with the corrupt line, which ended the flush.
- `testDrainFlushEndsAtOnceWhenTheServerClosesAfterAnErrThatKeepsTheConnectionOpen` - Guard: 'maximum subscriptions exceeded' followed by EOF ends drain()'s flush at once (under 2 s of a 10 s budget), Closed, with the -ERR the only report.
- `testDrainFlushStillEndsAtOnceAtAPongTheSocketWouldNotTake` - Guard: a server PING whose PONG write fails ends drain()'s flush at once (under 2 s of a 10 s budget) on a socket whose read would otherwise block, Closed, with the write failure reported once. Reading on after it took the whole budget.
- `testAServiceReadingDuringADrainReportsAPongTheSocketWouldNotTakeOnce` - A service's run() loop reads when the server PINGs during drain()'s flush and the PONG write finds the socket gone: the failure is reported once and the drain ends Closed within 1 s. Marking it as ending the connection would have the loop's read throw it, and the loop swallow it.
- `testDrainSubscriptionWhoseFlushMeetsAnErrAndThenACloseStartsTheReconnectAndResolvesBeforeItGivesUp` - With reconnect on (3 attempts, dials refused), drainSubscription()'s flush meets 'maximum subscriptions exceeded' and then the server's close: its read reports the EOF and starts the reconnect, whose first attempt ends the flush's PONG, so the call reports 'Connection lost before the server answered the PING' and resolves with the subscription gone while the connection is still Connecting (#178); the reconnect then gives up on its own, so the events are Connected, Disconnected, Closed, the Closed event carries 'Connection refused', and the connection is Closed after four dials. In 2.10.0 the call ended at the -ERR and left the dead connection Open; in 2.10.1 and 2.10.2 the -ERR itself ended the connection; up to 2.13.0 the call lasted until the reconnect had given up and reported 'Reconnect attempts exhausted'.
- `testDrainKeepsToItsBudgetWhenTheErrorListenerIsSlow` - (a handler that throws / a SubscriptionQueue whose buffer is full) With a 0.3 s budget and an error listener taking 150 ms per report, drain()'s flush brings twenty messages that each make a report: the drain ends in under 2 s (reports made after the delivery pass would take 3 s), reports fewer than twenty, and reports the rest as discarded ("drain deadline exceeded"), once. Reports made only after the delivery pass used to run every message past the deadline.
- `testDrainSubscriptionThatCannotWaitForAReconnectReportsAHandlerThatThrowsAndDeliversTheRest` - The same with waiting disabled while reconnecting, where delivery happens at once without a flush (the reconnect is still in flight when it resolves): two throwing handlers are reported and the messages around them are delivered. The drop is noticed by an operation's read, which leaves the remainder queued (#186).
- `testDrainSubscriptionDuringADrainWindingDownPastItsDeadlineStopsAfterAThrowingHandler` - A drain that ran out of time waiting for a reconnect (parked mid-dial) winds down without a connection, its backlog pass busy in another handler; a drainSubscription() issued then stops after a throwing handler like the drain's own delivery, reporting that handler ('bad2') and the one message it discards. The application's read that met the one chunk threw slow's first failure, reported orders' first ('bad1') and left each subscription's remainder queued (#177). The drop is noticed by an operation's read, which leaves the remainder queued (#186).
- `testDrainSubscriptionWhoseUnsubscribeIsHeldUpEndsWithinItsBudget` - A drainSubscription() UNSUB write stalled for 3 s ends the call within its 200 ms budget (under 1 s), reports "drainSubscription timed out writing UNSUB (transport backpressure)" and removes the subscription.
- `testDrainRunningOutOfTimeWhileAReconnectThatGaveUpClosesTheTransportEndsWithOneClosedEvent` - A reconnect gives up some 40 ms into the drain's 1.2 s budget but its transport close takes 1.5 s: the drain's budget runs out meanwhile, and the events are Connected, Disconnected, Closed - the one Closed, with the reconnect's error.
- `testConnectionReopenedAfterADrainAnnouncesItsNextCloseToo` - drain, connect, drain, connect, disconnect emit Connected/Closed three times: each connect() starts the Closed bookkeeping afresh.
- `testDisconnectAfterADrainDoesNotAnnounceTheCloseAgain` - drain() then disconnect() twice emits Connected, Closed: disconnect() of a connection already closed and announced closes it quietly.
- `testSupervisorReconnectingAfterADrainFromADisconnectedListenerFailsInsteadOfHanging` - A Disconnected listener, which runs inside the reconnect, awaits drain() while a supervisor reconnects on the Closed it announces: the supervisor's connect() fails at once with "Recovery was aborted before the connection opened" instead of joining the reconnect that waits on that listener, and everything ends Closed within the bound (it used to hang for good).
- `testSupervisorReconnectingAfterADrainFromAReconnectedListenerOpensANewConnection` - The same from a Reconnected listener, which runs once the reconnect is over: the drain drains the reopened connection (UNSUB 1 on it), and the supervisor's connect() opens a new connection (Open, epoch 2). It used to be refused with "Recovery was aborted before the connection opened", the reconnect still waiting for its listener.
- `testConnectAnsweringAClosedThatComesWhileADrainRunsIsRefusedUntilTheDrainIsOver` - A reconnect that gives up while drain() waits announces Closed during the drain; a supervisor's connect() then is refused with "Cannot connect: drain in progress", and one after the drain opens a new connection.
- `testDisconnectInterruptingAWaitingDrainIsNotReportedAsTheDrainRunningOutOfTime` - disconnect() with a slow close while a drain waits for a reconnect ends the drain Closed without a "Drain ran out of time" report: disconnect() is the lossy close that discarded the buffer.
- `testDrainEndsPromptlyWhenTheSocketDiesDuringItsFlush` - The socket dies right after the drain's PING: the drain ends at once (under 2 s of its 10 s budget), Closed, without reconnecting, instead of reading the dead socket until the budget runs out.
- `testDrainEndsPromptlyWhileAnotherFiberReads` - Data provider (an application's processIncoming() loop / a service's run()): with that fiber parked in its read and a server that answers the drain's PING at once, drain() ends Closed in under 1 s of its 2 s budget. That fiber's read took the PONG before the flush first read, and the flush then waited out the whole budget.
- `testDrainIssuedRightAfterARequestEndsPromptly` - drain() issued in the same tick as a request() nobody answers, against a server that answers its PING at once, ends Closed in under 1 s of its 2 s budget. The request's read took the PONG in the hop before the drain's read started, and that read then waited on the idle socket until the budget ran out.
- `testFlushEndsPromptlyWheneverAnotherFibersReadStartsAroundIt` - Data provider (drain() / drainSubscription() / flush() / rtt()): a request() or a processIncoming() is made after each of 0 to 16 event-loop hops, before the flush or after it, and every flush ends in under 2 s of its 10 s budget. Another fiber's read that started just before the flush's own read took the PONG, and the flush's read then waited out the budget.
- `testDrainEndsPromptlyAlongsideALoopThatLeftTheRestOfAFailingSubscriptionQueued` - An application's processIncoming() loop stops at a throwing handler and parks its next read with that subscription's next message queued (its handler awaits 50 ms; a read that throws leaves only its failing subscription's remainder queued, #177); the server answers the drain's PING after 10 ms. drain() ends in under 1 s of its 2 s budget with both messages delivered. It fails on a flush whose read delivers that message before it reads and then skips its check for the PONG: the loop takes the PONG and parks another read meanwhile, and the flush waits for that read until the budget runs out. Delivering first while still checking for the PONG ends promptly, so that alone is not caught.
- `testYourReadDuringADrainLeavesARemainderUnderWayToItsDeliveryAndReads` - s stops at s1; your next read X continues s with s2, whose handler holds up (`tests/Support/HeldUpDelivery.php`); drain() unsubscribes, flushes (its read taking the PONG) and waits for that delivery; your read Y, made during the drain, takes the socket with s3 left to the delivery under way; once the handler returns, X delivers s3 and the drain ends Closed (#186). Decided by what had been delivered when Y took the socket. A read that went on from its continuation only on an Open connection spins there for good, and the test never ends; on 2.19.0 X took the socket at once, with s2 and s3 queued.
- `testADrainEndsPromptlyWhileAServiceLoopDeliversARead` - Data provider (Service::drain() / NatsClient::drain() / NatsClient::drainSubscription()): a service's run() is in a handler that awaits 50 ms, with a message queued behind it whose handler awaits 150 ms, when the drain starts; the server answers its PING as it is written, so the PONG is in while the first handler still awaits. The drain ends in under 5 s of its 10 s budget with both handlers run. The flush bug it catches takes two changes together: a flush read that delivers the queued message before it reads, and then reads without checking again for its PONG, so that the loop takes the PONG meanwhile and parks its next read, which the flush then waits for until the budget runs out (10 s). Either change alone passes here; skipping the check alone is caught by `testFlushEndsPromptlyWheneverAnotherFibersReadStartsAroundIt`.
- `testServerPingDuringADrainCannotHoldItPastItsBudget` - A server PING during the drain's flush is answered with a PONG write that stalls for 3 s: the drain still ends within its 300 ms budget (under 1 s).
- `testDrainWhileADisconnectIsClosingTheTransportFailsWithNothingToDrain` - drain() while disconnect() is in its slow close during a reconnect (state Connecting, close intent set; a transport that cannot stop its dial keeps the reconnect in flight) fails with "Connection is not open", and the close is announced once.
- `testDrainWaitingForAReconnectWhoseLoggerThrowsOnEveryAttemptStillEndsClosed` - A logger throwing on every reconnect attempt no longer ends the reconnect, so a drain waiting for it winds down at its budget and ends Closed with one Closed event and the subscription removed, instead of failing with the logger's error.
- `testDrainStillAnnouncesTheCloseWhenTheLoggerThrowsOnIt` - A logger throwing while the Closed event is logged neither keeps the event from the listener nor makes drain() fail.
- `testHandlerDrainingItsOwnSubscriptionStillGetsTheMessagesQueuedBehindIt` - A handler that awaits drainSubscription() of its own sid still receives the two messages queued behind the current one before the subscription is removed; one UNSUB is written and nothing is reported as discarded.
- `testDrainSubscriptionStopsAtTheAutoUnsubscribeMaxWithoutReportingTheRest` - With an auto-unsubscribe max of 3 (the first delivery threw), drainSubscription() delivers up to the max and reports nothing for the message the max holds back.
- `testRemovedSubscriptionStaysGoneWhenTheReplayWindowUnsubscribeFails` - The UNSUB that ends the replay window for a removed subscription finds the connection dead: that attempt fails, and the next one re-subscribes only the kept subscription.
- `testPublishDuringAReplayWindowUnsubscribeGoesOutBeforeTheConnectionOpens` - A publish made while the reconnect's replay-window UNSUB write is held up goes out before the connection opens, in publish order before a later direct publish (it used to stay in the buffer).
- `testAutoUnsubscribeArmedWhileAReconnectReplaysReachesTheServer` - unsubscribe($sid, 2) during the replay window sends "UNSUB <sid> 2" on the new connection before it opens; the subscription stays until the max is delivered.
- `testOperationsOtherThanReadsAreRefusedWhileADrainRuns` - While drain() waits in its flush, subscribe() and flush() are refused with "Connection is not open"; no SUB reaches the wire.
- `testReaderWaitingForAReconnectThatADrainThenDrainsKeepsReading` - A reader and a drain wait for the same reconnect; the drain resumes first and starts draining, and the reader then reads instead of failing because the connection is no longer Open.
- `testChangesToSeveralSubscriptionsInOneReplayWindowAllReachTheServer` - Two subscriptions removed and an auto-unsubscribe armed on a third during one replay window all reach the new connection before it opens: "UNSUB a", "UNSUB b", "UNSUB c 5".
- `testAutoUnsubscribeArmedWhileAReconnectReplaysCountsWhatWasReceivedBefore` - With two messages received before the outage and one more delivered by the replayed SUB before the arm, unsubscribe($sid, 5) during the replay window sends "UNSUB <sid> 3": the replayed SUB counts from zero on the server, and counts its own delivery itself.
- `testServerPingDuringADrainIsAnsweredOnce` - A server PING while drain() waits for its flush PONG is answered with exactly one PONG.
- `testHandOverWhoseDeliveryHitsAThrowingHandlerStillDeliversTheRest` - The delivery drainSubscription() handed the rest over to stops at a throwing handler: the subscription stays until the next read's delivery pass hands over the last message, and only then is removed.

### tests/Unit/DrainScanPendingDirtyTest.php
- `testMessageToOneOfManyIdleSubscriptionsIsDelivered` - With 50 idle subscriptions, asserts a single MSG for one mid-list sid is still delivered to exactly that handler - the #162 dirty set routes the lone active sid without depending on scanning every live subscription.
- `testCrossSidDeliveryOrderMatchesRegistrationOrderNotWireOrder` - Feeds one chunk whose wire order is deliberately non-ascending (c1,a1,b1,c2,a2 across three sids) and asserts delivery is grouped by ascending-sid (registration) order with per-sid FIFO preserved (a1,a2,b1,c1,c2) - pinning that the #162 dirty-set drain keeps the old full-map scan's cross-sid ordering.
- `testDirtySetTracksOnlyActiveSidAndClearsWhenQueueEmpties` - With three idle subscriptions, enqueuing one message via `enqueueMessage()` puts ONLY that sid in `pendingDirty`; after `drainAllPending()` the message is delivered, the dirty entry is removed so it is not re-scanned (#162), and the emptied `SplQueue` is still retained for every sid (#139 no-realloc).
- `testHasUndeliveredDrainBacklogTracksDirtySet` - Asserts `hasUndeliveredDrainBacklog()` is false for an idle sid, true once a message is enqueued, and false again after the drain - staying consistent with the dirty set that `drain()`'s round-up loop (#149/#150) depends on.

### tests/Unit/ExceptionHierarchyTest.php
- `testNatsExceptionHierarchyImplementsMarker` - Asserts NatsException, ConnectionException, JetStreamException and SlowConsumerException are all instances of the NatsThrowable marker interface, and SlowConsumerException is a ConnectionException.
- `testTransportExceptionsImplementMarkerWhileRemainingRuntimeExceptions` - Asserts TransportClosedException and TlsRequiredException implement NatsThrowable and remain RuntimeExceptions, while deliberately NOT being NatsException instances.
- `testCatchNatsThrowableCatchesATransportException` - Asserts a thrown TransportClosedException is caught by a `catch (NatsThrowable)` block and the caught value is the TransportClosedException type.
- `testFallbackClientVersionIsCurrentSemver` - Asserts ProtocolCodec::FALLBACK_CLIENT_VERSION is a string matching semver `\d+.\d+.\d+` and is not the stale `1.0.1` placeholder.

### tests/Unit/FailedControlWriteTest.php
A control frame - a SUB, an UNSUB, the PING of flush() or rtt() - whose write finds the socket dead, over the scripted server in `tests/Support/ReconnectingTransport.php` (`failNextWriteContaining()` drops the session and fails that write). Such a write used to leave the connection Open on the dead socket, its raw error reaching the caller; each test fails with the fix reverted.
- `testSubscribeWhoseSubWriteFindsTheSocketDeadRecoversAndStaysSubscribed` - The SUB write fails; the connection reconnects, the replay subscribes the sid on the new connection, subscribe() returns it, and a message sent there reaches the handler.
- `testSubscribeWhoseSubWriteFindsTheSocketDeadWithReconnectOffClosesTheConnection` - With reconnect off, subscribe() fails with `Reconnect is disabled`, the connection is Closed and a Closed event was emitted.
- `testSubscribeGivesUpAtItsOwnTimeoutWhenTheReconnectOutlastsIt` - With dials refused, subscribe() throws `Subscribe to "orders" timed out waiting for the connection to be re-established` after its 0.2 s timeout; once the reconnect succeeds the subscription is not replayed, the next subscribe gets sid 2, and a frame for sid 1 is discarded (#116).
- `testSubscribeWithWaitingDisabledFailsAtOnceWhileTheConnectionRecovers` - With waitForReconnect off, subscribe() fails in under 2 s, while the recovery's dials are still refused, with `Connection is not open` (the socket's error as its previous), and the connection reconnects once dials are let through, without the subscription.
- `testRecoveryThatNothingWaitsForFailsWithoutAnUnhandledError` - With waiting disabled and reconnect off, subscribe() fails at once and the recovery nothing waits for closes the connection; its "Reconnect is disabled" does not escape to the event loop as an unhandled error (checked after the recovery's future is collected).
- `testSubscribeWaitingForTheRecoveryFailsWhenTheUserClosesTheConnection` - disconnect() while subscribe() waits for the reconnect: subscribe() fails with `Connection is not open`, and the connection is Closed.
- `testSubscribeWhoseWriteFailsAfterTheConnectionWasReplacedFails` - A SUB write that fails only after the application closed the connection and opened a new one: subscribe() fails with `...failed: the connection was closed`, and the new connection is left alone (no reconnect, nothing subscribed).
- `testJetStreamFetchWhoseInboxSubscriptionFindsTheSocketDeadFetchesOnTheNewConnection` - The messenger's case: a fetchBatch() whose inbox SUB finds the socket dead returns the message served on the new connection.
- `testRequestWhoseReplyInboxSubscriptionFindsTheSocketDeadIsAnsweredOnTheNewConnection` - A request whose reply-inbox SUB finds the socket dead gets its reply on the new connection.
- `testRequestThatGivesUpOnItsReplyInboxLetsTheNextRequestSubscribeItAgain` - With dials refused the request fails with its own timeout, `Request timed out for subject svc.echo while waiting for the reply inbox to be set up`, which bounds the inbox's set-up too (#194, #213; it used to report the subscribe's own timeout); after the reconnect the next request subscribes the reply inbox exactly once and gets its reply (#118).
- `testARequestWhoseReplyInboxSubFindsTheSocketDeadWaitsOnlyWithinItsOwnBudget` - Data provider (request() timing out / requestMany() timing out / request() cancelled by its caller): requestTimeoutMs 10 s, the reply inbox's SUB failing and the reconnect's dials refused: a 300 ms request or requestMany() ends with `Request timed out for subject svc.echo while waiting for the reply inbox to be set up`, and a five-second request its caller cancels at 0.1 s with CancelledException, each well within 5 s, the connection still Connecting (#194). Each used to wait the whole requestTimeoutMs that the set-up started on its own. The bound was one second until a CI runner under coverage took that long for a 300 ms request; the outcome alone tells the two behaviours apart.
- `testARequestWhoseReplyInboxSubFindsTheSocketDeadIsNotSentAfterItsDeadline` - The same with the dials let through at 1 s: the 300 ms request has timed out by then and is never published on the new connection, where the next request subscribes the inbox and is served (#194). It used to be published there after its deadline.
- `testPingWhoseWriteFindsTheSocketDeadFailsTheOperationAndTheConnectionRecovers` - (flush() / rtt()) The PING write fails; the operation fails with `Connection lost before the server answered the PING` (the socket's error as its previous), the connection has reconnected, and the next call succeeds.
- `testFlushWhosePingWriteFindsTheSocketDeadGivesUpAtItsBudget` - With dials refused, flush() throws `Flush timed out waiting for the connection to be re-established` within its budget, the reconnect still running.
- `testUnsubscribeWhoseUnsubWriteFindsTheSocketDeadDoesNotThrow` - unsubscribe() returns; the next publish reconnects, and the subscription is not replayed.
- `testAutoUnsubscribeWhoseWriteFindsTheSocketDeadIsArmedOnTheNewConnection` - unsubscribe($sid, 3) returns; after the next publish reconnects, `UNSUB <sid> 3` is on the new connection.

### tests/Unit/FailedRequestWriteTest.php

Request PUB/HPUB failure coverage for `request()`, `requestWithHeaders()` and `requestMany()` (#215). Held recovery dials prove requests end independently of recovery; inline-wedged writes mirror real socket backpressure.

- `testTimeoutEndsTheRequestBeforeRecoveryAndDoesNotResendItLater` - All four request variants time out before the held recovery finishes, leave no mux waiters, and send nothing after recovery.
- `testCancellationEndsTheRequestBeforeRecoveryAndDoesNotResendItLater` - Caller cancellation ends each request during recovery and prevents a late retry.
- `testWaitingDisabledFailsBeforeRecoveryWithoutBufferingTheRequest` - With `waitForReconnect: false`, a failed request write reports `Connection is not open` with the write error as its previous, while recovery continues without buffering the request.
- `testRecoveryWithinBudgetReplaysTheInboxAndRetriesTheRequestOnce` - Recovery restores the inbox, retries once and delivers the reply; outbound message and payload counters record the successful send once.
- `testReconnectDisabledClosesTheConnectionInsteadOfRetrying` - With reconnect disabled, a failed request write reports `Reconnect is disabled`, closes the connection and leaves no mux waiters.
- `testCancellationInTheReconnectedListenerPreventsTheRetry` - Cancellation during the Reconnected listener prevents sending on the restored connection.
- `testCancellationBeforeTheScheduledRetryRunsPreventsTheWrite` - Cancellation queued between recovery completion and the scheduled writer prevents the retry, proving the writer itself checks the budget.
- `testAnInvalidatedReplayedInboxPreventsTheRetry` - Permission rejection or a subscription-limit drop of the inbox replay fails the request before its retry is sent.
- `testASecondFailedWriteDoesNotRetryTheRequestAgain` - A failed retry surfaces its transport error without another recovery or request attempt.
- `testInlineWriteBackpressureIsBoundedByTheRequestTimeout` - Inline socket backpressure cannot park a request past its timeout; teardown releases the abandoned write without a retry.
- `testInlineWriteBackpressureIsBoundedByCallerCancellation` - Caller cancellation ends a request parked inside transport write() before the test guard expires.
- `testAnAbandonedWriteThatLaterFailsRepairsTheConnectionWithoutResending` - A write failure surfacing after request timeout still repairs the connection, with no late request frame.
- `testCollectionTimeoutAfterASuccessfulRetryReturnsTheCollectedReplies` - After a successful PUB/HPUB retry, requestMany() collection expiry returns either its reply or an empty collection.

### tests/Unit/FeatureSupportTest.php
- `testRequiredVersion` - Asserts the version registry returns the minimum NATS version for known fields (filter_subjects->2.10, allow_msg_ttl->2.11, allow_atomic->2.12) and null for unknown fields.
- `testUnsupportedFromApiErrorMapsKnownField` - Asserts an "unknown field" API error for a registered feature returns an UnsupportedFeatureException (also a JetStreamException) carrying feature, requiredVersion, serverVersion, code 400, and a message mentioning "requires NATS server 2.12+" and the server version.
- `testUnsupportedFromApiErrorIgnoresUnregisteredField` - Asserts an "unknown field" error for a field not in the registry returns null (treated as an ordinary error).
- `testUnsupportedFromApiErrorIgnoresOtherErrors` - Asserts a non-"unknown field" error (e.g. "stream not found", 404) returns null, not a feature-gap exception.
- `testUnsupportedFromApiErrorWithUnknownServerVersion` - Asserts a null server version produces an UnsupportedFeatureException with null serverVersion and a message containing "reports unknown".

### tests/Unit/HandlerFailureDuringOperationTest.php
A subscription handler that throws while an operation's read delivers to it (#173), over the scripted server in `tests/Support/ReconnectingTransport.php`. Operations read the socket while they wait for a result of their own, so their reads deliver messages for every subscription; another subscription's handler that threw used to fail the operation, its result lost. It is now reported through the error listener, the rest of the read is delivered, and the operation completes; `NatsOptions::$handlerErrorsFailOperations` restores the old failure. The operations are scripted by `tests/Support/OperationsThatRead.php`, shared with SlowConsumerErrorPolicyTest. Each regression test fails against the code before the fix; the guards pass on both.
- `testOperationCompletesDespiteAnotherSubscriptionsHandlerFailure` - (request() / requestMany() / flush() / rtt() / JetStream fetchBatch() / JetStream directGetBatch() / JetStream pull consumer / Key/Value keys() / Key/Value history() / SubscriptionQueue::fetch() / SubscriptionQueue::next() / SubscriptionQueue::next() with a timeout / SubscriptionQueue::fetchAll()) The read that brings the operation its result also brings a message whose handler throws and one for a later subscription: the operation returns its result, the failure is reported once, and the later message is delivered by the same read. Each used to fail with the handler's exception.
- `testOperationFailsWithAnotherSubscriptionsHandlerFailureWhenConfiguredTo` - (the same data sets) With `handlerErrorsFailOperations: true`, each fails with the handler's exception, as before the option existed, and nothing is reported.
- `testARequestAServiceEndpointMakesCompletesDespiteAnotherSubscriptionsHandlerFailure` - (option off / on) The case #173 reported: an endpoint handler makes a request() whose reply arrives behind a message whose handler throws. By default the endpoint answers with the reply ('got:answer'), counts no error, keeps no last error, and the failure is reported; with the option it answers with a HANDLER_ERROR, counts one error and keeps 'poison handler' as its last error, as it used to.
- `testAnOperationWhoseReadRunsAReconnectGetsItsResultBehindAHandlerFailure` - A SubscriptionQueue::next() with a timeout runs the reconnect after its connection drops, and the replay brings a message whose handler throws, then the job: the delivery after the reconnect reports the failure and delivers the job, and the poll returns it.
