Over `tests/Support/ReconnectingTransport.php`, whose every dial opens a new session (epoch 0, then 1 after the failover), with the lame-duck INFO naming the second server in `connect_urls`, so that the pool grows to two and the failover dials it; a delivery is held up by `tests/Support/HeldUpDelivery.php`. The failover's dial is held (`holdNextDial()`) until the operation has returned, and let go only once it is held: the outcome is decided by the session (0) and the state (Connecting) the connection was in when the operation returned, and a 5 s time-out only guards against a hang. Each regression test failed on the code before the fix with "the operation returned while the failover's dial was held", its read running the failover inline and not returning until the dial was let go, unless its entry says otherwise; the guards pass on both.
- `testARequestWhoseOwnReadBringsTheLameDuckInfoTimesOutAtItsDeadlineWhileTheFailoverDials` - A request() with a 300 ms timeout whose own read brings [INFO ldm | PING], the reply inbox set up first: it times out with "Request timed out for subject svc.echo" on session 0 with the connection Connecting; once the dial goes through the connection is Open on the second server (one dial of it), and no PONG was written on either connection.
- `testAnOperationWhoseOwnReadBringsTheLameDuckInfoEndsAtItsDeadlineWhileTheFailoverDials` - (requestMany(max 1), 0.3 s / SubscriptionQueue::next(), timeout 0.3 s / fetchBatch(1), expiry 0.3 s / PullConsumerIterator::handle(), one iteration, expiry 0.3 s / KeyValueBucket::keys(), 0.3 s) The operation's own read brings [INFO ldm | PING] where its result would have come: it ends at its deadline as it does when nothing comes (nothing collected, null, a 408, nothing handled, "Key enumeration stalled"), on session 0 with the connection Connecting, and the failover then completes on the second server; no PONG written.
- `testAReplyBehindTheLameDuckInfoIsReturnedWhileTheFailoverDials` - [INFO ldm | reply] read by a request with a 2 s timeout: it returns reply-1 on session 0, Connecting.
- `testADeliveryDuringTheFailoverBringsTheOperationItsResult` - The poll's own SubscriptionQueue holds one message (maxPendingMessagesPerSubscription 1): the application's read overflows it, and the drop report's error listener holds that delivery up with the buffer empty, so the poll (3 s timeout) finds nothing of its own to take (#179) and reads the socket, where it brings the lame-duck INFO. Once the failover's dial is held the test lets the delivery go on, and the poll returns job-1 on session 0, Connecting: its wake-up ended the wait. (A message queued behind another subscription's held-up delivery would not do: the poll takes it before it reads, #179.)
- `testWithWaitingDisabledAnOperationWhoseReadBringsTheLameDuckInfoFailsAtOnce` - (the INFO alone / the reply behind it) With waitForReconnect off, the request whose read brings the INFO fails at once with "Connection is not open", with no previous exception, on session 0 with the connection Connecting, the failover not having dialled yet; with its reply in the same chunk it returns reply-1 instead, the wake-up winning. Under amphp/amp 3.1.0 to 3.1.2 the reply case failed, the composite not yet reporting the wake-up that fired in the dispatch: the library requires `amphp/amp ^3.1.3` (#200).
- `testWithWaitingDisabledARequestManyWhoseReadBringsTheLameDuckInfoReturnsWhatItCollected` - (two replies, then the INFO / the INFO alone) With waitForReconnect off, requestMany() for three replies (30 s total, a counting cancellation), each reply in a read of its own, then the lame-duck INFO alone: it returns [r-1, r-2], or with nothing collected fails with "Connection is not open", on session 0, Connecting, with a look per read. Pins the collection's catch: thrown out of it, the read's failure would lose the two.
- `testWithWaitingDisabledAFetchWhoseReadBringsTheLameDuckInfoReturnsThePartialBatch` - (two messages, then the INFO / the INFO alone) With waitForReconnect off, fetchBatch() of three (30 s expiry), each message in a read of its own, then the lame-duck INFO alone: it returns [m-1, m-2], or with nothing received fails with "Connection is not open", on session 0, Connecting, within a few event-loop microtask rounds (counted up to a cap: a fetch that looked again without pausing would take them all, its pause a timer that never ran). Pins the fetch's catch: thrown out of it, the read's failure would lose the two.
- `testAFlushWhoseReadBringsTheLameDuckInfoFailsAsSoonAsItsPongIsLost` - (flush() / rtt()) The server answers the flush's PING with [INFO ldm] alone: the flush fails with "Connection lost before the server answered the PING" as soon as the failover's first attempt ends the leaving connection's PONGs, on session 0, Connecting.
- `testAFlushWhosePongCameBehindTheLameDuckInfoSucceeds` - (flush() / rtt()) [INFO ldm | PONG] answers the flush's PING: the PONG still completes the leaving connection's slot, and the flush succeeds on session 0, Connecting.
- `testWithWaitingDisabledAFlushWhosePongCameWithTheLameDuckInfoSucceeds` - (flush() / rtt(), [PONG | INFO ldm] / [INFO ldm | PONG]) With waiting for a reconnect disabled, the PONG that comes in the same chunk as the lame-duck INFO completes the flush's slot, and the flush succeeds on session 0, Connecting. Pins the read's look at the flush's slot: the flush's wake-up fires only from a queued callback, after the read has looked, and going by it the read would fail the flush with "Connection is not open".
- `testWithWaitingDisabledAReadThatMeetsTheFailoverOnlyAnnouncingTheNewConnectionLooksAgain` - With waitForReconnect off, a request's read brings [INFO ldm | MSG of a subscription whose handler awaits]: the handler holds the read up while the failover reopens the connection on the second server (session 1, Open) and its Reconnected listener awaits; the request's reply is pushed on the second server, and once the handler is let go the request returns reply-1. Pins the read's look at the generation: failing at once there, the request would fail with "Connection is not open" on an open connection. Before: the test's 2 s wait for the handler to begin ended with a CancelledException, the inline failover, held up in the Reconnected listener, coming ahead of the message's delivery.
- `testAReadWhoseWakeUpAndCallerBothFiredDuringTheWaitThrowsTheCallersOwnCancellation` - readIncomingForOperation() for a subscription of its own brings [INFO ldm | MSG of that subscription]: its own delivery fires its wake-up before it waits for the failover, and the Disconnected listener, in the failover's fiber, cancels the caller's cancellation with a reason before the wait resumes. The read throws the caller's own CancelledException, with the reason, on session 0, Connecting. Pins the caller's throwIfRequested() in awaitLameDuckFailoverFrom(): without it, the wake-up's exception, which won inside the composite, would reach the caller without the reason.
- `testTheRestOfTheChunkIsTheLeavingServersWhileTheFailoverIsUnderWay` - A request's read brings [INFO ldm | -ERR 'maximum subscriptions exceeded' | INFO of the first server]: the request times out on its own, the error listener gets "The server the connection left sent an error frame: 'maximum subscriptions exceeded'", and serverInfo() is still the lame-duck INFO's (S1, ldm) when the request returns on session 0, Connecting, and the second server's (n1, no ldm) once the failover is over.
- `testADrainUnderWayLeavesThePingBehindTheLameDuckInfoAnswered` - A drain()'s flush read brings [INFO ldm | PING | PONG] under the drain's close-intent: no failover (one dial), one PONG on the draining connection, the connection Closed, and the events Connected, DiscoveredServers, LameDuck, Closed. Guard: a failover started in a fiber of its own there would not run, and the PING behind the INFO, marked as the leaving server's, would get no PONG.
- `testAFailoverThatGivesUpIsReportedOnceAndTheOperationEndsAtItsNextLook` - Dials refused and one attempt: the request (2 s) whose read brought the INFO ends with "Connection is not open" once its read's wait ends with the failover, the connection Closed, one "Reconnect attempts exhausted" reported, one Closed event, two dials in all. Guard: the inline failover ended the same way.
- `testACollectionWhoseFailoverGivesUpReturnsWhatItReceivedAtItsNextLook` - (fetchBatch(3) / requestMany(max 3), 30 s budget; two received / nothing received) Two received, each in a read of its own, then the lame-duck INFO alone, with dials refused and one attempt: the read waits for the failover, which gives up ("Reconnect attempts exhausted" reported once, two dials), and at its next look the collection finds the connection Closed and returns the two; with nothing received it fails with "Connection is not open". Before: the fetch failed with "Connection is not open" with the two received as well. Guards: the nothing-received data sets, and requestMany(), whose look at a terminal close returned what it had collected before as well.
- `testTheEventsComeInOrderAroundTheOperation` - Connected, DiscoveredServers, LameDuck and the failover's Disconnected come before the request returns at its 300 ms deadline (session 0, Connecting), since the failover's fiber starts while the read waits; Reconnected only once the dial is let go.
- `testYourOwnReadStillRunsTheFailoverInline` - processIncoming() bringing [INFO ldm | PING] has not returned once the failover's dial is held, and returns 2 once it is let go, the connection Open on the second server; no PONG written. Guard.
- `testALameDuckInfoBehindTheReconnectsHandshakeLeavesThePingBehindItAnswered` - The handshake PONG of the reconnect that processIncoming() runs after a drop carries [INFO ldm | PING]: the reconnect's own dispatch keeps the inline failover, a no-op inside the reconnect (#157), so LameDuck is announced, no failover of its own dials (two dials in all), the PING is answered on the new connection, and the events are Connected, Disconnected, DiscoveredServers, LameDuck, Reconnected. Guard: started in a fiber of its own, the failover would have marked the new connection as being left, its PING unanswered.
- `testTheFailoverMarkIsClearedWhenTheFailoverReturns` - (a failover that reached the second server / one that did not run, the LameDuck listener having started a disconnect() it did not await) By reflection: the mark is there when the request returns while the failover dials, and gone once the failover's future completes. When the failover did not run, close-intent being set before its fiber started, the request ends with "Connection is not open", one dial in all, no PONG for the PING behind the INFO (the rest of the chunk was the leaving server's all the same), and the mark is gone. Before: ReflectionException, the property not existing.
- `testAFailoverThatReturnsLeavesTheMarkOfALaterOneAlone` - The Reconnected listener of the failover a request's read started makes a request of its own (300 ms) whose read brings the second server's lame-duck INFO, naming a third: a second failover starts in a fiber of its own, its dial held, and the listener's request times out while it dials. Once the first failover has returned, the mark (by reflection) is still the second one's, its failover under way, and it goes once that one returns, on session 2; three dials in all. Before: ReflectionException, the property not existing (and the listener's request ran the second failover inline, held at its dial).
- `testAnOperationsReadWaitsForTheFailoverOnlyWithinItsWait` - (its 0.3 s deadline / its wake-up, the subscription it waits for removed while the dial is held) readIncomingForOperation() for a subscription of its own brings the lame-duck INFO: it throws its deadline's own CancelledException, or returns what it read (one frame, bytes consumed), on session 0, Connecting.
- `testWithWaitingDisabledAnOperationsReadFailsAtOnce` - The same read with waitForReconnect off and a 4 s deadline fails at once with "Connection is not open", no previous exception, on session 0, Connecting: the read itself fails, rather than return and leave its operation to meet the closed connection, or its PONG failed by the failover, at its next read. Pins the throw in awaitLameDuckFailoverFrom()'s waiting-disabled branch, which a request alone does not: its next read fails with the same message.
- `testAnOperationsReadReturnsOnceTheFailoverIsOver` - The same read with a 4 s deadline, the dial let go once it is held: it returns its one frame with the connection Open on session 1. Guard: the inline failover returned then too.
- `testAnOperationInTheReconnectedListenerOfTheFailoverDoesNotWaitForIt` - The Reconnected listener of a failover that a request's read started makes a request on the new connection and gets "ok", the server sending a +OK ahead of the reply in a read of its own: the listener runs in the failover's fiber, whose mark is still set, and the new connection's reads do not wait for that failover, which a read bringing nothing for the request would otherwise do until the request's timeout. Guard.
- `testAPingTheSecondServerSendsDuringTheFailoverIsAnsweredThere` - The second server's handshake PONG carries a PING behind it, dispatched while the first connection is still marked as being left: one PONG on the second connection, none on the first. Guard.
- `testAPullConsumerWhoseReadBringsTheLameDuckInfoReissuesItsPullOnTheSecondServer` - A pull consumer run (expiry 30 s) whose read brings the lame-duck INFO, the dial let go once it is held: its read's wait ends with the failover, and the engine re-issues its pull on the second server and handles m-after there, one pull on each session, well within the 5 s guard rather than at the lost pull's expiry. Guard.
- `testAFailoverWhoseConnectionAReconnectReplacedDuringTheLameDuckListenerLeavesTheNewOneAlone` - (the read of a request, the failover in a fiber of its own / your own processIncoming(), the failover inline) The LameDuck listener drops the session and awaits a flush(), whose failed write starts a reconnect that replaces the connection before the listener returns (the flush fails with "Connection lost before the server answered the PING"): the failover, once it has returned, has left the new connection alone, one reconnect in all (session 1, two dials) and the events Connected, DiscoveredServers, LameDuck, Disconnected, Reconnected. Before: your own read's inline failover failed the new connection over as well, session 2 ("one reconnect, the flush's"); the other data set, which waits for the mark by reflection, ended with a ReflectionException, the property not existing.

### tests/Unit/OperationReadReconnectLifecycleTest.php
The reconnect that an operation's own read starts (#178, see `OperationReadReconnectTest`) through its life and beside other fibers, over `tests/Support/ReconnectingTransport.php` seen through `tests/Support/WatchedTransport.php`, with deliveries held up by `tests/Support/HeldUpDelivery.php`: the delivery that brings the result at any moment of the reconnect, the deadline landing around its end, two reads failing together with the heartbeat, a close during it, a flush or a poll joining a reconnect the heartbeat started, stalls of the event loop, a reconnect nobody waits for any more, the Reconnected listener's order, the application's read parked behind, a listener's poll, three outages in a row, the pull consumer's re-issued pull and the cost of waiting. Where the order of events can decide, it does: the second session is not up yet (epoch 0), or the connection is still Connecting, when an operation returns before the reconnect is over.
- `testTheDeliveryThatBringsTheResultEndsTheWaitWhateverTheHopsAfterTheDrop` - (request() 0 and 8 hops) The reply held up in a lower-sid handler, queued behind it on the mux inbox, goes on that many event-loop hops after the drop: before the operation's read met the EOF, while it reported it, or once it waited for the reconnect it started. The operation gets its reply while the second session is still refused (epoch 0 on return; dials refused for 0.5 s), and the connection comes back with the events Connected, Disconnected, Reconnected. (Own-sid operations are not here: #179 takes a queued own-sid message before the drop, so it cannot be a during-outage delivery; their reconnect-gets-result path is `OperationReadReconnectTest::testAnOwnSidOperationWhoseOwnReadRunsTheReconnectGetsItsResultOnTheNewConnection`.)
- `testTheDeliveryThatBringsTheResultEndsTheWaitDuringTheDialOrTheReplay` - (request(), as the first dial's CONNECT is written / as the first SUB is replayed) The reply goes on as the reconnect writes that: the operation gets it within 1.5 s of its 2 s deadline (0.3 s outage) and the reconnect completes. Guard: the old read, which looked again once the reconnect was over, got it too. (Own-sid operations moved to the new-connection staging for the reason above.)
- `testTheDeadlineLandingAroundTheReconnectsEnd` - (next() with its deadline 0.1 s before, at and 0.1 s after the end of a 0.8 s outage, the last with a message on the new connection; request() at and 0.1 s after) Nothing is delivered during the outage: the operation ends within 0.6 s of its deadline, with its timeout (nothing, or a TimeoutException) or with the message the new connection brings, and a request afterwards gets reply-1 on the second session.
- `testTwoReadsThatFailTogetherStartOneReconnect` - (the heartbeat's PING written and unanswered / its write failing with the drop / no heartbeat) A poll's read on the socket and a request's read parked behind it fail together, with dials refused for 1 s: one reconnect; both operations end at their 0.5 s deadlines while the connection is Connecting, the poll with nothing and the request with its timeout; then a request succeeds, and the events are Connected, Disconnected, Reconnected.
- `testACloseDuringTheReconnectAnOperationsReadStartedWins` - (next() / request(), disconnect() / drain()) The reconnect backs off between refused dials when the application closes: disconnect() stops it, which dials no more, and the operation, with a 3 s deadline, ends with the close within 2 s; drain() waits for the reconnect (dials accepted 0.5 s later), drains the new connection (epoch 1) and closes, and the operation ends with that close. One Closed event, and nothing dials on after the close.
- `testAFlushWhoseFailedReadJoinsAReconnectTheHeartbeatStartedMeanwhile` - The flush's read fails while its error listener awaits 150 ms, during which the heartbeat's PING write fails and the heartbeat starts the reconnect: back from the listener the read joins it within the flush's wait, and the flush fails with "Connection lost before the server answered the PING" within 0.7 s of a 1 s outage while the connection is Connecting; one reconnect, the EOF reported once.
- `testAPollWhoseReadFailsWhileTheHeartbeatsReconnectIsUnderWayJoinsItWithinItsDeadline` - The heartbeat's PING write fails while a poll's read is on the socket; the heartbeat's reconnect closes the socket and the poll's read joins it: the poll returns nothing at its 0.5 s deadline while the connection is Connecting (1 s outage), and one reconnect reopens it.
- `testAStallOfTheEventLoopAroundTheDropChangesNothing` - (the test blocks the loop for 0.2 s right after the drop / the error listener reporting the EOF does / the Disconnected listener does) Nothing is queued for the poll before the drop (with #179 a queued own-sid message is taken before the drop), so the poll's own read notices the drop and runs the reconnect, and its message arrives on the new connection once the SUB is replayed (0.6 s outage): the poll gets it once the loop runs again and the connection is back (epoch 1 on return), and the reconnect completes whatever the stall.
- `testAReconnectNobodyWaitsForAnyMoreGivesUpOnItsOwnTerms` - A poll's read starts a reconnect of three attempts 300 ms apart with dials refused, and the poll returns nothing at its 0.3 s deadline (Connecting): the reconnect then gives up on its own, the events are Connected, Disconnected, Closed, the Closed event carries "Connection refused", four dials were made, and a request afterwards is refused at once with "Connection is not open".
- `testAClosedListenerThatSuspendsWhileTheReconnectGivesUp` - (the Closed listener suspends 0.3 s and the poll's deadline is 3 s / it suspends 0.8 s and the deadline is 0.3 s) The reconnect a poll's read started gives up (three attempts, dials refused) and the Closed listener suspends: the poll whose deadline outlasts the listener gets "Reconnect attempts exhausted" with its cause after the listener returned (#172); the one whose deadline comes first returns nothing before the listener returned, the connection Closed by then.
- `testAPollThatWaitsForAllOfTheReconnectReturnsAfterTheReconnectedListener` - The Reconnected listener pushes the poll's message and then suspends 0.2 s: the poll, whose read started the reconnect and waited for all of it, returns the message after the listener returned (README reconnect item 8).
- `testAnOperationIssuedDuringTheReconnectAPollsReadStartedWaitsWithinItsOwnTimeout` - A request issued during that reconnect times out at its own 0.3 s while the connection is Connecting; once dials go through, the poll, with a 3 s deadline, gets the message pushed on the new connection.
- `testTheApplicationsReadParkedBehindTheOperationsReadWaitsForTheWholeReconnect` - processIncoming() is parked behind a poll's read when the connection drops (1 s outage): the poll ends at its 0.5 s deadline (Connecting), the parked read returns with 0 frames when the poll's read ends (Connecting), and the application's next read joins the reconnect, returns once it is over with the connection Open, and reads the one frame on the new connection, which the next poll gets.
- `testAPollFromAReconnectedListenerWhoseOwnReadFindsTheNewConnectionGoneReconnectsItAgain` - A Reconnected listener polls, and the new connection drops as its read takes the socket: that read starts a second reconnect in a fiber of its own, the second open is announced from the event loop while the listener call is under way, and the listener's poll gets the message the third connection brings; two reconnects, the events Connected, Disconnected, Reconnected, Disconnected, Reconnected.
- `testAPollThroughThreeOutagesInARow` - One poller rides out three outages (0.6 s each, dials refused), its read the only one on the socket each time: each poll returns nothing at its 0.3 s deadline while Connecting, the connection comes back each time, and a message on each new connection reaches the next poll; three reconnects, seven events.
- `testAPullConsumerWhoseOwnReadStartedTheReconnectReissuesItsPullRightAfterIt` - The pull consumer's read meets the drop with no message during the 0.5 s outage: once the reconnect is over the engine re-issues the pull the old server forgot (#120), one pull on each session, and the message the new pull finds reaches the handler within 2.5 s rather than at the lost pull's 4 s expiry.
- `testAnOperationParkedOnTheReconnectItStartedDoesNotSpin` - (next() / request()) Parked for a second on a reconnect backing off 300 ms between refused dials: no read of the dead socket, at most 6 dials, fewer than 10 looks of the operation for its result, where a wait that returned at once would have it look about a thousand times. Nothing is timed: the poll's buffer is swapped for `tests/Support/CountingBuffer.php`, which counts how often next() checks it, and the request gets a `tests/Support/CountingCancellation.php` as its cancellation, which counts how often the request checks it, each operation looking once as it starts, which shows the count sees its looks. A wait that polled the recovery on a timer inside the connection, without returning to the operation, would escape the look count; no path of a parked operation has such a loop, and a process-wide count of the event loop's registrations that would have caught it was tried and dropped, since a reconnect attempt of a client an earlier test leaves behind registers its 500 handshake polls in a burst. The test used to bound the CPU time of the second (under 0.15 s) instead, which was dropped for depending on the environment: under Xdebug with coverage on a busy CI runner the second's own cost reached it, while either spin cost less than it on a fast machine. Under amphp/amp 3.1.0 to 3.1.2 the request() data set counted no look, the composite request() wraps the counting cancellation in never asking it: the library requires `amphp/amp ^3.1.3` (#200).

### tests/Unit/OperationReadReconnectTest.php
An operation whose own read is the first to notice a lost connection (#178): a SubscriptionQueue poll, request() or requestMany(), fetchBatch() or directGetBatch(), the pull consumer, Key/Value keys() or history(), or a flush waiting for its PONG, whose read is on the socket as the connection drops. That read used to run the reconnect inline, so neither the operation's deadline nor its wake-up (#174) could end the wait; it now starts the reconnect in a fiber of its own and waits for it only within the operation's own wait, or with waiting disabled fails at once, while your own processIncoming() and the heartbeat's read still run the reconnect themselves. A requestMany() or fetchBatch() whose read fails that way, or because the reconnect gave up or reconnect is off, returns what it has received, and so does one whose read meets a fatal -ERR, also once the reconnect has reopened the connection (#196). Over `tests/Support/ReconnectingTransport.php` seen through `tests/Support/WatchedTransport.php`, which tells the test when the operation's read is on the socket, with deliveries held up by `tests/Support/HeldUpDelivery.php`. The outcomes are decided by the order of events where possible: the connection is still Connecting when an operation returns before the reconnect is over, and Open once a read waited for all of it. Each regression test fails on the code before the fix; the guards pass on both. The reconnect's life beside other fibers is in `OperationReadReconnectLifecycleTest`.
- `testARequestWhoseOwnReadRunsTheReconnectGetsTheReplyStillUnderWay` - (request() / requestMany(max 1)) The application's read took a chunk holding, ahead of the reply, a message for a lower-sid subscription whose handler awaits; the operation's read takes the socket during the hold-up and the connection drops with dials refused for 2 s, so that read starts the reconnect. The handler returns 50 ms later and the delivery brings the reply (queued on the mux inbox) during the outage: the operation, with a 1 s deadline, returns it within 1 s, and the connection is Open on the second session once dials go through. It used to return after the whole 2 s outage. (A reply shares the mux inbox, not an own sid, so #179's own-sid take does not reach it.)
- `testAnOwnSidOperationWhoseOwnReadRunsTheReconnectGetsItsResultOnTheNewConnection` - (next() / fetchAll(1) / PullConsumerIterator::handle() / KeyValueBucket::keys() / KeyValueBucket::history()) Nothing is queued for the operation when it looks - with #179 a queued own-sid message is taken before the drop - so its result is answered only on the new connection (epoch 1). The operation's own read, the sole reader, goes to the socket, notices the drop (dials refused for 0.2 s) and starts the reconnect; once the connection is back the poll's SUB is replayed, or the pull/Key-Value consumer re-issued, and the result arrives, within its 2 s deadline, on the second session. fetchBatch() and directGetBatch() are one-shot and do not re-issue after a reconnect, so they are not here (their own-sid take is in `OperationTakesItsQueuedMessageTest`, fetchBatch's reconnect deadline in `testAnOperationWhoseOwnReadRunsTheReconnectEndsAtItsOwnDeadline`).
- `testAnOperationWhoseOwnReadRunsTheReconnectEndsAtItsOwnDeadline` - (next() / request() / requestMany(max 1) with a 0.5 s deadline; fetchBatch(1) with a 0.5 s expiry, its deadline 1.5 s) Nothing is delivered; the operation's read, the only one on the socket, meets the dropped connection with dials refused for 3.5 s. The operation ends with its own timeout at its deadline, within a second of it (null, "Request timed out for subject svc.echo", an empty collection, a 408), while the connection is still Connecting; once dials go through the connection is Open and a request succeeds. It used to end only after the whole outage, with the connection Open.
- `testWithWaitingDisabledAnOperationWhoseOwnReadRunsTheReconnectFailsAtOnce` - (next() / request() / requestMany(max 1) / fetchBatch(1)) With waitForReconnect off, the same operation fails within 0.5 s with "Connection is not open", the read's TransportClosedException ("Socket closed by peer (EOF)") as its previous, while the connection is Connecting, and the reconnect its read started reopens the connection on its own. It used to wait for the whole 1.5 s outage, and then time out or return nothing.
- `testAFlushWhoseOwnReadRunsTheReconnectFailsAsSoonAsItsPongIsLost` - (flush() / rtt()) The connection drops once the flush's PING is out, with dials refused for 1.5 s: the flush fails with "Connection lost before the server answered the PING" within 1 s, as soon as the reconnect's first attempt ends the lost connection's PONGs, while the connection is still Connecting; it used to fail with the same error once the reconnect was over.
- `testWithWaitingDisabledAFlushWhoseOwnReadRunsTheReconnectFailsAtOnce` - (flush() / rtt(), over a transport that closes at once / whose close takes 50 ms) With waitForReconnect off, the flush whose PING is out when the connection drops fails within 0.5 s with "Connection is not open" and the read's EOF as its previous, whatever the close latency, while the connection is Connecting; the reconnect reopens it on its own. A flush that read on instead met the lost PONG or the closed connection, depending on how long the transport's close took.
- `testYourOwnReadThatIsTheFirstToNoticeStillRunsTheReconnectAndWaitsForAllOfIt` - processIncoming() with a 5 s cancellation meets the dropped connection with dials refused for 0.3 s: it returns 0 frames once the connection is Open again, on the second session, after more than two dials. Guard.
- `testOperationsFromAListenerInsideAReconnectAPollsReadStartedAreRefusedAtOnce` - A next() poll's read is the first to notice the dropped connection and starts the reconnect; from the Disconnected listener, request(), subscribe(), flush() and processIncoming() are refused within 1 s with "Connection is not open" and connect() with "cannot join the in-flight recovery from a connection/error listener", the reconnect completes (Open, one reconnect) and the poll returns null at its 1 s deadline. Guard: the fiber the read starts the reconnect in is the recovery fiber (#145).
- `testTheHeartbeatsReadThatNoticesTheLostConnectionStillRunsTheReconnectItself` - The connection drops once the heartbeat's PING is out, with dials refused for 0.3 s and nobody else reading: the heartbeat's read runs the reconnect, and a request issued meanwhile waits for it within its 2 s timeout and gets its reply on the second session. Guard.
- `testAnOperationWhoseOwnReadNoticesTheLossLearnsWhyWhenReconnectIsOff` - (next() / request()) With reconnect off, the operation whose read met the dropped connection fails with "Reconnect is disabled" whose previous exception is the read's TransportClosedException ("Socket closed by peer (EOF)"), and the connection is Closed (#172). Guard.
- `testAnOperationWhoseOwnReadRunsAReconnectThatGivesUpGetsItsError` - (next() / request()) A reconnect of three attempts with dials refused gives up: the operation whose read started it fails with "Reconnect attempts exhausted" and its cause within 2 s of its 3 s deadline, the connection Closed after four dials (#172). Guard.
- `testACollectionWhoseReadFailsWithTheConnectionGoingReturnsWhatItReceived` - (requestMany(max 3) / fetchBatch(3), 30 s budget; waiting disabled / a reconnect of three attempts with dials refused / reconnect off; two received / nothing received) The collection has received two, each in a read of its own, when the connection drops under its next read, which fails with "Connection is not open" (the connection Connecting), "Reconnect attempts exhausted" or "Reconnect is disabled" (Closed): the collection returns the two within 5 s of its 30 s budget; with nothing received it fails with that error, the read's EOF its cause (the exhausted reconnect's error with a cause of its own). Before: that error with the two received as well, which were lost. The nothing-received data sets are guards.
- `testACollectionWhoseReadMeetsAFatalErrorReturnsWhatItReceivedAlsoOnceTheReconnectReopenedTheConnection` - (requestMany(max 3) / fetchBatch(3), 30 s budget; 'Stale Connection' or 'User Authentication Expired' in the read after the two received, 'Stale Connection' in the chunk of the two; guards: 'Stale Connection' with waiting disabled and the dial held, a reconnect of three attempts with dials refused, reconnect off, and the collection's deadline before the held dial (300 ms; a 200 ms expiry, its deadline 1.2 s), and every one of the five with nothing received) The read that meets the -ERR recovers the connection before it throws (#171), and with the reconnect quick the connection is Open again, one reconnect counted, by the time the collection sees the error: the collection returns the two within 5 s, its request or pull sent once, a fetch's inbox released, and the reopened connection takes the next request. The guards return the two with the connection Connecting or Closed; with nothing received every one fails with "Server sent error frame: '<error>'", which `endedTheConnection()` recognises. Before (#196): the -ERR thrown with the two received, which were lost, because the connection was Open.
- `testACompleteCollectionWhoseChunkAlsoBringsAFatalErrorReturnsItsResult` - (requestMany(max 1) / fetchBatch(1) / fetchNext()) The one message and 'Stale Connection' come in one chunk: the read hands the message over, meets the -ERR and has the connection reopened before the collection sees it is complete. It returns the message, the connection Open after one reconnect, the request or pull sent once. Before (#196): the -ERR, and the complete result lost.
- `testACollectionWhoseReadFailsWithTheConnectionStayingOpenStillFails` - (requestMany(max 3) / fetchBatch(3), two received, `handlerErrorsFailOperations` on; an -ERR refusing a reply subject ('Permissions Violation for Publish with Reply of ...'), 'Invalid Publish Subject', another subscription's handler throwing a ConnectionException in the words of a fatal -ERR, or throwing once its own publish lost the connection and the reconnect reopened it) The collection fails with that error, which `endedTheConnection()` does not recognise, the connection Open (after the handler's one reconnect in the last case), the request or pull sent once. Guard: #196 asks which failure ended the connection, not how the connection looks, so a count of reconnects cannot pass the handler's off as the collection's.

### tests/Unit/OperationReadWakeupTest.php
Each operation that reads the socket while it waits for a result of its own - SubscriptionQueue next() and fetchAll(), request() and requestMany(), fetchBatch(), directGetBatch(), the pull consumer, Key/Value keys() and history() - gets that result as soon as it is delivered, by whichever fiber's read, over the scripted server in `tests/Support/ReconnectingTransport.php` (#174). Each test drives the interleaving by the order of events: a hold-up ends when a read takes the socket during it, which only the operation's can, and the hop sweeps count event-loop hops. The old reads returned at their deadline, 3 s or 1.5 s in the hop sweeps, in every case; the fixed ones return within milliseconds. `READ_WAKEUP_VERBOSE=1` prints the measurements and `READ_WAKEUP_HOPS` sets the sweeps' range, 32 by default.
- `testOperationGetsWhatADeliveryUnderWayInAnotherFiberBrings` - (next() / fetchAll(1) / request() / requestMany(max 1) / fetchBatch(1) / directGetBatch() / PullConsumerIterator::handle() / KeyValueBucket::keys() / KeyValueBucket::history(); guards: next() and request() behind a handler that makes a request()) The application's read took a chunk holding, ahead of the operation's message, a message for a lower-sid subscription whose handler awaits a timer, as it would an HTTP or database call, until a read takes the socket or for 0.5 s. The operation's message is queued behind that delivery, so the own-sid operations take it at once and return their result within 1.5 s of its 3 s deadline, while the handler is still held (#179); request() and requestMany(), with no own sid, still read the socket and wake on their reply. The guard's handler makes a request() of its own, which gets its reply, and the poll its message, without either waiting for the other: the read slot stays free during a delivery.
- `testOperationGetsWhatADispatchWaitingOnAPongWriteBrings` - (the same nine operations) The chunk holds a server PING ahead of the operation's message; the dispatch awaits the PONG's write, held up like one on a socket under backpressure until a read takes the socket, and the message is not even queued meanwhile. The operation returns its result within 1.5 s of its 3 s deadline.
- `testOperationEndsPromptlyWhateverTheHopsBetweenItAndAnotherParty` - (next() and fetchAll(1) with their message arriving around them, with a processIncoming() and with a request() around them; fetchBatch(1), directGetBatch(), the pull consumer, keys() and history() with a processIncoming() and with a request() around them; guards: request() and requestMany(max 1) with a processIncoming() and with a request() around them, which look at the read slot themselves and park on their reply, so that no hop count catches them) Sweeps of 0 to 32 event-loop hops in both orders between the operation's start and the other party's, with a fresh connection per run; a queue's message is on the socket before the reads start, or arrives as the other party. Every run returns the result within 0.5 s of its 1.5 s deadline. The old reads missed one hop count per sweep, 2 to 23 hops from the operation's start, and returned at the deadline there.
- `testOperationGetsWhatTheDeliveryAfterAReconnectBrings` - (next(), fetchAll(1), request() and fetchBatch(1) waiting behind the application's read when the connection drops; next() and fetchAll(1) called while the reconnect is under way) The application's read loop holds the socket when the connection drops at +20 ms, dials are refused until +80 ms, and the server sends the operation's message right after the replayed SUB, which the reconnect reads and delivers; a request's reply comes on the reconnected reply inbox, as one from a service that answers late. The operation, whose read waited for the reconnect, returns the result about 0.1 s in, within 1.5 s of its 3 s deadline, instead of reading the new socket until the deadline.

### tests/Unit/OperationTakesItsQueuedMessageTest.php
An operation's read takes what is already queued for its own subscription before it reads or waits for another fiber's read (#179), over the scripted server in `tests/Support/ReconnectingTransport.php` seen through `tests/Support/WatchedTransport.php`. An earlier read leaves the operation's message queued behind a delivery it has not finished - held up in a lower sid's handler that awaits, which is the very handler the operation runs in when a handler polls a SubscriptionQueue of its own. Before the fix the operation read the socket with its message already queued and returned null / [] / a fetch's expiry at its deadline; the regression tests fail on that code and pass on the fix, the order deciding it (`tests/Support/HeldUpDelivery.php` is still held when the result is in). The guards hold on both.
- `testAHandlerPollingItsOwnQueueGetsTheMessageQueuedBehindIt` - (next() / fetchAll(1)) #179 itself: a user handler on "trigger" (the lower sid) polls the "jobs" queue, and the chunk the read delivers holds trigger ahead of job-1, so job-1 is queued behind trigger's delivery, which this handler runs in. The poll returns job-1 from inside the handler within 0.5 s of its 1 s timeout, without a socket read. Before: the poll read the socket with nothing more to come and returned null at the timeout.
- `testAnOperationTakesItsMessageQueuedBehindAHandlerThatAwaitsWithoutReading` - (next() / fetchAll(1) / fetchBatch(1)) The application's read is held up in a lower-sid "slow" handler that awaits, with the operation's message queued behind it. The operation takes it without a socket read of its own and before the handler is let go - HeldUpDelivery still held when the result is in. Before: the operation read the socket and the handler, let go only by a read taking the socket, outlasted its deadline. The server leaves PINGs after the handshake unanswered: the PONG for the PING the fetch writes behind its inbox's SUB (#175) would end the application's parked read and make it read again, a read the count would take for the operation's.
- `testTheOwnSidDeliveryLeavesTheOtherSubscriptionsMessagesQueued` - The chunk holds trigger (lowest sid), then "other", then the poll's "jobs"; trigger's handler polls jobs and gets job-1 while "other", queued between them, stays undelivered until the handler returns and the read's own pass reaches it. The order recorded is poll:job-1 then other:o: only the operation's own subscription is taken ahead.
- `testAnOverflowOfTheOwnSubscriptionMetThisWayFailsThePoll` - (SlowConsumerPolicy::Error) With the connection-level queue left unbounded and the queue's polling buffer one message, the chunk holds trigger and two jobs messages; the poll from trigger's handler delivers them and the second overflows its own buffer, so the poll throws the SlowConsumerException for its own sid, as it did when the read delivered it.
- `testTheOwnSidDeliveryIsSkippedWhileThatSidIsBeingDelivered` - With the sid marked as being dispatched (a handler of it on the stack), deliverQueuedForOwnSid() delivers nothing and leaves the message queued for the suspended delivery: it never invokes a handler on top of itself. Guard.
- `testTheOwnSidDeliveryIsSkippedWhileADisconnectIsClosing` - With close-intent set (and not Draining), the delivery is a no-op and the message is left for the close to discard (leftoversBelongToAClose()). Guard.
- `testRequestIsUnchangedByTheOwnSidDelivery` - request() reads with no own sid: its reply, queued behind a held-up handler in the application's read, still reaches it from that read, in order. Guard.
- `testAnOwnSidOverflowTakenThisWayIsThrownAndReportedOnlyWithTheOption` - (slowConsumerErrorsFailOperations off / on) The overflow the take meets follows deliverPending()'s rules for the option: thrown to the poll either way, reported through the error listener as well only with the option on; the first message stays buffered, the second is dropped (droppedCount 1).
- `testAnOwnSidHandlerFailureFailsTheReadAndLeavesTheRestQueued` - An own-sid handler that throws fails the read that took the message, leaves the rest of the subscription queued and the sid dirty with no socket read, and the next take continues with it: a and then b are seen across two takes, the second returning IncomingChunkResult(0, false) at once.
- `testTheDirtySetAndTheWakesAfterAnOwnSidTake` - After the take only the other sid stays dirty with its wake untouched; the own sid is clean, its emptied queue kept for reuse (#139), its wake fired, and dispatchingSids is empty.
- `testAHandlerPollingItsQueueGetsItsMessageWhateverThePositions` - (nine chunk shapes: trigger first/middle/last on the wire, its sid below and above the queue's, with an "other" subscription queued between them) The handler on "trigger" polls the "jobs" queue whose message is in the same chunk: the poll gets job-1 within 0.5 s of its 1 s timeout and with no socket read in every shape.
- `testAnOwnSidPollDuringAReconnectWaitsForTheReconnect` - (#179 boundary, by design) The take sits after the read's wait for a reconnect, so a poll whose message is already queued does not take it during an outage: with dials refused the poll returns null at its 0.3 s timeout while the connection is Connecting and the handler still held, and once dials are accepted a poll returns the message queued before the drop. Guard.

### tests/Unit/ProtocolCodecTest.php
- `testEncodeConnectContainsName` - asserts encodeConnect output starts with `CONNECT ` and contains the configured `"name":"test-client"` field.
- `testEncodeHeaderPublishBlockMatchesEncodeHeaderPublish` - asserts the precomputed-block variant `encodeHeaderPublishBlock` produces byte-identical frames to `encodeHeaderPublish` both with and without a replyTo.
- `testEncodeConnectAdvertisesResolvedClientVersion` - asserts CONNECT carries a `"version":"..."` resolved from the installed package and no longer the hardcoded `0.1.0-dev` literal.
- `testEncodeConnectContainsPasswordAuthFields` - asserts CONNECT includes `"user":"alice"` and `"pass":"s3cr3t"` for username/password auth.
- `testEncodeConnectContainsTokenAuthField` - asserts CONNECT includes `"auth_token":"token-123"` for token auth.
- `testEncodeConnectUsesTokenProviderPerConnect` - asserts the tokenProvider callback is invoked per encode (rotated-token-1, then -2), overrides the static token, and is called exactly twice.
- `testEncodeConnectUsesUrlUserPassword` - asserts URL-embedded user/pass (`url-user`/`url-pass`) are used and override the static options' credentials.
- `testEncodeConnectUsesUrlToken` - asserts a URL-embedded token is emitted as `"auth_token":"url-token"`.
- `testEncodeConnectUsesJwtProviderPerConnect` - asserts the jwtProvider callback supplies a fresh JWT per encode, overrides the static jwt, and the nonce is signed (`"sig":"sig:nonce-1"`).
- `testEncodeConnectContainsJwtAuthFields` - asserts CONNECT includes `"jwt"`, `"nkey"`, and a server-nonce-signed `"sig"` for JWT auth.
- `testEncodeConnectJwtRequiresSignerAndNonce` - asserts JWT auth without a nonce signer throws ProtocolException.
- `testEncodePublishWithoutReply` - asserts encodePublish produces `PUB orders.created 3\r\nabc\r\n` using payload length and CRLF framing.
- `testParseServerInfo` - asserts parseServerInfo maps serverId, serverName, jetStreamEnabled, and maxPayload from an INFO payload.
- `testEncodeHeaderPublish` - asserts HPUB output starts with `HPUB orders.created ` and embeds the `NATS/1.0` header block, header line, and CRLF-framed payload.
- `testEncodeConnectContainsNoEchoFalse` - asserts CONNECT includes `"echo":false` when noEcho is enabled.
- `testEncodeConnectDefaultEchoTrue` - asserts CONNECT includes `"echo":true` by default when noEcho is disabled.
- `testEncodeConnectStandaloneNkeyAuth` - asserts standalone NKey auth emits `"nkey"` and signed `"sig"` but no `"jwt"` field.
- `testEncodeConnectNkeyRequiresSigner` - asserts standalone NKey auth without a nonce signer throws ProtocolException with the "requires a nonce signer" message.
- `testEncodeConnectNkeyRequiresServerNonce` - asserts standalone NKey auth without a server nonce throws ProtocolException with the "requires server nonce from INFO" message.
- `testEncodeConnectJwtWithSignerButNullNonceThrows` - asserts JWT auth with a present signer but null nonce throws ProtocolException ("requires server nonce from INFO").
- `testEncodeConnectJwtWithSignerButEmptyNonceThrows` - asserts JWT auth with a present signer but empty-string nonce throws the same server-nonce ProtocolException.
- `testEncodeConnectNkeyMismatchWithSeedSignerThrows` - asserts a configured nkey that does not match the public key derived from the NkeySeedSigner seed throws ProtocolException (skips without the sodium extension).
- `testDecodeInfoLineThrowsOnNonInfoPrefix` - asserts decodeInfoLine throws ProtocolException ("Expected INFO line from server") for a non-INFO line like `PING`.
- `testDecodeInfoLineThrowsOnBareInfoWithoutSpace` - asserts a bare `INFO` line without a trailing space throws the "Expected INFO line" prefix error.
- `testDecodeInfoLineReturnsCommandAndPayload` - asserts decodeInfoLine returns command `INFO` and the raw JSON payload on the happy path.
- `testDecodeInfoLineTrimsWhitespace` - asserts decodeInfoLine trims surrounding CRLF before checking the prefix and still returns command and payload.
- `testEncodeConnectFallsBackToConstantVersionWhenPackageMetadataIsUnavailable` - asserts that when Composer runtime metadata cannot report this package's version (datasets blanked via reflection to simulate a from-source or vendored checkout), CONNECT still advertises the non-empty `FALLBACK_CLIENT_VERSION` constant rather than failing the handshake or sending an empty version.

### tests/Unit/ProtocolParserFuzzTest.php
- `testArbitraryBytesOnlyRaiseProtocolException` - asserts 3000 seeded random byte chunks (0-80 bytes) only ever raise ProtocolException (never another Throwable) and the parser never hangs, with all iterations counted.
- `testRandomProtocolTokenSoupOnlyRaisesProtocolException` - asserts 3000 seeded chunks built from real protocol tokens with adversarial spacing/truncation (plus an empty follow-up push) only raise ProtocolException, all iterations counted.
- `testByteAtATimeReassemblyMatchesSinglePush` - asserts a multi-frame stream (PING/MSG/PONG/HMSG-with-reply/+OK/INFO/empty-payload MSG) fed one byte at a time produces frames identical (type, subject, sid, replyTo, payload, headerBytes, totalBytes, infoPayload) to a single push.
- `testUnterminatedControlLineIsBoundedNotUnbounded` - asserts a >1 MiB control line with no CRLF throws ProtocolException rather than buffering unboundedly.
- `testOversizedDeclaredMsgPayloadIsRejected` - asserts an MSG declaring a 1000-byte payload against a 64-byte bound is rejected with ProtocolException at header-parse time.
- `testParserResyncsAfterMalformedControlLine` - asserts after a malformed `GARBAGE-NOT-A-VERB` line throws, a subsequent `PING\r\n` still parses, proving the buffer resynced past the bad line.

### tests/Unit/ProtocolParserTest.php
- `testRejectsOverflowingSizeField` - asserts a 20-digit MSG size field (exceeding PHP_INT_MAX) is rejected with ProtocolException instead of saturating.
- `testParsesControlFrames` - asserts a stream of PING/PONG/+OK/-ERR parses into four frames with correct types and the -ERR error string `'boom'`.
- `testParsesControlLinesWithMultiSpaceAndEmbeddedTabSeparators` - asserts the whitespace-tolerant control-line split (pinned across the #140 explode fast path and its #163 preg_split revert): multi-space-separated MSG/HMSG lines tokenize, and a tab hidden inside a space-separated token (making a 4-token-looking MSG line really the 5-token reply-to form) still yields the reply-to.
- `testControlLineTokenizationTreatsAnyWhitespaceRunAsSeparator` - asserts the control-line tokenizer treats ANY whitespace run as a separator (guarding the #163 revert of splitControlLine to preg_split): a vertical tab or form feed embedded in an otherwise space-separated MSG/HMSG line still delimits the real fields, a mixed space+tab run collapses to one separator, and tab-only HMSG reply-to lines parse - cases a naive explode(' ') would miscount.
- `testParsesFragmentedMsgFrame` - asserts an MSG split across two pushes yields no frame on the first and a complete frame (subject, sid 17, payload `hello`) on the second.
- `testParsesHmsgFrame` - asserts an HMSG (no replyTo) parses with subject, sid, headerBytes 12, totalBytes 17, and the combined header+payload bytes.
- `testParsesHmsgFrameWithReplyTo` - asserts an HMSG with a reply subject parses replyTo `inbox.reply` along with subject, sid, byte counts, and payload.
- `testBuffersPartialControlLineUntilCrLf` - asserts `PIN` then `G\r\n` buffers until CRLF and then yields a single Ping frame.
- `testThrowsForUnsupportedFrame` - asserts an unsupported command (`WAT ...`) throws ProtocolException.
- `malformedMessageLineProvider` - data provider yielding malformed MSG/HMSG control lines (too few/too many fields).
- `testRejectsMalformedMessageLines` - asserts each malformed MSG/HMSG line from the provider throws ProtocolException.
- `testRejectsMessagePayloadWithoutTerminatingCrLf` - asserts an MSG whose payload is not terminated by the expected CRLF throws ProtocolException.
- `testPropertyStyleFragmentedMsgReassembly` - asserts an MSG wire reassembles to one correct frame across many deterministic fragmentation patterns.
- `testPropertyStyleFragmentedHmsgReassembly` - asserts an HMSG wire (with a Status header) reassembles to one correct frame across many fragmentation patterns.
- `testParsesLargeFragmentedMsgWithEmbeddedCrlf` - asserts a 6000-byte MSG payload containing embedded CRLF bytes reassembles correctly when fed one byte at a time.
- `testParsesLargePayloadDeliveredInTransportSizedChunks` - asserts a 300000-byte MSG payload (embedded CRLFs) delivered in 8 KiB chunks reassembles byte-identically via the #140 pending chunk-list accumulation, a mid-accumulation empty push emits nothing, and a PING arriving in the completing chunk parses as the next frame.
- `testCompletedPendingFrameLeavesTrailingBytesForNextFrame` - asserts a pending MSG payload completes and a trailing PONG in the same push parses as a second frame.
- `testRejectsMsgFrameExceedingMaxSize` - asserts a MSG payload exceeding the configured maxFrameSize throws ProtocolException ("MSG frame payload size is invalid").
- `testRejectsHmsgFrameExceedingMaxSize` - asserts an HMSG payload exceeding the configured maxFrameSize throws ProtocolException ("HMSG frame payload size is invalid").
- `testDefaultMaxFrameSizeAllowsLargePayloads` - asserts the default (8 MiB) parser accepts a 1024-byte MSG payload, yielding one frame of length 1024.
- `testRejectsNonNumericMsgSid` - asserts a non-numeric MSG sid (`xyz`) throws ProtocolException ("Invalid sid") rather than coercing to 0.
- `testRejectsNonNumericMsgSize` - asserts a non-numeric MSG size (`abc`) throws ProtocolException ("Invalid payload size").
- `testRejectsNegativeMsgSize` - asserts a negative MSG size (`-5`) throws ProtocolException ("Invalid payload size").
- `testRejectsHmsgHeaderBytesExceedingTotal` - asserts an HMSG whose header bytes exceed total bytes throws ProtocolException ("header bytes exceed total bytes").
- `testBuffersSubCapControlLineWithoutCrlf` - asserts a 1000-byte partial control line with no CRLF is buffered (returns no frames), not rejected.
- `testRejectsUnterminatedControlLineExceedingBound` - asserts a control line over 1 MiB with no CRLF throws ProtocolException ("Control line exceeds maximum length") as an OOM guard.
- `testResyncsPastMalformedControlLineInsteadOfPoisoning` - asserts after a malformed `BADOP` line throws, the offending line is consumed so a subsequent `PING\r\n` parses normally.
- `testRetainsFramesParsedBeforeMidChunkFailureForCatchSiteDrain` - asserts a chunk of `[valid MSG][garbage line]` throws but retains the parsed MSG (subject/sid/payload intact) for `takeParsedFrames()`, and a second take returns empty (draining is destructive) (#147).
- `testUndrainedRetainedFramesArePrependedToNextPushResult` - asserts retained frames a catch site never drained are returned by the next push ahead of newly parsed frames (MSG then PING), preserving wire order (#147).
- `testTakeParsedFramesIsEmptyAfterSuccessfulPush` - asserts a clean push returns all frames via its normal return, leaving nothing in `takeParsedFrames()` to duplicate into a later drain.
- `testParsesVerbsCaseInsensitivelyAndWithTabSeparators` - Parses protocol verbs case-insensitively and accepts tab separators per the wire spec, while preserving the case and whitespace of arguments and payloads.

### tests/Unit/PullConsumerClientDrainTest.php
The client's `drain()` hands over what a pull consumer run's pulls hold (#207). The pipelined engine behind `PullConsumerIterator::handle()` hands a pull's messages to the handler only when the pull completes, and the client's `drain()` delivered only the connection's own queues: it closed the connection under the run, whose read then failed and handed nothing over (gone on a consumer without acks, redelivered after ack_wait otherwise), and a handler handing a pull over ran on after the drain had returned, every ack failing. The run is now a drain participant: once the drain's flush is done it hands every pull in flight over while the connection is Draining, the drain waiting for that, the handler included, within its budget, and `handle()` resolves with the count; the run issues no pull while the connection is Draining, and a drain whose budget runs out discards the rest from its "drain deadline exceeded" report on, which counts it, also while it closes the connection; a `disconnect()` ends the hand-over, as it ends any delivery of the run. Over `tests/Support/ReconnectingTransport.php` seen through `tests/Support/WatchedTransport.php` and `tests/Support/DrainFlushWatcher.php`, which tells when the drain has written the PING of its flush and when it has read the PONG; the order of events decides, a handler holding a message on a gate the test opens and the server answering the drain's PING only when the test says so, and the time bounds only keep a broken run from hanging, except in the tests that show the drain does not wait, whose bounds sit far from both outcomes (a quarter of a second against a 500 ms backoff, a second against a two-second budget). Each test fails on 2.23.0, where the drain asked no run, except the three declared guards, which pass on both.
- `testAMessageArrivingDuringTheDrainsFlushIsHandedOverWithTheRest` - Batch 3, depth 1: the pull holds m-1 and m-2 when the client drains, and the server sends m-3, which fills the pull, right after the drain's UNSUB of the inbox, ahead of its PONG: the handler, which acks, gets m-1, m-2 and m-3 while Draining, three acks reach the server, handle() returns 3, drain() resolves after the hand-over, and the run pulled once. Before: drain() resolved while the handler was at m-2, and m-3 came on the Closed connection, its ack failing.
- `testTheDrainAsksOnlyOnceItsFlushHasReadWhatTheServerSentBeforeTheUnsubscribe` - Batch 3, depth 1: the pull holds m-1 and m-2, the handler holds m-1 on a gate, and the server does not answer the drain's PING; once the drain has written its UNSUBs and its PING, m-3 comes in and fills the pull, which the run hands over; then the gate opens and the PONG comes: the handler gets m-1, m-2 and m-3 while Draining, three acks, handle() returns 3. Kills the participants asked before the flush, which dropped m-3 as a straggler. Before (2.23.0): the drain did not wait for the run, the ack of m-2 failed with "Connection is not open", and m-3 came on the Closed connection, its ack failing.
- `testTheDrainsHandOverGoesThroughThePullsInIssueOrder` - Depth 2, batch 2, the second pull's write held up while the client drains, so the engine waits in its issue phase: the drain's flush reads m-1, m-2 (the first pull, full) and m-3 (the second) with its PONG and asks the run; once the write is let through the run hands both pulls over at the top of its loop, the handler getting m-1, m-2, m-3 in that order while Draining, and handle() returns 3. Kills the drain's hand-over in reverse issue order, which gave m-3 first. Before (2.23.0): the handler got nothing.
- `testADrainFromAnotherFiberWaitsForTheHandlerAndTheRestOfThePull` - Batch 2, the server filling the pull with m-1 and m-2, so the retire phase hands it over, and the handler holding m-1 on a gate when the client drains from the test's fiber: once the drain has read its own PONG, drain() is still pending; the gate opens, the handler acks m-1 and gets m-2 while Draining, two acks, handle() returns 2, and drain() resolves only then. Before: drain() had resolved while the handler held m-1, and m-2 came on the Closed connection, both acks failing.
- `testADrainAskingWhileTheHandlerRunsAlsoGetsThePullsStillInFlight` - Depth 2, batch 2: the first pull answered with m-1, m-2 and m-3 in one chunk (m-3 into the second pull, which stays open); the retire phase hands the first pull over and the handler holds m-1 on a gate while the client drains; once the drain has read its own PONG and asked the run, the gate opens: the handler gets m-2, and the run goes back to the top of its loop, rather than wait in its pump read with the drain's wake-up fired, and hands m-3 over too, all while Draining; three acks, handle() returns 3, drain() resolves with no deadline report. Kills the check of the drain's request before the pump read. Before: drain() had resolved while the handler held m-1, and m-2 came on the Closed connection.
- `testAHandlerOutlastingTheDrainsBudgetHasTheRestDiscardedAndReported` - (a full pull, in the retire phase / a pull still open, in the hand-over the drain asks for) A half-second budget (requestTimeoutMs 500); the handler acks m-1 once the drain is called and holds it until drain() has resolved: the drain waits to its deadline, reports "drain deadline exceeded: 1 buffered message(s) were not delivered before close" (m-2, which the run still held, in the pull being retired or in the pull in flight), and closes; m-2 is discarded, handle() returns 1, one ack, one pull. Kills the report without what the participants hold, the count without the pull being retired or without each message counted as handed over as it goes to the handler, and, with the full pull, the issue phase without its check of the drain's request, which wrote a pull on the connection the drain had closed. Before: drain() resolved at once with no report, and the handler got m-2 on the Closed connection (full pull) or nothing (open pull).
- `testTheDrainsReportAddsUpWhatEveryRunAndEveryPullStillHolds` - (two runs on one connection, each holding the rest of its pull / one run of depth 2, holding the rest of one pull and all of the next) A half-second budget, batch 2, the server answering each run's first pull with all its messages in one chunk, so that the retire phase hands the full pull over, and each handler holding its first message on a gate the test opens once drain() has resolved: the drain waits to its deadline, and its report names the sum of what the runs still hold, 2 for C's c-2 and D's d-2, and 3 for c-2 in the pull being retired and c-3 and c-4 in the second pull, still in flight; no handler gets more, and each handle() returns 1. Kills the count of only the last participant the drain asked (the report then named 1) and of only the last pull of a run (2). Before (2.23.0): the drain asked no run and resolved without waiting, reporting nothing, and each handler got the rest on the Closed connection, handle() throwing "Connection is not open".
- `testWhatTheDrainsReportNamesIsNotHandedOverWhileTheDrainClosesTheTransport` - A half-second budget, a transport whose close takes 0.2 s (as a TLS or WebSocket close does), the pull full with m-1 and m-2, and the handler holding m-1 on a gate that the transport's close opens, in the retire phase: the drain reports "drain deadline exceeded: 1 buffered message(s) were not delivered before close" and closes; the handler, resuming during the close with the connection still Draining, acks m-1, and m-2 is not handed over; handle() returns 1, one ack. Kills the discard signal read from the state alone, which handed m-2 over and acked it during the close while the report named it as not delivered, and the cursor not moved as each message goes to the handler (the report then named 2). Before (2.23.0): the drain neither waited for the run nor reported anything.
- `testAHandlerGoingOnAsTheBudgetRunsOutGetsNoneOfWhatTheReportNamed` - (a close at once, a handler that does not ack / a close that takes 100 ms, a handler that acks / an error listener that awaits 50 ms after the report, a handler that acks) A half-second budget, the pull (batch 4) holding m-1, m-2 and m-3, the handler holding m-1 on a gate in the drain's hand-over; the error listener that gets the "drain deadline exceeded" report opens the gate, in the drain's fiber, right before the drain awaits the transport's close with the connection still Draining, or while it awaits in the listener itself: the handler gets nothing more, the report names 2, handle() returns 1, and no ack but m-1's reaches the server. Kills the discard signal read from the state alone, which handed m-2 and m-3 over right after the report (their acks out with the slow close) and returned 3, the delivery phase ended only after the report (the third data set), and the cursor not moved as each message goes to the handler (the report then named 3). Before (2.23.0): the drain asked no run, and the handler got nothing.
- `testADisconnectInterruptingTheDrainsHandOverEndsItAsItBegins` - The pull (batch 4) holds m-1, m-2 and m-3, the drain asks the run, and the handler holds m-1 on a gate when the application disconnects; the gate opens as the disconnect() starts closing the transport, the connection still Draining: the handler gets nothing more, and handle() returns 1. Kills a disconnect() that leaves the drain's delivery phase on, and the discard signal read from the state alone: either way the handler got m-2 and m-3 while the disconnect() was closing the connection, and handle() returned 3. Before (2.23.0): the drain asked no run, and the handler got nothing.
- `testADisconnectThatInterruptsTheDrainsFlushLeavesTheRunToFail` - Guard, passes on both, where 2.23.0 asked no run at all: the pull (batch 3) holds m-1 and m-2, the server does not answer the drain's PING, and the application disconnects once it is out: the drain asks no run, the handler gets nothing, and handle() throws "Connection is not open", as after any disconnect(). Kills the drain asking its participants on a connection a disconnect() closed, and a disconnect() that leaves the drain's delivery phase on: the run, asked all the same, discarded what it held and returned 0.
- `testARunGoingOnWhileTheDrainFlushesWritesNoPullAfterTheUnsubscribe` - Batch 2, the pull full, the handler holding m-1 on a gate, and the server not answering the drain's PING: once the drain has written its UNSUBs and its PING the gate opens, the handler acks m-1 and gets m-2 while Draining, and the run, not asked yet, issues nothing while the connection is Draining and returns 2, the connection still Draining; no pull after the drain's UNSUB of the inbox, and the drain closes once its PONG comes. Kills the issue phase without its Draining check. Before: the run wrote its next pull after the UNSUB and threw "Connection is not open" once the drain had waited out its budget.
- `testTheDrainWaitsForEveryRunOnTheConnection` - Two runs on one connection (consumers C and D), each pull holding one message (c-1, d-1), each handler holding it on a gate of its own: both hand over while Draining, C's run ends with 1 while drain() is still pending for D, then D's ends with 1 and drain() resolves; two acks, no pull after either inbox's UNSUB. Before: neither handler was called, and both runs threw "Connection is not open".
- `testARunThatEndedBeforeTheDrainIsNotWaitedFor` - Guard, passes on both: a finite run of one iteration gets m-1 and returns 1; drain() then resolves in under a second of its two-second budget, reporting nothing. Kills the participant left registered once the run has ended.
- `testARunInItsIdleBackoffIsWokenByTheDrainAndEndsAtOnce` - Seven no_wait pulls answered with 404 back the run off up to 500 ms, and a timer 20 ms into the seventh backoff, no read on the socket, drains the client: handle() returns 0 within a quarter of a second, seven pulls in all, and drain() resolves. Kills the backoff without the drain's wake-up. Before: the run waited out the backoff (about 480 ms) and failed at its next pull's write with "Connection is not open".
- `testADrainThatAsksWhileOnErrorAwaitsSkipsTheIdleBackoff` - Six no_wait pulls answered with 404 and a seventh with 503, whose report onError awaits on a gate; the client drains, reading its own PONG, and asks the run, and the gate then opens: the run skips the 500 ms backoff and returns 0 within a quarter of a second, seven pulls in all, onError having got only the 503. Kills the backoff without the check of the drain's request. Before: the run waited out the backoff (about 500 ms) and failed with "Connection is not open".
- `testAHandlerThatThrowsDuringTheHandOverEndsTheRunAndTheDrainDoesNotWaitForIt` - The pull (batch 3) holds m-1 and m-2, and the handler throws on m-1 during the drain's hand-over: handle() throws the handler's exception, m-2 is not handed over, and drain() resolves in under a second, with no "drain deadline exceeded" report. Kills the run's end that leaves the drain's future pending. Before: the handler was never called, and handle() threw "Connection is not open".
- `testAStopDuringTheHandOverLeavesTheRestUndelivered` - The handler acks m-1 and stops the run during the drain's hand-over: m-2 is not handed over, handle() returns 1, one ack, and drain() resolves in under a second, reporting nothing. Before: the handler was never called.
- `testADrainThatWaitedForAReconnectStillGetsWhatThePullsHeld` - The pull (batch 3) holds m-1 and m-2 when the connection drops, the reconnect's dial held, so that the engine's read and the drain both wait for the reconnect; once the dial is let through, the server answers the drain's flush 200 ms later, so that the engine comes round the top of its loop before the drain asks: the handler gets m-1 and m-2 while Draining, only once the drain's flush is done, two acks, handle() returns 2, no pull after the drain's UNSUB and none on the new connection. Kills a run that ends its pulls at the reconnect while the client drains, handing them over before the flush is done (#187); the reset at the top of the loop used to drop them there, the hand-over finding nothing and handle() returning 0. Before (2.23.0): the drain asked no run, and the handler got nothing.
- `testADrainWithoutAConnectionAsksNoRun` - Guard, passes on both: the pull holds m-1 and m-2 when the connection drops with the reconnect's dials refused, and the client drains with a 0.3 s budget, which runs out while it waits for the reconnect: no run is asked, the drain closes, the run's read fails and discards what the pull held, and handle() throws "Connection is not open". Kills the participants asked without a connection, whose run returned 0.
- `testAnAckSyncDuringTheDrainsHandOverIsConfirmed` - Data provider (the reply inbox made before the run, or the first request of the client; the handler catching the failure, or letting it throw): batch 3, the pull holding m-1 and m-2 when the client drains, and the scripted server confirming each +ACK request on a reply subject it still holds: both ackSync() calls are confirmed while Draining, the server gets two +ACK requests, handle() returns 2, nothing is reported, and the reply inbox's UNSUB follows the acks on the wire (#213). On 2.24.3 both failed at once with "Connection is not open", none reached the server, and a handler that let it throw ended the run with it.
- `testAJetStreamPublishAKeyValueReadAndARequestManyDuringTheDrainsHandOverGetTheirReplies` - The handler of m-1 publishes a derived message to JetStream with a message id (an HPUB answered with a PubAck), reads a Key/Value entry (a Direct Get answered with the stored value and its revision), makes a requestMany() of two replies and acks with ackSync(): each is answered while Draining and handle() returns 1 (#213). On 2.24.3 the JetStream publish failed and the run ended with it.
- `testARequestOutlastingTheDrainsBudgetEndsWithTheDeliveryPhase` - A half-second budget, the pull holding m-1 and m-2, and a server that never confirms the +ACK, the handler's ackSync() waiting with a five-second timeout: at the deadline the ackSync() fails with ConnectionException "Connection is not open", not its own timeout, well within the drain's budget; m-2 is not handed over and is reported once, handle() returns 1, and only m-1's +ACK request went out (#213).
- `testTheDeadlineReportCountsWhatTheRunHeldWhenTheDeliveryPhaseWasSealed` - A half-second budget, the pull (batch 4) holding m-1, m-2 and m-3, the handler of m-1 waiting on an unanswered ackSync(): the seal counts m-2 and m-3 (not m-1, which the handler has) before it ends the request, so the report names two, once, although the handler returns at once and the run ends and leaves the drain before the drain looks again; nothing more is handed over (#213).
- `testADeadlineDuringTheFlushCountsWhatTheRunsHoldBeforeTheyAreAsked` - Guard, passes on both: the server never answers the drain's PING and the budget is 300 ms; the seal counts the two messages the run holds, not yet asked, the report names them once, and the run, asked once the flush has ended, hands nothing over and returns 0 rather than fail at the close (#213).

### tests/Unit/PullConsumerConnectionEndingFrameTest.php
An infinite pull consumer run outlives a connection that a frame ended as it outlives a lost one (#210). With reconnect on and waiting for it enabled, the pump read that meets a fatal -ERR ('Stale Connection', 'User Authentication Expired') or a PING whose PONG the socket will not take recovers the connection and throws the frame's error once the reconnect is done, or once its own wait ends (#171). 2.22.0 ended the run there, handing over what its pulls held (#197), with the connection Open again, where after an EOF the run pulled again on the new connection. The run now goes on: the frame's error goes to the error listener and the logger, what the pulls held goes to the handler as a delivery of the run (counted in its total, `stop()` and a close that discards ending it, a group pin captured from it, the idle state reset as by any delivery), and the run pulls on the new connection; while a reconnect slower than the read's wait is still under way it goes round as after an EOF whose reconnect outlasts that wait, the pulls still open kept in flight, and a reconnect that gives up after the read stopped waiting ends it as after an EOF, with the reconnect's error. Over `tests/Support/ReconnectingTransport.php` seen through `tests/Support/WatchedTransport.php`, which counts the reads of the socket; the order of events decides, except for the pull deadlines the slow-reconnect tests let pass (100 ms expiries, a half-second margin between two deadlines), and the other time bounds only keep a broken run from hanging. Each test fails on 2.22.0, where handle() threw the frame's error, except the EOF control data sets and the three declared guards, which pass on both; the two tests of a drain() of the client still Draining, which lets the hand-over deliver what the pulls held and has the run issue no pull (#207), fail on 2.23.0 as well.
- `testAnInfiniteRunGoesOnAfterAFrameEndsTheConnectionAsAfterAnEof` - (an EOF, the control / -ERR 'Stale Connection' / -ERR 'User Authentication Expired' / a PING whose PONG write fails; nothing buffered / the pull holds m-1) Batch 3, depth 1, 30 s expiry, the reconnect reopening the connection at once, and the server answering the pull on the new connection with m-2 to m-4, where the handler stops the run: the run goes on, the handler gets m-1 (when the pull held it) then m-2, handle() returns the number of messages the handler got, one pull on each session, the events Connected, Disconnected and Reconnected, the state Open, and the error listener and the logger got the reason once ("Socket closed by peer (EOF)" from the connection, the frame's error from the engine). After an EOF only m-2 as the last message is checked: the reset at the top of the loop drops what the pull held (#187). Before: every frame data set threw the frame's error (ConnectionException "Server sent error frame: ...", or TransportClosedException "The connection is gone"), and the EOF ones passed.
- `testWhatThePullsHeldGoesToTheHandlerInIssueOrderAndCountsTowardsTheRun` - Depth 2, batch 2: one chunk brings m-1 and m-2 (the first pull), m-3 (the second) and a fatal -ERR; the handler gets m-1, m-2 and m-3, then m-4 on the new connection, where it stops the run, and handle() returns 4 (a hand-over left out of the count returned 1); two pulls on each session. Before: threw the -ERR's error.
- `testAPinnedGroupPullsOnTheNewConnectionWithThePinTheHandedOverMessageBrought` - A grouped run whose pull holds m-1, carrying Nats-Pin-Id pin-1, when a fatal -ERR ends the connection: the hand-over captures the pin as the retire phase does, the pull on the new connection carries "id":"pin-1" (the first one carried none), and the handler gets m-1 then m-2. Before: threw the -ERR's error.
- `testAnUnpinnedGroupFansOutOnTheNewConnectionOnlyOnceTheHandOverDelivered` - (the pull holds m-1 / nothing buffered) A grouped run of depth 2 bootstraps one pull at a time; with m-1, which carries no pin, handed over it is a group that has delivered and pulls two at once on the new connection (epochs [0, 1, 1]); with nothing handed over it still pulls one at a time there ([0, 1]); no request carries a pin. Kills the delivery's reset without its anyDelivered, or run when nothing was delivered. Before: threw the -ERR's error.
- `testTheHandOverReArmsTheNoRespondersSignalAsADeliveryDoes` - The first pull is answered with 503 (onError once); the next holds m-1 when a fatal -ERR ends the connection; after the hand-over the first pull on the new connection is answered with 503 again, which onError hears again ([503, 503]), and the next one brings m-2. Before: threw the -ERR's error.
- `testAHandlerThatThrowsDuringTheHandOverEndsTheRunWithItsOwnException` - The pull holds m-1 when a fatal -ERR ends the connection, and the handler throws on m-1: handle() throws the handler's exception, the run pulls nothing on the new connection, and the error listener got the -ERR's error and only that. Before: handle() threw the -ERR's error, the handler's exception going to the error listener.
- `testAStopFromTheHandlerDuringTheHandOverEndsTheRunWithItsCount` - Depth 2, batch 2, m-1, m-2 and m-3 held when a fatal -ERR ends the connection, and the handler stops the run on m-1: m-2 and m-3 stay undelivered, handle() returns 1, and no pull goes to the new connection. Before: threw the -ERR's error.
- `testADrainOfTheIteratorDuringTheReconnectWaitEndsTheRunWithItsCount` - The pull holds m-1 and m-2 when a fatal -ERR ends the connection, the reconnect's dial held; the iterator's drain() wakes the read's wait for the reconnect, and the run hands both over and returns 2 with the connection still Connecting and the -ERR's error reported; no pull after the drain, and the connection comes back once the dial is let through. Before: threw the -ERR's error.
- `testADisconnectDuringTheHandOverEndsItAndTheRunWithTheClosesError` - Depth 2, batch 2, m-1, m-2 and m-3 held when a fatal -ERR ends the connection, the reconnect reopening it; the handler disconnects on m-1: m-2 and m-3 stay undelivered, and handle() throws "Connection is not open" from the run's next pull, which never reached the server, the connection Closed. Kills the hand-over without the close check. Before: threw the -ERR's error.
- `testAMessageReadWhileTheHandlerRunsDuringTheHandOverGoesToAPullStillToBeHandedOver` - Depth 2, batch 3, the first pull holds m-1 and m-2 and the second none when a fatal -ERR ends the connection, the reconnect reopening it; on m-1 the handler's own processIncoming() reads m-3 from the new session: it goes into the second pull and is handed over after m-2; the run then gets m-4 on the new connection and returns 4. Kills the hand-over without each pull marked done first. Before: threw the -ERR's error.
