- `testAReconnectSlowerThanThePumpReadsWaitStillLeavesTheRunGoing` - 100 ms expiry, so the read waits for the reconnect only up to its pull's deadline (1.1 s), and the reconnect's dial is held past it: the read throws the -ERR's error with the reconnect under way, the run reports it, hands m-1 over and goes round, still running with the connection Connecting; once the dial is let through it pulls on the new connection, the handler gets m-2, and handle() returns 2. Only the first pull's session and the last one's are checked: the pull issued during the reconnect is #187's. Before: threw the -ERR's error at the read's deadline.
- `testAReconnectSlowerThanThePumpReadsWaitReplacesOnlyThePullsWhoseDeadlinePassed` - Depth 2, batch 1, 100 ms expiry, the first pull answered with m-1 half a second in so that the deadlines are staggered, the reconnect's dial held and let through by the logger as the engine reports the -ERR's error at the second pull's deadline: the run keeps the third pull in flight, emptied, replaces only the second, and once the reconnect is done pulls a fresh generation, so the new connection gets three pulls ([0, 0, 0, 1, 1, 1]), as after an EOF; the handler gets m-1 then m-9. Before (2.22.0): threw the -ERR's error; with the hand-over clearing every pull: four pulls on the new connection, a whole generation issued during the reconnect.
- `testARunThatCannotGoOnPastTheFrameStillHandsOverAndThrowsItsError` - Guard, passes on both: (a finite run of one iteration / waiting for the reconnect disabled, the dial held / reconnect off / the reconnect gives up while the read still waits for it, three attempts with dials refused; a fatal -ERR / a PING whose PONG write fails) The pull holds m-1 when the frame comes: the handler gets m-1, handle() throws the frame's error (ConnectionException "Server sent error frame: 'Stale Connection'", or TransportClosedException "The connection is gone"), one pull, the state Open, Connecting, Closed and Closed, and the error is not reported. Kills the go-on condition without its finite, waitForReconnect or Closed check.
- `testAnErrKeepingTheConnectionOpenStillEndsTheRun` - Guard, passes on both: -ERR 'Invalid Publish Subject', which the server keeps the connection open for (#209), fails the read; the run hands m-1 over and throws it, the connection Open, nothing reconnected and nothing reported. Kills the go-on condition without its connection-ending check.
- `testAConnectionDrainUnderWayWhenTheFramesErrorReachesTheEngineEndsTheRunAfterTheHandOver` - The pull holds m-1 when a fatal -ERR ends the connection, the reconnect's dial held, and the application drains the connection, which waits for the reconnect; once the dial is let through the drain takes the reopened connection over before the engine's read throws: the run does not go on, a close being under way, the hand-over before it fails gives m-1 to the handler while the connection is Draining (#207), nothing is reported, and handle() throws the -ERR's error, no pull on the new connection. Kills the go-on condition without its close-intent check, and the deliveries ending at any close. Before (2.23.0, where a guard pinned it): the run handed nothing over.
- `testAClientDrainDuringTheHandOverLetsItFinishAndTheRunWritesNoPullAfter` - (the drain asks for the rest while the handler holds m-1 / the drain still flushing when the hand-over ends) The pull holds m-1 and m-2 when a fatal -ERR ends the connection, the reconnect reopening it, and the handler holds m-1 on a gate while the client drains: once the drain has read its own PONG, and so asked the run, or has written its PING with the server not answering it, the gate opens; the handler gets m-2 while Draining, handle() returns 2, no pull follows the drain's UNSUB of the inbox (none on the new connection), drain() resolves, and the -ERR's error was reported once. Kills the issue phase without its Draining check. Before (2.23.0): the hand-over ended at the drain's close intent, m-2 undelivered, and the run wrote its next pull, then threw "Connection is not open".
- `testAFatalErrThatAnotherFibersReadMeetsLeavesTheRunGoing` - Guard, passes on both: batch 1; the handler awaits a gate on m-1 while the application's processIncoming() takes the socket, then the engine's next pull goes out and its read waits for the application's. The -ERR comes: the application's read fails with it once the reconnect is done, the run pulls on the new connection, where the handler gets m-2 and stops it, handle() returns 2, pulls on sessions [0, 0, 1], and the engine reports nothing about the -ERR, which its own read did not meet.
- `testAReconnectThatGivesUpAfterThePumpReadStoppedWaitingEndsTheRunAsAfterAnEof` - (an EOF, the control / -ERR 'Stale Connection' / a PING whose PONG write fails) 100 ms expiry, so the read waits for the reconnect only up to its pull's deadline (1.1 s), and the reconnect's first dial is held past it: the run hands m-1 over and goes on; then the dials are refused and the held one let through, and the reconnect gives up after three attempts. handle() throws "Reconnect attempts exhausted", as after an EOF, not the frame's error, the error listener having heard why the connection ended first, once; no pull reached a new connection (the one the run issued meanwhile waited in the reconnect buffer, which the give-up discarded), and the connection is Closed. Before: the frame data sets threw the frame's error at the read's deadline.
- `testAnErrorListenerThatStopsTheRunOnTheFramesErrorEndsItBeforeTheHandOver` - The pull holds m-1 when a fatal -ERR ends the connection, the reconnect reopening it, and the error listener stops the run when it hears the -ERR's error: the error is reported before the hand-over, so handle() returns 0, the handler got nothing and no pull went to the new connection, as a listener that stops the run on an EOF ends it. Kills the report made after the hand-over. Before: threw the -ERR's error.
- `testAPinCapturedInTheHandOverMakesALaterPinLossPullOneAtATime` - A grouped run of depth 2 whose pull holds m-1, carrying pin-1, when a fatal -ERR ends the connection: it fans out two pulls carrying pin-1 on the new connection, both answered with 423, and then pulls once without a pin (pins [null, pin-1, pin-1, null]), the server answering that pull with m-2 (pin-2), m-3 and m-4 and the handler stopping the run on m-2. Kills the pin captured in the hand-over without marking the run as a pinned group's (#170), which fanned out pulls without a pin again. Before: threw the -ERR's error.

### tests/Unit/PullConsumerConnectionLossTest.php
What a pull consumer run does with what its pulls have received when it ends with a failure of its own read or of a pull's write (#197). The pipelined engine behind `PullConsumerIterator::handle()` hands a pull's buffer to the handler only when it retires the pull, so a run whose read failed with the connection going, whose pull's write failed, whose inbox the server rejected, or whose read failed for a reason the options make its own, used to end with that error and drop what its pulls held (since #210 an infinite run goes on past a fatal -ERR whose reconnect reopens the connection, so the tests that used one for the failure of an infinite run use reconnect off, or waiting for the reconnect disabled, instead; see `tests/Unit/PullConsumerConnectionEndingFrameTest.php`): messages the server counted as delivered, redelivered only after the ack wait, or never on a consumer without acks. The run now hands them to the handler first, in issue order, `stop()` checked before each message, then throws that error unchanged; a close that discards (`disconnect()`, or the client's `drain()` once it has closed the connection) before or during that delivery ends it as a `stop()` does, and a handler that throws during that delivery ends it and is reported to the error listener. Since #207 the close rule covers every delivery of the run, as it covers the connection's own queues: a `disconnect()` also ends the hand-over of a pull the run retires, and the client's `drain()`, while Draining, hands over what the pulls hold (see also `tests/Unit/PullConsumerClientDrainTest.php`). Over `tests/Support/ReconnectingTransport.php` seen through `tests/Support/WatchedTransport.php`, which counts the reads of the socket: each message goes out once the engine's previous read took the last one and its next read is on the socket, and the failure comes only then, so the pulls hold what the test sent. The order of events decides; the time bounds only keep a broken run from hanging. Each #197 test failed on 2.21.0 with the handler getting nothing, or not the message the failure left, except the five guards (one of them the `disconnect()` data set of the application close test), which pass on both; the five #207 tests (the `drain()` data set of that test, a `disconnect()` from the handler or from another fiber while a retired pull is handed over, and the two that keep a pull a `disconnect()` emptied from counting as an empty retire) fail on 2.23.0.
- `testARunWhoseReadFailsWithTheConnectionGoingHandsTheHandlerWhatItsPullReceivedFirst` - (an infinite run / a finite run of one iteration; waiting disabled / a reconnect of three attempts with dials refused / reconnect off / a fatal -ERR, its reconnect reopening the connection under the finite run and with reconnect off under the infinite one, which goes on past it otherwise (#210) / a lame-duck INFO with waiting disabled, the failover's dial held) Batch 3, depth 1, 30 s expiry: the pull received m-1 and m-2, each in a read of its own, and the handler has seen neither; then the connection goes under the engine's next read. The handler gets m-1 and m-2, in order, and handle() then throws the read's ConnectionException ("Connection is not open", the connection Connecting; "Reconnect attempts exhausted" or "Reconnect is disabled", Closed; "Server sent error frame: 'Stale Connection'", Open for the finite run and Closed for the infinite one; "Connection is not open" for the lame duck, Connecting), one pull on the first session. Before: the same error with nothing handled.
- `testWhatTwoPullsReceivedReachesTheHandlerInIssueOrder` - Depth 2, batch 2, reconnect off: one chunk brings m-1 and m-2 (filling the first pull), m-3 (into the second) and a fatal -ERR, so the read buffers the three and fails before the engine retires anything: the handler gets m-1, m-2 and m-3 in issue order, then handle() throws the -ERR's error, the connection Closed, two pulls on the first session. Before: nothing handled.
- `testARunWhosePullWriteFailsHandsTheHandlerWhatAnEarlierPullReceivedFirst` - (reconnect off / a reconnect of three attempts with dials refused) Depth 2, batch 2: the server answers the first pull with m-1, m-2 and m-3 in one chunk (m-3 into the second pull) and the third pull's write finds the socket dead. The engine retires the first pull (m-1, m-2) and refills: the third pull's write fails, its reconnect off or giving up, and the handler gets m-3 too before handle() throws "Reconnect is disabled" (the write's TransportClosedException "The connection is gone" its cause) or "Reconnect attempts exhausted" ("Connection refused"), the connection Closed and the third pull never on the wire. Before: the same error with m-3 lost.
- `testAPullWriteRefusedOnAnOpenConnectionStillHandsOverWhatAnEarlierPullReceived` - Depth 2, batch 2: the server's first answer brings m-1, m-2 and m-3 (m-3 into the second pull) and an INFO lowering max_payload to 16 bytes, so the refill's 33-byte pull request is refused with a ProtocolException before it is written, the connection staying open: the handler gets m-1, m-2 and m-3, and handle() throws the ProtocolException. Kills the publish catch narrowed to ConnectionException. Before: m-3 dropped.
- `testARunWhoseReadFailsOnAnOpenConnectionHandsTheHandlerWhatItsPullReceivedFirst` - (another subscription's handler throws, handlerErrorsFailOperations / the same handler throws an \Error / a SubscriptionQueue overflows, slowConsumerErrorsFailOperations under the Error policy with room for two) Every failure of the read counts, not only the connection going: with m-1 and m-2 in the pull, the read that delivers to the throwing handler, or brings the queue a third message, fails the run with what was thrown (the handler's RuntimeException or \Error; a SlowConsumerException naming the queue's sid); the handler gets m-1 and m-2 first, and the connection stays Open. The \Error kills the read's catch narrowed to \Exception. Before: nothing handled.
- `testAHandlerThatThrowsDuringThatDeliveryEndsItAndIsReported` - The two pulls hold m-1 and m-2, and m-3, when the read fails with a fatal -ERR, reconnect off, and the handler throws on m-2: m-3 is not handed over, the error listener gets exactly the handler's exception, and handle() throws the read's error, which came first. Before: nothing handled, nothing reported.
- `testAnErrorTheHandlerThrowsDuringThatDeliveryIsReportedToo` - With reconnect off the connection drops while the pull holds m-1 and m-2, and the handler throws a TypeError on m-1: the delivery ends, the error listener gets the TypeError, and handle() throws "Reconnect is disabled". Kills that delivery's catch narrowed to \Exception. Before: the handler was never called.
- `testOnAClosedConnectionThatDeliveryEndsAtTheFirstAckThatFails` - With reconnect off, the connection drops while the pull holds m-1 and m-2: the handler gets m-1 and acks it, the ack fails on the closed connection, the handler lets that exception out, which ends the delivery, the ack's failure goes to the error listener, no ack reaches the server, and handle() throws "Reconnect is disabled". Before: nothing handled.
- `testAnApplicationCloseDiscardsWhatThePullHoldsOnADisconnectAndHandsItOverOnADrain` - (disconnect(), a guard that passes on both / the client's drain()) The application closes the connection while the pull holds m-1 and m-2 and the engine reads for more, the handler acking: a disconnect() discards them (handed over, they reached the handler only after the close had returned, every ack failing), the handler gets nothing, no ack goes out, and handle() throws "Connection is not open"; the client's drain() hands both over while Draining before drain() returns, both acks reach the server, and handle() returns 2 (#207). One pull, the connection Closed. Before (2.23.0): the drain() data set got what the disconnect() one gets.
- `testADisconnectDuringThatDeliveryEndsIt` - The two pulls hold m-1 and m-2, and m-3, when the read fails with a fatal -ERR, reconnect off, and the handler calls disconnect() on m-1, which marks the close as the application's: neither m-2 nor m-3 is handed over, and handle() still throws the read's error. Before: nothing handled.
- `testADisconnectWhileAPullWriteIsHeldUpLeavesWhatAnEarlierPullReceivedUndelivered` - Guard, passes on both: depth 2, batch 2, the first pull answered with m-1, m-2 and m-3 in one chunk (m-3 into the second pull) and the third pull's write held up, the socket under backpressure; a disconnect() under it fails that write with the transport's TransportClosedException "The connection is gone", not a ConnectionException, since the write's recovery leaves the application's close alone. The close discards m-3, the handler having got m-1 and m-2 from the retired pull, and handle() throws that error, the connection Closed. Kills the close check dropped where a pull's write fails.
- `testADisconnectFromTheHandlerEndsTheHandOverOfARetiredPull` - Batch 2, depth 1: the server answers the pull with m-1 and m-2, which fill it, so the retire phase hands it over, not the failure delivery, and the handler awaits a disconnect() on m-1: m-2 is not handed over (#207), and handle() then throws the next pull's write error, "Connection is not open" with no previous, that pull never on the wire. Kills the deliveries without their check of a close that discards. Before (2.23.0, where a guard pinned it): the retired pull was handed over whole, m-2 on the Closed connection.
- `testADisconnectFromAnotherFiberDuringTheRetirePhaseLeavesTheRestUndelivered` - Batch 2, the pull full with m-1 and m-2; the handler acks m-1 and then holds it on a gate while the application disconnects from its own fiber, and the gate opens once disconnect() has returned: m-2 is not handed over, the one ack on the wire is m-1's, made before the close, and handle() throws "Connection is not open" from the next pull's write (#207). Before (2.23.0): the handler got m-2 on the Closed connection, after disconnect() had returned, its ack failing.
- `testADisconnectThatDiscardsAFullPullDoesNotEndAFiniteRunAsAnEmptyRetire` - A finite run of three iterations (depth 1, batch 2) and one chunk bringing a message on a plain subscription, whose handler calls disconnect(), and m-1 and m-2, which fill the pull: the delivery discards both, the pull is still retired as one that brought messages, and handle() fails at the run's next pull, the handler having got nothing. Kills the retire classified by what the handler got, with which the run ended as for an exhausted stream and handle() resolved with 0. Before (2.23.0): the handler got m-1 and m-2 on the closing connection, and the run then failed.
- `testADisconnectDuringTheRetirePhaseDoesNotTurnALaterPullsStatusIntoTheRunsEnd` - Depth 2, batch 2: one chunk brings m-1 and m-2 (the first pull), m-3 (the second) and a 409 Consumer Deleted for the second pull, and the handler awaits disconnect() on m-1: m-2 and m-3 are discarded, onError gets nothing, and handle() throws "Connection is not open". Kills the retire classified by what the handler got, with which the second pull counted as an empty retire, onError got the 409 and handle() resolved with 1. Before (2.23.0): the handler got m-2 and m-3 on the Closed connection, and handle() threw "Connection is not open".
- `testARunWhoseInboxIsRejectedHandsTheHandlerWhatItsPullReceivedFirst` - The server rejects the run's inbox mid-run with "Permissions Violation for Subscription to "<inbox>.*" (sid "<sid>")", as nats-server does when a configuration reload withdraws the permission, while the pull holds m-1 and m-2: the handler gets both, then handle() throws the JetStreamException saying the inbox was rejected by server permissions, the connection Open and the inbox released once. Before: nothing handled.
- `testAMessageReadWhileTheHandlerRunsGoesToAPullStillToBeHandedOver` - Depth 2, batch 3: the first pull holds m-1 and m-2, the second none, when the read fails with a fatal -ERR, waiting for the reconnect disabled, so that the read fails at once while the reconnect reopens the connection in the background. On m-1 the handler waits for the connection to be Open, the server sends m-3 on the new session, and the handler's own processIncoming() reads it: the first pull, being handed over, takes nothing more, so m-3 goes into the second pull and the handler gets it after m-2. Before: nothing handled; with the pull not marked done first, m-3 went into the buffer being handed over and was dropped with it.
- `testADrainedRunWhoseReadFailsStillHandsTheHandlerWhatItsPullReceived` - The iterator's drain() while the pull holds m-1 and m-2 wakes the engine's read once (one cancelled read) and the engine reads on for the pull; the connection then drops with reconnect off: the handler gets both and handle() throws "Reconnect is disabled", one pull. Before: nothing handled.
- `testAStopAsTheReadFailsEndsTheRunAsAStoppedOne` - Guard, passes on both: with reconnect off, the error listener stops the run when the read's EOF is reported; the stop's wake-up ends the read, and the run returns 0 as a stopped run, m-1 and m-2 never handed over and no error thrown.
- `testAStopBeforeTheFailureLeavesWhatThePullsReceivedUndelivered` - Guard, passes on both: in the shape of the pull-write test with reconnect off, a connection listener stops the run on the Closed event that the third pull's failed write raises before it throws: the handler, which had m-1 and m-2 from the retired pull, does not get m-3, and handle() throws "Reconnect is disabled". Kills a delivery that ignores stop().
- `testAStopFromTheHandlerDuringThatDeliveryLeavesTheRestUndelivered` - The two pulls hold m-1 and m-2, and m-3, when the read fails with a fatal -ERR, reconnect off, and the handler stops the run on m-1: neither m-2 nor m-3 is handed over, and handle() still throws the read's error. Before: nothing handled.
- `testAHandlerThatThrowsInTheRetirePhaseStillEndsTheRunAtOnce` - Guard, passes on both: depth 2, batch 2, the first pull answered with m-1, m-2 and m-3 in one chunk and no connection failure; the handler throws on m-1, and handle() throws its exception at once, m-2 and m-3 never handed over and nothing reported.

### tests/Unit/PullConsumerIteratorTest.php
- `testFluentBuilderSetsProperties` - verifies the fluent builder (`setBatching`/`setExpiresMs`/`setIterations`) returns a `PullConsumerIterator` and stores 10/5000/3 via the getters.
- `testDefaultValues` - asserts a freshly created pull consumer defaults to batching 1, expiresMs 3000, and null iterations (infinite).
- `testSetBatchingRejectsZero` - `setBatching(0)` throws `JetStreamException`.
- `testSetExpiresMsRejectsZero` - `setExpiresMs(0)` throws `JetStreamException`.
- `testSetIterationsRejectsZero` - `setIterations(0)` throws `JetStreamException`.
- `testSetIterationsAcceptsNull` - `setIterations(5)` then `setIterations(null)` leaves iterations null (clearable).
- `testHandleProcessesOneIteration` - with batching 1 and 2 iterations, one delivered message is processed then a terminal 404 breaks the loop; returns total 1 and payload `['order-1']`.
- `testStopAbandonsRestOfBatch` - calling `stop()` inside the handler ends the consume loop after the first message of a 3-message batch, abandoning the rest; total is 1 and only `['m1']` processed (#32).
- `testDrainFinishesBatchThenStops` - calling `drain()` inside the handler lets the in-flight batch of 3 finish but issues no further pull; total is 3 and all `['m1','m2','m3']` processed (#32).
- `testOnErrorFiresOnTerminalStatus` - a terminal 409 Consumer Deleted status surfaces a `JetStreamException` to the onError callback (code 409, message contains "Consumer Deleted"), delivers no message, and returns total 0 (#63).
- `testOnErrorNotFiredOnRoutineEmptyWindow` - a routine 404 No Messages status does not fire the onError callback (#63).
- `testHandleStopsOnNoMessages` - an immediate terminal 404 stops handle() without invoking the handler and returns total 0.
- `testHandleInfiniteModeContinuesPastEmptyWindow` - in infinite mode a 404 empty window does not stop the loop; polling continues, the next message is delivered (total 1, `['order-1']`), then a terminal 409 stops it.
- `testHandleInfiniteModeContinuesPastTransient409` - in infinite mode a transient 409 (Exceeded MaxAckPending) keeps polling; the subsequent message is delivered (total 1, `['job-7']`) and a terminal 409 Consumer Deleted stops the loop.
- `testHandleInfiniteModeContinuesPastMaxBytes409` - a 409 "Message Size Exceeds MaxBytes" is a pull-completion status, not terminal: an infinite loop with setMaxBytes() keeps pulling past it and delivers the next message (total 1, `['fits-now']`) instead of stopping the worker permanently (#153).
- `testHandleInfiniteModeContinuesPastBatchCompleted409` - a 409 "Batch Completed" is a pull-completion status, not terminal: the infinite loop re-pulls and delivers the next message (total 1, `['next-batch']`) (#153).
- `testNoWaitInfiniteModePacesConsecutiveEmptyPulls` - three consecutive immediate 404s on a no_wait infinite loop are paced by the escalating idle backoff (monotonic elapsed >= 65ms for the 10+20+40ms schedule; exactly one pull per scripted response, no storm), then the message is delivered and a terminal 409 stops the loop (#153).
- `testSetGroupRejectsInvalidName` - `setGroup()` with an over-long/invalid group name throws `JetStreamException`.
- `testSetGroupAcceptsNull` - `setGroup('g1')` then `setGroup(null)` clears the group without throwing and returns the iterator.
- `testSetPriorityRejectsNegative` - `setPriority(-1)` throws `JetStreamException`.
- `testSetPriorityRejectsAboveNine` - `setPriority(10)` throws `JetStreamException`.
- `testSetPriorityAcceptsValidValues` - `setPriority(5)` returns `$this`; boundary values 0 and 9 are accepted.
- `testSetPriorityAcceptsNull` - `setPriority(3)` then `setPriority(null)` clears the priority and returns the iterator.
- `testSetMinPendingStoresValue` - `setMinPending(42)` returns `$this`.
- `testSetMinPendingAcceptsNull` - `setMinPending(10)` then `setMinPending(null)` clears the threshold and returns the iterator.
- `testSetMinAckPendingStoresValue` - `setMinAckPending(7)` returns `$this`.
- `testSetMinAckPendingAcceptsNull` - `setMinAckPending(5)` then `setMinAckPending(null)` clears the threshold and returns the iterator.
- `testSetMaxBytesStoresValue` - `setMaxBytes(1024)` returns `$this`.
- `testSetMaxBytesAcceptsNull` - `setMaxBytes(512)` then `setMaxBytes(null)` clears the cap and returns the iterator.
- `testSetNoWaitStoresValue` - `setNoWait(true)` returns `$this`; `setNoWait(false)` clears the flag.
- `testSetNoWaitDefaultsToTrue` - `setNoWait()` with no argument returns `$this` (defaults to true).
- `testBuildPullIncludesAllOptionalFields` - with priority/minPending/minAckPending/maxBytes/noWait set, the issued CONSUMER.MSG.NEXT pull JSON contains `"priority":3`, `"min_pending":10`, `"min_ack_pending":5`, `"max_bytes":65536`, and `"no_wait":true`.
- `testBuildPullOmitsUnsetOptionalFields` - when those optional fields are unset, none of `"priority"`, `"min_pending"`, `"min_ack_pending"`, `"max_bytes"`, `"no_wait"` appear in the pull payload.
- `testReusedIteratorAfterStopStartsFresh` - an iterator that called `stop()` in run 1 (processing only `['m1']`) is not pre-stopped on a second `handle()`; the second run delivers `['fresh-msg']`, proving resetLifecycle clears the stop flag.
- `testReusedIteratorAfterDrainStartsFresh` - an iterator that called `drain()` in run 1 (`['run1']`) is not pre-drained on a second `handle()`; the second run polls again and delivers `['run2']`, proving resetLifecycle clears the drain flag.
- `testOnErrorNotFiredOnRoutine408` - a routine 408 Request Timeout status does not fire the onError callback.
- `testHandleRePinsOnStalePin` - a 423 Nats-Pin-Id Mismatch drops the pin id and re-pulls without it (#7); the new pin id from the next delivery is captured and resent on the following pull; asserts total 2, payloads `['order-9','order-10']`, and that pull 1 carries `"group":"g1"` with no `"id"`, pull 2 has no `"id"`, and pull 3 carries `"id":"pin-new"`.
- `testSetDepthRejectsZero` - setDepth(0) throws, so a pipeline depth that would issue no pulls cannot be configured.

### tests/Unit/PullConsumerOverflowTest.php
What an infinite pull consumer run receives that no pull in flight has room for goes to its handler (#187): the run holds it in its overflow, which follows the rules of everything else the run holds (arrival order behind the pulls, no pull issued while it holds messages, the iterator's and the client's drain(), stop(), disconnect(), the drain's deadline report, failures, a terminal status, a frame that ended the connection, group pins); a finite run has none. Over the scripted server in `tests/Support/ReconnectingTransport.php`, which answers a pull with more messages than the run asked for, standing in for the requests a reconnect leaves on the server. Each test fails on 2.24.2, except the declared guard.
- `testMoreMessagesThanThePullsInFlightHaveRoomForAllReachTheHandlerInOrder` - Depth 2, batch 1, the second pull answered with m-1 to m-5: the pulls take m-1 and m-2, the overflow m-3 to m-5, and the drained run hands all five over in order and returns 5 after two pulls. Before: m-3 to m-5 were dropped as stragglers, and the run returned 2.
- `testNoPullIsIssuedWhileTheOverflowHoldsMessages` - Depth 2, batch 1, the first pull's write held up while the application's reads give m-1 to the pull and m-2 to the overflow: once the write is let go, the run issues no second pull, the handler gets m-1 and m-2 with one pull written, and the drained run returns 2. Kills the issue phase that issues while the overflow holds messages. Before: m-2 was dropped.
- `testWhatArrivesWhileTheHandlerRunsFollowsTheOverflowAndItsRules` - (the iterator's drain() / stop() / a disconnect() / the client's drain() / the client's drain() running out of budget / the handler throwing / the client's drain() running out of budget over two runs) Batch 1, depth 1, each run's first pull answered with four messages, the handler holding the overflow's first on a gate while the application's read gives two more to the overflow: the iterator's drain() and the client's drain(), which waits for the handler, deliver all six in order and return 6; stop() and a disconnect() deliver nothing more; a drain whose 200 ms budget runs out reports four messages per run, each counted once (eight over two runs), and delivers none of them after the close; a handler that throws ends the run with its exception. One pull per run. Before: the handler got two, and the report counted none of the rest.
- `testATerminalStatusHandsThePullsBehindItAndThenTheOverflowOverBeforeTheRunEnds` - Depth 2, batch 1, the second pull answered with a 409 Consumer Deleted for the first, then C-1 (taken by the second pull) and C-2 (the overflow): onError gets the 409 once, the handler gets C-1 then C-2, and the run returns 2. Before: C-1 was abandoned and C-2 dropped, and the run returned 0.
- `testARunThatFailsHandsTheOverflowOverAfterItsPulls` - (the handler gets everything / the handler throws on the overflow's first message) Batch 1, depth 1, the pull answered with C-1 to C-3 and a message for another subscription whose handler throws, failing the engine's read under handlerErrorsFailOperations: the handler gets C-1, C-2 and C-3 in order and handle() throws the read's failure; a handler that throws on C-2 ends that delivery, its exception reported to the error listener, and handle() still throws the read's failure. Before: C-2 and C-3 were dropped.
- `testARunWhoseInboxIsRejectedHandsTheOverflowOverAfterItsPull` - Batch 1, depth 1, the pull answered with C-1 to C-3 and a Permissions Violation -ERR for the run's inbox in one chunk: the handler gets all three in order, handle() throws the JetStreamException naming the rejected inbox, and the connection stays Open. Before: C-2 and C-3 were dropped.
- `testReconnectsWhileTheHandlerGoesThroughTheOverflowKeepItsOrder` - Batch 1, depth 1, the first pull answered with C-1 to C-3, the handlers of C-2 and C-3 each running a reconnect through a publish on the dropped connection, and the server sending two more messages for the run as each reconnect subscribes the inbox again: the handler gets C-1 to C-7 in order, and the drained run returns 7 after two reconnects, with one pull written. Before: C-2 to C-7 were dropped.
- `testAFrameThatEndedTheConnectionLeavesTheOverflowToTheNextPass` - Batch 1, depth 1, the pull answered with C-1 to C-3 and an -ERR 'Stale Connection' in one chunk: the frame's error goes to the error listener, the handler gets C-1 from the hand-over of #210 and C-2 and C-3 on the next pass, and the drained run returns 3 after one reconnect. Before: C-2 and C-3 were dropped.
- `testAGroupsFirstDeliveryFromTheOverflowCapturesItsPinAndEndsTheBootstrap` - (a pinned_client group: the pin is captured / an overflow group, which never pins: the bootstrap ends) Depth 2, a group, the bootstrap pull answered with a 408 for it and C-1, which goes to the overflow: the handler gets C-1 and the next generation fans out to two pulls, carrying the pin C-1 brought for a pinned group and none for an overflow group; their 409s end the run with 1: the overflow's delivery captures the pin and ends the bootstrap as a pull's does. Before: C-1 was dropped, and the run pulled again alone, without a pin.
- `testAFiniteRunStillDropsWhatItsLastPullHasNoRoomFor` - Guard, passes on both: setIterations(1), batch 1, the one pull answered with two messages: the handler gets the first, the run returns 1 after one pull, and the second is dropped, as fetchBatch() drops one past its batch.

### tests/Unit/PullConsumerReconnectTest.php
An infinite pull consumer run hands its handler every message it received across a reconnect (#187). The engine ends its pulls in flight once the connection's reconnect count moves and pulls again at once (#120); it used to drop them with what they held, and to drop as a straggler any message no pull had room for: a message the reconnect's own delivery gave the pull in flight, the answer to a pull issued on the new connection before the engine saw a reconnect the handler ran, and what a request the server kept across the reconnect, or a pull written from the reconnect buffer, brought. Over the scripted server in `tests/Support/ReconnectingTransport.php` seen through `tests/Support/WatchedTransport.php`, with deliveries held up by `tests/Support/HeldUpDelivery.php`; where two outcomes compete, the test records which event comes first. Each test fails on 2.24.2.
- `testAMessageTheReconnectsDeliveryBuffersIntoThePullInFlightReachesTheHandler` - The pull consumer's own read (depth 1) brings m1 behind a lower-sid message whose handler holds the delivery up; the connection drops, the application's processIncoming() runs the reconnect, whose delivery puts m1 into the pull in flight, and the hold ends once the connection is Open again: the handler gets m1 before any pull goes out on the new connection, the server is asked once, and the events are Connected, Disconnected, Reconnected. Before: the engine dropped the pull with m1 at its reconnect reset and pulled again.
- `testAnEndedPullWithoutMessagesDoesNotHoldBackTheMessageOfTheNext` - Depth 2: the chunk answering the second pull brings the slow message, a 408 for the first pull and m1, which goes to the second; with the same hold, drop and reconnect, the run ends both pulls, the empty first one unclassified, and m1 reaches the handler before any pull on the new connection; two pulls, both on the first connection. Kills a retire phase that stops at the empty ended pull. Before: a pull on the new connection came first.
- `testADrainAcrossTheReconnectStillDeliversWhatThePullInFlightReceived` - The first test's shape without stop(), the iterator drained once the hold began: handle() returns 1, the handler gets m1, and the run writes one pull. Before: 0, the handler never having run.
- `testAPullIssuedWhileTheHandlerRanTheReconnectKeepsItsMessage` - No hold: the server answers the first pull with m1 and the second with m2, and m1's handler drops the connection and acks, the ack's failed write running the reconnect inline: the run sees the reconnect before it pulls again, and m2 reaches the handler before any third pull, one pull on each connection. Before: the run issued the second pull before it saw the reconnect, then dropped it with m2 and pulled a third time.
- `testAPullIssuedAfterAReconnectInTheHandlerIsNotEndedWithTheOldOnes` - Batch 2: the first pull answered with m1 and a 409 Batch Completed, m1's handler running the reconnect through its ack; the second pull, on the new connection, answered with m2, and m3 sent once the engine's next read takes the socket: m2 and m3 reach the handler before any third pull. Before: the second pull was dropped with m2.
- `testAReconnectInTheHandlerIsSeenBeforeTheNextPullsStatusIsClassified` - Batch 1, depth 2: the second pull's answer brings m-1 (taken by the first pull) and a terminal 409 Consumer Deleted for itself, and m-1's handler runs the reconnect through its ack: the run ends the second pull before its 409 is classified, onError gets nothing, and the run pulls twice on the new connection, whose m-2 reaches the handler; handle() returns 2. Kills a retire phase that classifies the next pull's status after the handler's reconnect, as the next two tests do. Before: the old 409 ended the run through onError with 1.
- `testAStalePinStatusTheOldConnectionBroughtDoesNotDropThePin` - A pinned group (depth 2): the bootstrap pull captures pin-1 from m-1, the next two pulls carry it, and the answer to the second brings m-2 (taken by the first) and a 423 for itself; m-2's handler runs the reconnect through its ack: the 423 is not classified, the two pulls on the new connection still carry pin-1, and m-3 reaches the handler. Before: the 423 dropped the pin, the pull on the new connection went out without it, and the reset then dropped it with m-3.
- `testWhatARequestTheServerKeptAcrossTheReconnectBringsReachesTheHandler` - Batch 1, depth 1: the server holds the first pull and the connection drops with no message; the server, still holding that request, answers it and the run's pull on the new connection together with m-1 and m-2: the pull takes m-1, the overflow m-2, and the drained run hands both over and returns 2, both pulls on the run's one inbox. Before: m-2 was dropped as a straggler.
- `testWhatAPullWrittenFromTheReconnectBufferBringsReachesTheHandler` - Batch 2, depth 1, a pull that expires at once: its deadline passes while the reconnect's dial is held, and its replacement goes into the reconnect buffer, which the test sees before it lets the dial through; the reconnect writes it on the new connection, the run ends it and pulls again, and the server answers both with m-1 to m-4: all four reach the handler in order, pulls on epochs 0, 1, 1. Before: m-3 and m-4 were dropped.
- `testAPullWhoseOwnWriteRanTheReconnectIsReplacedAndWhatItsRequestBringsStillReachesTheHandler` - Batch 2, depth 1, the first pull's write meeting the dead socket and running the reconnect inline: the run ends that pull and pulls again, the server answers both requests with four messages, and the drained run hands all four over in order, both pulls on the new connection. Before: the run read on until that pull's deadline, then dropped the two messages its next pull had no room for.
- `testAPullWhoseOwnWriteRanTheReconnectIsTheLastIssuedUnderTheOldCount` - Depth 2, the first pull's write running the reconnect inline: the run issues no second pull under the old count but ends the first and issues a generation of two, three pulls on the new connection before it reads; the server answers them with m-1 to m-3, and all three reach the handler. Kills an issue phase that goes on after its own write ran a reconnect, and a run that reads on without checking the reconnect count after its writes. Before: the run read on until its pulls' deadline.
- `testAReconnectThatOnErrorRanIsSeenBeforeTheIdleBackoff` - no_wait, depth 1: six 404 pulls grow the idle backoff to 320 ms, and the seventh pull's 503 reaches onError, which publishes on the dropped connection and so runs the reconnect: the run pulls on the new connection before a 250 ms timer started when onError returns fires, instead of waiting out a 500 ms backoff, and m-1 reaches the handler. Kills a retire phase that does not check the reconnect count after onError, or that breaks out of the phase on seeing it. Before: the pull came after the backoff.

### tests/Unit/PullConsumerWakeUpTest.php
- `testAStopFromAnotherFiberEndsThePumpReadAtOnce` - a `stop()` from another fiber while the pump read waits on a silent server (expiry 3 s) ends the run within 0.5 s of the stop with nothing processed, one pull on the wire and the pull inbox released once; the engine used to return only at the pull's deadline, about 4 s after the pull (#181).
- `testAStopFromAnotherFiberEndsThePumpReadWithAPullAlreadyRetired` - the same with depth 2 once the first pull was answered empty at once and retired and the depth refilled: the stop ends the read waiting for the two silent pulls within 0.5 s, with 3 pulls on the wire and no further one (#181).
- `testAStopLatchedDuringThePullsWriteEndsTheRunWithoutAReadToTheDeadline` - a `stop()` latched while the issue phase awaits the pull's own write, after its wake-up could be composed into any wait, is seen by the flag check before the pump read: the run ends within 0.5 s with no read of the socket at all, instead of a read to the pull's deadline (#181).
- `testAStopFromATimerEndsTheIdleBackoffAtOnce` - a `stop()` from a timer 20 ms into the seventh (500 ms) idle backoff of a no_wait run answered empty at once, with no read on the socket at that moment, ends the run within 0.25 s of the stop with 7 pulls on the wire; the engine used to wait out the backoff first (#181).
- `testADrainFromATimerEndsTheIdleBackoffAtOnce` - a `drain()` from a timer during that same 500 ms backoff ends the run within 0.25 s of the drain, nothing being in flight, with no further pull (#181).
- `testADrainFromAnotherFiberWakesThePumpAndStillDeliversTheInFlightPull` - a `drain()` from another fiber while the pump read waits for a pull in flight ends that read at once (one cancelled read, counted on the watched transport) and the engine reads again for the pull, issues no further pull, delivers the batch when the server answers and returns with it processed (#181).
- `testADrainWithASilentPullInFlightWaitsForItsDeadlineWithOneMoreRead` - a `drain()` with a silent pull in flight (expiry 500 ms) still waits for the pull's deadline (at least 1.4 s after the pull) as before, and the latched drain does not spin: exactly one more read of the socket, to the deadline, follows the one the drain ended (#181).
- `testAStopAfterADrainStillWakesThePump` - a `stop()` after a `drain()` ends the read that followed the drain within 0.5 s: the drain's fired wake-up is left out of that read, the stop's is not (#181).
- `testARunStartedAgainAfterAStopGetsFreshWakeUps` - after a run stopped from another fiber, a second `handle()` on the same iterator reads the socket once and stays in that read for 0.2 s (no stale wake-up ends it), then delivers the message the server answers with and ends on the handler's `stop()`, one pull per run (#181).
- `testASecondHandleWhileARunStillWaitsDoesNotMakeThatRunSpin` - a second `handle()` on the same iterator while the first run waits on the socket replaces the first run's wake-ups, which Amp fires as it destructs them: the first run's read ends once and the run then waits as before #181 (fewer than 10 reads of the socket, where a spin made 50 within milliseconds and is braked at the fiftieth), stays active, and still delivers its pull's batch when the server answers it; the finite second run ends by itself on an empty answer, one pull and one UNSUB per run (review of #181).
- `testAStopThenHandleAgainInOneTickDoesNotMakeTheOrphanedRunSpin` - the restart idiom, `stop()` and `handle()` in one tick without awaiting the first run: the second `handle()` clears the stop before the first run saw it and replaces its wake-ups, and the first run goes on without a spin (fewer than 10 reads, braked at 50), still delivering its pull; the runs share the flags, which is why a run is to be awaited before the next starts (review of #181).
- `testAStopLatchedDuringTheFirstPullsWriteIssuesNoSecondPull` - a `stop()` latched while the first pull of a depth-2 generation is written ends the generation there: one pull on the wire, no read of the socket, the run over within 0.5 s and the inbox released once; the issue phase used to write the second pull first (review of #181).
- `testADrainLatchedDuringTheFirstPullsWriteIssuesNoSecondPullAndStillPumpsTheFirst` - guard: a `drain()` latched while the first pull of a depth-2 generation is written leaves the second pull unwritten, and the first, in flight, is pumped and delivered when the server answers it, the run returning with it processed.
- `testAFiniteRunStoppedFromAnotherFiberEndsAtOnce` - guard: a finite run (`setIterations(3)`) stopped from another fiber while its pull is pumped returns within 0.5 s of the stop with one pull on the wire and the inbox released once; it used to return at the pull's deadline as well (#181).

### tests/Unit/PullPipelineTest.php
- `testSinglePullDeliversItsBatch` - asserts a single finite pull delivers its whole batch (total 2, payloads `['a','b']`) and that the pipelined engine uses one long-lived pull inbox: exactly one `SUB _INBOX.JS.PULL.` and one `UNSUB` at teardown, with no per-pull subscription churn (#120).
- `testOverlapIssuesSecondPullBeforeDrainingTheFirst` - verifies depth 2 pipelines even at batch 1: both pulls are on the wire before the first is drained (2 pull PUBs by the first delivery) and the run processes 2 messages before a terminal 409 stops it (#120).
- `testFiniteModeIssuesExactlyNPullsAndStopsOnFirstEmpty` - asserts finite mode (iterations 3) runs serially and stops at the first empty window: a delivering pull followed by a 404 ends the run at 2 pull PUBs instead of issuing all N.
- `testTerminalStatusStopsAndReportsOnErrorExactlyOnce` - asserts a terminal 409 Consumer Deleted stops the run with total 0 and surfaces exactly one `JetStreamException` to onError whose message names the status.
- `testInfiniteRunPollsThroughTransientNoResponders` - verifies a 503 No Responders is transient rather than terminal: the infinite run polls through it and delivers the next message (total 1, `['a']`), reporting the first 503 of the streak once (code 503) plus the later terminal 409, two onError reports in all.
- `testInfiniteRunWithoutOnErrorSurvivesTransientNoResponders` - asserts an infinite worker with no onError configured survives a momentary 503 and still processes the following message (returns 1), instead of terminating silently and resolving like a clean drain.
- `testNoRespondersSignalReArmsAfterRoutineNonEmptyGap` - asserts the one-shot no-responders signal re-arms on any routine non-503 retire (a 404 proves the JS API answers again), so a second outage on an idle stream that never delivers between outages is still reported: both 503s plus the terminal 409 reach onError.
- `testPermissionRejectedPullInboxFailsFastInsteadOfSpinning` - verifies a pull reply inbox rejected by server permissions fails the run promptly with a `JetStreamException` whose full message pins the rejected subject, the server error, the `_INBOX.JS.PULL.>` wildcard to grant and the remedy, rather than spinning forever with no signal on any channel (#167).
- `testStopAbandonsRemainingBatch` - asserts `stop()` inside the handler abandons the rest of the in-flight batch: only the first of 3 messages is processed (total 1, `['a']`).
- `testGroupedUnpinnedRunFansOutAfterFirstDelivery` - asserts a priority group that never receives a `Nats-Pin-Id` pulls serially only until its first bootstrap delivery and then fans out to `setDepth()`, putting 3 pull PUBs on the wire by the second delivery instead of staying clamped to serial (#120).
- `testPinnedGroupReserializesPinRecaptureAfterStalePin` - verifies a pinned_client group whose pin is cleared by a 423 re-serializes its pin re-capture, pulling one at a time (4 pull PUBs by the next delivery) instead of racing pin-less pulls at full depth again (#170).
- `testDeliveringPipelineIsNotClampedByInterleavedNoWaitEmpties` - asserts a lone no_wait 404 interleaved with deliveries does not latch the idle drain (streak below depth): the pipeline keeps refilling at full depth with no backoff pause and no serial clamp, reaching 6 pull PUBs (#169).
- `testProductiveThenIdleStreamStillBacksOff` - asserts the empty streak survives the engine's continuous refill, so a stream that delivers and then goes idle still latches the idle drain, paces its relaunch by the first 10ms backoff step, and clamps the next generation serial (exactly 5 pulls) (#169).
- `testDrainFinishesInFlightBatchThenStops` - asserts `drain()` inside the handler lets the whole in-flight batch of 3 finish (total 3, `['a','b','c']`) while issuing no further pull.
- `testStopLatchedOutsideRetirePhaseEndsRunAtNextLoopTurn` - verifies `stop()` latched while the engine is suspended outside the retire phase (inside a pull request's own publish) ends the run at the next loop-top stop check with no 4th pull issued, and still releases the long-lived pull inbox with exactly one `UNSUB`.
- `testIdleHeartbeatMissWakesTheEngineButIsNotTerminal` - asserts a route-wide idle-heartbeat miss is a wake-up and not a terminal error for the pipelined engine: the miss deadline bounds the first read segment (at least 2 started reads prove the early wake), the pull still waits out its own deadline, and the finite run ends with total 0 and no onError, unlike `fetchBatch()` which throws on the same silence.
- `testExpiredSiblingDeadlineDuringSlowHandlerRetiresWithoutBlocking` - asserts a sibling pull whose deadline expires while the handler is busy is retired on the next loop turn without building a read wait from a negative remainder and without blocking on a read first: the run keeps refilling (4 pull PUBs) and reports the genuine terminal 409 exactly once.
- `testCapturedPinPersistsIntoTheNextRun` - verifies resetLifecycle() keeps the captured pin between runs: run 1's bootstrap pull carries no `"id"`, and the first pull of the second `handle()` re-joins the pin with `"id":"p1"` instead of racing an unpinned bootstrap.
- `testFirstNoRespondersAfterADeliveryIsStillSignaled` - asserts a delivery re-arms the one-shot no-responders signal, so the first 503 of an outage that starts after a productive stretch is still reported (code 503) ahead of the terminal 409.
- `testConsecutiveNoRespondersSignalOnlyOnce` - asserts the no-responders signal is one-shot per outage episode: two consecutive 503s produce a single onError report and only the terminal 409 adds a second, so a downed JetStream is never reported once per poll.
- `testNoRespondersEmptiesPaceTheirRelaunches` - asserts a 503 empty retire warrants the escalating idle backoff: three consecutive 503 generations pace their relaunches by 10 + 20 + 40 ms (monotonic elapsed >= 60ms) before the terminal pull, instead of hot-polling a downed JetStream API (#153).
- `testDrainRetiresAllDueEmptiesInOneTurnWithoutDeadlineWait` - asserts the retire phase retires every due head pull in one turn: with depth 2 and both in-flight pulls answered 404 in a single read chunk under `drain()`, the run ends promptly (< 800ms) rather than sitting out the second pull's client-side deadline on a blocking read.

### tests/Unit/ReadPathProgressTest.php
- `testReadIncomingReportsConsumedBytesOnPartialFrameAndIdleOnEmptyRead` - Feeds a 50-byte MSG frame one byte at a time and asserts `readIncoming()` reports `consumedBytes=true` on every byte-consuming read even while the frame is incomplete (frames=0), exactly one read completes the frame (frames=1), a subsequent empty read reports `consumedBytes=false`, and the reassembled payload is byte-identical (#119). Falsifiable: a naive `consumedBytes = frames>0` would report false on the partial reads.
- `testProcessIncomingStillReturnsFrameCountForMultiChunkMessage` - Splits a 1600-byte message into 7-byte chunks and asserts the public `processIncoming()` still sums to exactly one frame across the partial reads and delivers the payload identically, pinning that the #119 refactor left the frame-count contract unchanged.
- `testChunkedMessageIsCollectedWithoutIdleSleepPerChunk` - Drives `SubscriptionQueue::fetchAll(1)` over a 317-chunk (one byte each) message and asserts the message is collected with the correct payload, and that the transport decorator in `tests/Support/LoopTickCountingTransport.php` counts no chunk read after an event-loop tick (#119). Falsifiable: on the pre-fix path each of the ~316 partial reads sleeps 1 ms, and the loop ticks during each sleep, so the count is 316 - verified by temporarily restoring the unconditional sleep. The count does not depend on the machine's speed (#176).
- `testIdleWaitRespectsDeadlineAndDoesNotBusySpin` - With a live-but-silent socket (blockWhenEmpty), asserts `next()` with a 50 ms timeout returns null at ~the deadline (>= 45 ms, does not bail early), terminates (does not hang), and bounds its read with a cancellation - the genuinely-idle path still yields and honors its deadline (#119).

### tests/Unit/ReadReportTest.php
What a read reports to the error listener about the frames it brought - a message DropOldest or DropNewest discarded, a recoverable -ERR, a malformed async INFO - over the scripted server in `tests/Support/ReconnectingTransport.php`, with subscriptions that hold two messages. The first three tests run for all four (DropOldest, DropNewest, a recoverable -ERR, a malformed async INFO); the SubscriptionQueue test runs for DropOldest and DropNewest.
- `testListenerThatReadsFindsTheRestOfTheReadQueuedFirst` - An error listener that reads on the connection when it gets the report finds the rest of that read queued first: the orders arrive x1, x2 (the report used to run mid-read, and the listener's read delivered x2 first).
- `testLoggerThrowingOnTheReportNeitherFailsTheReadNorCostsAMessage` - With a logger that throws on the report, the read completes, the error listener still gets the report once, the other subscription's message is delivered, and the slow subscription gets the messages its policy keeps (s2, s3 under DropOldest; s1, s2 under DropNewest). The read used to fail with the logger's exception, and under DropOldest s3 was lost.
- `testLoggerThrowingOnAQueueDropNeitherFailsTheReadNorCostsAMessage` - (DropOldest / DropNewest) The same for a SubscriptionQueue's own buffer, full because nobody polled it: with a logger that throws on its drop report, the read that delivers the next job completes, the report reaches the error listener once, the drop is counted, another subscription's message is delivered, and the queue holds what its policy keeps (j2, j3 / j1, j2). The read used to fail with the logger's exception, and under DropOldest j3 was lost.
- `testReportIsLoggedAtItsLevel` - The report is logged once: a drop policy's at debug level, the recoverable -ERR's and the malformed INFO's at error level.

### tests/Unit/ReadWakeupLifecycleTest.php
The wake-up of an operation's read through the life of its subscription and beside other pollers, over the scripted server in `tests/Support/ReconnectingTransport.php` seen through `tests/Support/WatchedTransport.php` (which counts the socket reads and says when one starts), with deliveries held up by `tests/Support/HeldUpDelivery.php`: it fires however the delivery ends, the subscription's removal in the same pass included; it is only ever a reason to look again; a dispatch held up before the poll's message is queued keeps the wire order across subscriptions, while a poll whose message is already queued takes it ahead of another subscription's queued with it (#179). The auto-unsubscribe and drain cases pin that the wake-up is an object the pass fires once, not a count of deliveries, which the subscription's removal would reset to the value a waiting poll had noted.
- `testAPollArmedToAutoUnsubscribeGetsItsMessageQueuedBehindAHandlerThatAwaits` - (next() and fetchAll(1) armed to auto-unsubscribe after 1, their first message; next() armed after 2 with one delivered earlier; guard: no auto-unsubscribe) The poll's message is queued behind a lower-sid handler that awaits; the poll takes it without a socket read and before the handler is let go (#179), and the delivery that hands it over also removes the subscription. The poll returns the message within 1 s of its 3 s timeout, and the subscription is gone.
- `testAPollArmedToAutoUnsubscribeEndsPromptlyWhateverTheHopsToAProcessIncoming` - (next() and fetchAll(1) armed after 1; guards without) A processIncoming() starts 0 to 16 hops before or after the poll, with the message on the socket: 0 of 33 runs late in each sweep.
- `testAPollGetsItsMessageWhenItsSubscriptionIsDrainedAroundIt` - drainSubscription() of the poll's queue while its message is queued behind a handler that awaits, and the poll started 0 to 40 hops after the drain: the drain's flush delivers the message and removes the subscription, and the poll returns the message in every run.
- `testAPollArmedToAutoUnsubscribeGetsWhatTheDeliveryAfterAReconnectBrings` - (armed after 1; guard without) The reconnect's delivery hands the message over and removes the subscription; the poll waiting for the reconnect returns the message within 1 s of its 3 s timeout.
- `testAPollGetsTheMessageWhoseDropReportIsHeldUp` - (the listener returns once a read takes the socket / after 0.3 s) A queue holding one message overflows under DropOldest, and the drop report's error listener awaits, leaving the queue's delivery under way with its buffer empty. The poll's read goes to the socket, and the poll returns the new message within 1 s of its 3 s timeout, once the pass is over; droppedCount() is 1.
- `testDeliveriesKeepTheWireOrderWhileADispatchIsHeldUp` - A dispatch held up before the poll's message is queued keeps the wire order: the chunk holds "first" (the lower sid), a server PING, and the poll's message, and the dispatch waits for the write of its PONG, so the poll's message is not even queued. The poll finds nothing of its own, reads the socket, and once the dispatch goes on "first" is delivered first and the poll returns its message, between 0.5 and 2 s in rather than at its 3 s timeout.
- `testAnOwnSidPollTakesItsQueuedMessageAheadOfAStillQueuedLowerSid` - When the poll's message is already queued behind a held-up error listener, with "first" (the lower sid) queued with it, the poll takes its own job at once, without a socket read, while "first" stays queued for the held-up pass to deliver when it resumes (#179): order is only kept within a subscription. The recorded order is poll:job-1 then first:a. Before #179 the poll read the socket and returned its message only once the pass went on, "first" ahead of it.
- `testTwoPollsOfOneQueueWithOneMessage` - (no other reader / the application's read loop) One message arrives 50 ms in: one poll returns it within 0.5 s, the other is woken, finds nothing and waits to its 2 s timeout, with fewer than 10 socket reads, fewer than 60 checks of the queue's buffer by the two polls together, where a woken poll whose next wait ended at once again and again would check it twice per pause of a millisecond, close to a thousand times or more over its wait as the loop's timer paces it, with the socket unread. Nothing is timed: the queue's buffer is swapped for `tests/Support/CountingBuffer.php`, which counts how often next() checks it, and both polls share it. Only a wait that returns to the poll is counted: a read looping on a timer inside the connection, returning to neither poll and never reading the socket, would escape the count, and no path of a poll on an open connection has such a loop; a process-wide count of the event loop's registrations that would have caught it was tried and dropped, since a reconnect attempt of a client an earlier test leaves behind registers its 500 handshake polls in a burst that landed in this wait on the CI runners. Each poll checks the buffer once as it starts, which the count sampled at the delivery shows, and twice when the message comes - the poll that takes it finds the buffer full at both checks, the woken one finds it empty at both and waits on without looking again - six in all. The test used to bound the CPU time of the whole wait (under 0.5 s) instead, which was dropped for depending on the environment: under Xdebug with coverage on a busy CI runner the wait's own cost reached it.
- `testConcurrentRequestsGetTheirRepliesAsTheyCome` - (5 and 20 requests, with and without the application's read loop) The server answers the waiting requests one at a time, 20 ms apart, in reverse order: each gets its own reply within 0.3 s of the answer, and the reads of the socket stay few.
- `testAParkedPollThroughALifecycleEvent` - (disconnect(), drain() and drainSubscription() with the message in flight, unsubscribe() of the queue, a reconnect with the message after the replay; each with and without the application's read loop) A poll with a 1.5 s timeout is parked when the event happens 50 ms in. disconnect() ends it with a ConnectionException within 1 s; the drains and the reconnect deliver job-1 within 1 s; unsubscribe() wakes it, and it waits to its timeout with fewer than 10 reads.

### tests/Unit/ReadWakeupTest.php
The wake-up of an operation's read (#174): the read of an operation waiting for a result of its own ends, without reading, as soon as that result may have come in another fiber's delivery, wherever the read waits then. These tests pin what OperationReadWakeupTest does not: over `tests/Support/ReconnectingTransport.php` seen through `tests/Support/WatchedTransport.php`, with deliveries held up by `tests/Support/HeldUpDelivery.php`, and on real loopback sockets, with `tests/Support/LoopbackNatsServer.php` as the server. Each regression test fails on the code before the wake-up; the guards pass on both.
- `testASocketReadEndedByItsCancellationLeavesEveryByteForTheNextRead` - (plain TCP / TLS) Six pieces of 1 B to 70 KiB, sent after the read was cancelled, right before it in the same event-loop turn, or well before: every byte arrives once and in order through AmpSocketTransport, over TLS too. Guard.
- `testAWebSocketReadEndedByItsCancellationKeepsThePartOfAFrameItHadRead` - (a small frame / a 200 KB frame collected in chunks) The WebSocket transport's read, cancelled with part of a frame taken off the socket, keeps that part, and the next read returns the whole frame. Guard.
- `testAnOperationOnARealSocketTakesItsQueuedMessageWithoutReading` - A delivery still under way on a real socket, through AmpSocketTransport: the chunk holds the poll's message behind a handler that awaits, so it is queued behind that delivery. The poll takes it without a socket read and before the handler is let go (#179), and next() returns job-1 within 1.5 s of its 3 s deadline with the server sending nothing meanwhile.
- `testAnOperationOnARealSocketTakesItsQueuedMessageThenReadsTheNextWhole` - The poll takes its queued job-1 without a read (#179); the handler is let go, a 120 KB message arrives, and the next poll reads it off the real socket and hands the parser a whole frame.
- `testAnOperationTakesItsQueuedMessageWithoutWaitingForAnotherFibersRead` - The poll's message is queued behind a held-up handler while a second application read holds the socket: the poll takes it at once, before the handler is let go and without waiting for or making a read, the other read still on the socket (#179).
- `testAnOperationWaitingForAReconnectGetsWhatADeliveryStillUnderWayBrings` - The poll waits for a reconnect another fiber runs, with dials refused, when the held-up delivery brings its message: it returns within 1 s, the connection still Connecting.
- `testAnOperationWhoseReadJoinsAReconnectGetsItsMessageAfterTheReplay` - The application's read is parked in a handler that awaits, so the poll's own read holds the socket with nothing queued for it (#179 leaves that wait intact); the server stops answering and refuses dials, the heartbeat reconnects, and the poll's read joins it. The server sends the message right after the replayed SUB, and the poll returns it once dials are accepted.
- `testAFlushGetsThePongADispatchWaitingOnAPongWriteBrings` - The application's read takes a chunk holding a server PING and, behind it, the PONG the flush waits for; the dispatch waits for the write of its own PONG, held up until the flush's read takes the socket. flush() returns within 1 s of its 2 s deadline, once that write goes on and the dispatch takes its PONG.
- `testARequestWaitingForANewReplyInboxDoesNotPollThroughAReconnect` - A request waiting for a new reply inbox to be confirmed while another fiber's read runs a reconnect with dials refused, the fence slot it reads for already failed by the reconnect's first attempt: it looks at its cancellation fewer than 30 times in 0.3 s of the outage (`tests/Support/CountingCancellation.php` counts the looks), and is sent once the new connection confirms the inbox. A slot already complete made a wake-up polled every millisecond. Guard.
- `testAFlushReadingManyChunksBeforeItsPongDoesNotPileCallbacksOntoItsSlot` - A flush that reads 2,000 chunks before its PONG leaves fewer than 10 callbacks on its pong slot: one wake-up per slot, not one per read. Guard.
- `testAPullConsumerWhoseReadWaitedForAReconnectRePullsAtOnce` - A pipelined pull consumer with one pull pending when the connection drops and the application's read runs the reconnect, dials refused for 60 ms: it re-pulls on the new session and handles the message within 1 s of the drop. Its pump read used to go on to the new socket, and it re-pulled at the lost pull's deadline, 4 s.
- `testAnOperationWaitingForAReconnectThatGivesUpGetsItsErrorWhenTheClosedListenerSuspends` - A reconnect that gives up after three refused dials while the Closed listener suspends: the poll fails with "Reconnect attempts exhausted" and its cause (#172), not with "Connection is not open" from a wake-up fired at the close.
- `testAnIdleOperationReadsTheSocketOnceForItsWholeWait` - (next() / fetchAll(1)) A 0.3 s wait on an idle connection makes exactly one socket read: nothing wakes an idle operation. Guard.
- `testADeliveryToAnotherSubscriptionDoesNotEndAnOperationsRead` - A delivery to another subscription, made by the held-up delivery, leaves the poll's read the only one on the socket; the poll then gets its own message. Guard.
- `testARequestsReadIsNotEndedByAReplyForAnotherRequest` - (request() / requestMany(max 1)) A late reply for a request that is gone, delivered on the shared reply inbox by the held-up delivery, does not end the read of the request still waiting, which then gets its own reply on that same read. Guard.
- `testARequestCancelledRightAfterItsReplyArrivedThrowsTheCallersOwnCancellation` - The caller's cancellation, with a reason, fires in the same delivery as the request's reply, right after it: the request throws the caller's own CancelledException, with its reason, not the wake-up's.
- `testAWakeUpThatFindsNothingCostsOneLookAndTheOperationWaitsOn` - The poll waits behind the application's read with nothing queued for it; that read delivers the message, and another poller takes it before the woken poll resumes: a higher sid's handler, delivered after the queue's message, queues the steal, which runs before the wake-up reaches the waiting read one event-loop hop later. The poll returns nothing wrong, waits on with one socket read in 50 ms, and returns the next message. Guard.
- `testAnApplicationReadIsNotEndedByADeliveryInAnotherFiber` - processIncoming() has no wake-up: it still waits for the server after the held-up delivery delivered a message. Guard.
- `testAWakeUpFiresOnceOnTheNextDeliveryAndIsNeverReset` - Reads for one sid share the wake-up until a delivery fires it, and the next read gets a new one; the subscription's removal fires it; a terminal close fires every one and leaves none; a subscription made after disconnect() and connect() gets a sid never used before.

### tests/Unit/ReconnectDisabledCauseTest.php
- `testAReadThatFailsSaysWhyTheConnectionEnded` - With reconnect off, a processIncoming() whose read hits EOF fails with "Reconnect is disabled" whose previous exception is the read's TransportClosedException ("Socket closed by peer (EOF)"). Before #172 it carried no cause.
- `testAPublishWhoseWriteFailsSaysWhyTheConnectionEnded` - A publish whose write fails gets "Reconnect is disabled" with the write's error as its previous exception.
- `testAControlWriteThatFailsSaysWhyTheConnectionEnded` - A subscribe whose SUB write fails waits for the recovery that write started and gets "Reconnect is disabled" with the write's error as its previous exception.
- `testAStreamThatCannotBeParsedSaysWhyTheConnectionEnded` - A read of an unparseable control line gets "Reconnect is disabled" with the parser's ProtocolException ("Unsupported control frame: BOGUS") as its previous exception.
- `testAnOperationJoiningARecoveryTheHeartbeatStartedLearnsWhyTheConnectionEnded` - Data provider (unanswered PINGs, PING write failure, socket closed during the PONG read, protocol violation during it, fatal -ERR during it): with the recovery the heartbeat started held in its socket close, an operation that joins it gets "Reconnect is disabled" with the heartbeat's reason as its previous exception, e.g. "The server did not answer the last 2 PINGs".
- `testAnOperationJoiningTheRecoveryAFatalErrStartedLearnsTheServersError` - An operation that joins the recovery a fatal -ERR started gets "Reconnect is disabled" with the server's error as its previous exception, while the read that met the -ERR still fails with the server's error itself (#171).

### tests/Unit/ReconnectedListenerTest.php
The listener a reconnect announces the new connection to - Reconnected, or Connected for a failed initial connect the reconnect completed - and what it calls, over the scripted server in `tests/Support/ReconnectingTransport.php`. The announcement used to be made inside the reconnect, which counted as in flight until the listener returned: an operation the listener called that found the new connection gone joined that reconnect, waited out its timeout or for good, and the connection stayed Open on the dead socket. Most tests run an action once from the listener (`tests/Support/ListenerAction.php`, recording what it returned or threw and how long it took), and a referenced timer keeps the event loop running (`tests/Support/KeepsEventLoopAlive.php`) so that the old deadlocks fail at their timeouts. `tests/Support/SlowLogger.php` is a logger that suspends on one message. Each regression test fails against the code before the fix, and each part of the fix was reverted alone to check that a test catches it.
- `testReadInAReconnectedListenerThatMeetsEofReconnectsAgain` - The listener drops the new connection and reads (5 s cancellation): the read returns without error in under 2 s, the connection is Open on a live session at epoch 2, and the events are Connected, Disconnected, Reconnected, Disconnected, Reconnected. It used to wait out its budget and fail with CancelledException, leaving the connection Open on the dead socket.
- `testUnboundedReadInAReconnectedListenerThatMeetsEofDoesNotHang` - The same read without a cancellation returns and the connection reconnects again (epoch 2); it used to never return.
- `testFatalErrReadInAReconnectedListenerReconnectsAgain` - The listener silences the connection, pushes -ERR 'Stale Connection' and reads: the read fails with the server's error in under 2 s of its 5 s budget, and the connection reconnected (epoch 2). It used to wait out its budget and stay on the connection the server had ended.
- `testFatalErrReadInAReconnectedListenerWithWaitingOffReconnectsAgain` - The same with waitForReconnect off: the read fails at once and the reconnect it starts reaches epoch 2 on its own; it used to end with the reconnect waiting for the listener, reopening nothing.
- `testPublishInAReconnectedListenerOnADeadNewConnectionIsSentOnTheNextOne` - A publish in the listener after the drop completes, and "PUB orders 17" is written on epoch 2; it used to never return.
- `testRequestInAReconnectedListenerOnADeadNewConnectionGetsItsReply` - A request (500 ms timeout) in the listener after the drop gets "re: from-the-listener"; it used to hang past its own timeout, its publish joining the reconnect with nothing to bound the wait.
- `testFlushInAReconnectedListenerOnADeadNewConnectionReconnects` - A flush in the listener after the drop fails in under 2 s of its 5 s budget with "Connection lost before the server answered the PING" and the connection reconnected (epoch 2); it used to time out after its budget, the connection left Open on the dead socket.
- `testSubscribeInAReconnectedListenerOnADeadNewConnectionSubscribesOnTheNextOne` - A subscribe in the listener after the drop returns its sid in under 2 s of its 5 s budget, and "SUB orders <sid>" is on epoch 2; it used to time out after its budget and drop the subscription.
- `testLameDuckInfoReadInAReconnectedListenerFailsOver` - The listener reads a lame-duck INFO naming another server: the connection fails over (Open, epoch 2). The read used to hang past its own 0.5 s in a failover that joined the reconnect.
- `testPingBehindALameDuckInfoReadInAReconnectedListenerIsNotAnsweredOnTheNewConnection` - The listener reads a lame-duck INFO with a PING behind it in the same chunk (#182): the read returns 2 without error, the connection fails over (Open, epoch 2), and no PONG was written on the connection the failover opened, nor anywhere. The dispatch used to go on with the chunk after the failover and write the PONG on the new connection.
- `testReadInAConnectedListenerOfARecoveredInitialConnectThatMeetsEofReconnects` - Initial dials refused, then accepted: the Connected listener of the reconnect that completed the connect drops the connection and reads; the read returns in under 2 s of its 5 s budget, the connection is Open at epoch 1, and the events are Connected, Disconnected, Reconnected. It used to wait out its budget, leaving the dead socket Open.
- `testReconnectStartedFromAReconnectedListenerThatGivesUpClosesOnce` - With 2 attempts and dials refused inside the listener, the listener's read fails with "Reconnect attempts exhausted", the connection is Closed, and the events are Connected, Disconnected, Reconnected, Disconnected, Closed - one Closed. It used to fail with CancelledException after 0.5 s, nothing announced.
- `testDisconnectFromAReconnectedListenerClosesOnce` - Guard: disconnect() from the listener returns in under 2 s (waiting for the reconnect would last until the 10 s connect timeout, or the listener's own 5 s bound), the events are Connected, Disconnected, Reconnected, Closed, and nothing reopens the connection (epoch 1).
- `testConnectionLostWhileItsReconnectedListenerRunsIsRecovered` - Another fiber's read meets EOF while the listener is still busy: the connection reconnects again (Open, live, epoch 2). That read used to join the reconnect and end with it, the connection left Open on the dead socket.
- `testHeartbeatThatFindsTheNewConnectionDeadWhileTheReconnectedListenerRunsRecoversIt` - With a 50 ms heartbeat and a listener busy for 0.2 s after dropping the connection, the heartbeat's recovery reaches epoch 2 within 1 s. It used to join the reconnect after cancelling its own timer, and nothing noticed any more.
- `testConnectedListenerOfARecoveredInitialConnectCanAwaitAConnectThatJoinedTheDial` - A connect() joins while the first dial is held; that dial fails and a reconnect completes the connect. The Connected listener awaits the joiner and gets it at once (under 2 s of the 5 s it allows): the joiner was settled before the listener ran. It used to wait out its whole bound.
- `testConnectOwnerFailsAndItsJoinerSucceedsWhenTheConnectedListenerLeavesAReconnectInFlight` - (first dial succeeds / first dial fails and a reconnect completes the connect) The Connected listener lets the connection die with dials refused and returns with the next reconnect in flight: the owner's connect() fails with "Connect was aborted before the connection opened", the joiner's succeeds, the state is Connecting, and the connection is Open once dials are accepted. Pinned as it is; the second row used to report success to both, on the dead socket.
- `testOperationsWaitingForTheReconnectCompleteWhileItsListenerRuns` - A request and a flush issued during the outage have both completed when the Reconnected listener, busy for 0.3 s, checks them, and the request got its reply. They used to wait for the listener to return.
- `testPublishParkedOnTheSealedReconnectFlushIsWrittenWhileTheListenerRuns` - With waiting off and a held dial, a publisher fills the reconnect buffer until the flush seals it (checked on the flush gate) and parks; its publish has completed when the Reconnected listener, busy for 50 ms, checks it. It used to stay parked until the listener returned.
- `testDisconnectWhileTheReconnectedListenerRunsDoesNotWaitForIt` - disconnect() while the listener is busy, until the test has measured the disconnect(), returns in under 2 s, before the listener does, with one Closed; the listener's flush afterwards fails with "Connection is not open", and the message the reconnect read after its replay is not delivered. disconnect() used to wait for the listener, bounded only by the 10 s connect timeout.
- `testMessageTheReconnectReadIsNotDeliveredWhileTheListenersDisconnectIsUnderWay` - The listener calls disconnect() with a 0.2 s transport close and returns after 50 ms, the connection still Open: the message the reconnect read after its replay is not delivered when the reconnect goes on, nor after the close, which discards it; the events are Connected, Disconnected, Reconnected, Closed. It used to be delivered during the close, the reconnect checking only that the connection was still Open.
- `testMessageTheReconnectReadIsNotDeliveredByAServiceLoopWhileTheListenersDisconnectIsUnderWay` - The same with a service's run() whose loop's read runs the reconnect: its next read, during the 0.2 s close, leaves the message the reconnect read to the close, so nothing is delivered; the events are Connected, Disconnected, Reconnected, Closed. That read used to deliver it as what an earlier read left queued.
- `testMessageTheReconnectReadIsNotDeliveredByAServiceLoopThatWaitedForTheReconnectWhenTheListenerAwaitsItsDisconnect` - The same with an instant close and a listener that awaits its disconnect(): a request's read runs the reconnect while the service's loop waits for it, and the loop, which resumes once the disconnect() has set out to close the connection, does not deliver the message.
- `testPublishWhoseListenerLeavesAFailoverInFlightIsSentOnTheConnectionItOpens` - A publish whose failed write runs the reconnect, whose listener starts a lame-duck failover with its dial held and returns (state Connecting): the publish succeeds, buffered, and "PUB orders 7" is written on epoch 2 only, once the failover's dial is released. The failover used to join the reconnect and never happen; a retry written unconditionally would hit the socket the failover closed.
- `testPublishWhoseListenerLeavesAReconnectBackingOffIsSentOnTheNextConnection` - The same with the next reconnect backing off between refused dials: the publish succeeds and is written on epoch 2. It used to fail with "The connection is gone", the frame lost.
- `testPublishRetryIsNotWrittenAheadOfTheNextConnectionsConnect` - The same with the next reconnect dialled and its greeting held back (`holdNextGreeting()`): the first control line on epoch 2 is CONNECT, and "PUB orders 7" follows it. A retry written as soon as the reconnect returned would land ahead of CONNECT; the publish used to fail with "The connection is gone", the next reconnect joining the first.
- `testPublishRetryDoesNotGetTheConnectionClosedByAServerRequiringAuthentication` - The same against a server that refuses a connection whose first frame is not CONNECT (`$requireConnectFirst`): no Closed event, the connection is Open and the publish written on epoch 2. A retry written ahead of CONNECT would get the reconnect refused as unauthorized, which closes the connection for good; the publish used to fail with "The connection is gone".
- `testRequestWhoseListenerLeavesAFailoverInFlightGetsItsReply` - A request whose publish runs the reconnect, whose listener starts a held lame-duck failover, gets its reply once the failover's dial is released (epoch 2). The failover used to join the reconnect and never happen.
