- `testSubscribeWhoseListenerLeavesAReconnectInFlightWaitsForIt` - A subscribe whose failed SUB write starts the reconnect, whose listener leaves the next reconnect backing off (dials accepted 50 ms later): subscribe() waits for it within its budget and returns, and "SUB orders <sid>" is on epoch 2. Without that wait it would fail with "Connection is not open" at once and drop the subscription.
- `testPublishWhoseListenerClosesTheConnectionFailsWithTheErrorOfItsWrite` - (an error of this library / a raw stream error, as from a built-in transport) A publish whose failed write runs the reconnect, whose listener calls disconnect(), fails with TransportClosedException - "The connection is gone", the write's own error, or "Transport is not connected" carrying the raw Amp\ByteStream\ClosedException - with the connection Closed and no PUB written. The first row is a guard; in the second the raw error, thrown as it was, would escape a catch (NatsThrowable).
- `testPublishWhoseReconnectADrainWaitsForIsWrittenWhileTheDrainFlushes` - A publish whose failed write runs the reconnect, with its dial held, while drain() (500 ms budget) waits for that reconnect; the Reconnected listener is busy for 50 ms and the drain's PING goes unanswered, so the drain has sent UNSUB 1 on the reopened connection while the listener runs and is still flushing when it returns. The publish succeeds and "PUB orders 7" is written on that connection; the drain then ends Closed. Failing the publish with the error of its write instead would lose a frame the connection could still take.
- `testPublishWhoseReconnectADrainWaitsForFailsWhenTheDrainEndsFirst` - (the Reconnected listener is busy / the logger is slow, and no listener is set) The same, with the PING answered, so the drain is over - UNSUB 1 on the reopened connection, Closed - while the 100 ms announcement still runs: the publish fails with its write's TransportClosedException "The connection is gone" and no PUB is written. Pinned as it is: when the reconnect was announced from inside it, the retry came first.
- `testSubscribeWhoseListenerLeavesTheConnectionNotOpenFails` - (the listener closes the connection / the next reconnect gives up, 3 attempts) The subscribe fails with "Connection is not open", the SUB write's TransportClosedException as its previous exception, the connection Closed and sid 1 not registered.
- `testListenerCallsDoNotNestUnderAFlappingServer` - A server drops each new connection as the listener reads on every Reconnected, 200 times: at most two listener calls are ever under way, the read that ran the first reconnect returns while flaps are still left, no Reconnected call finds the connection not Open, epoch 201 is Open, and the events are Connected then 201 Disconnected/Reconnected pairs. Without the queued announcement the calls would nest one level per connection; the old code stopped at the first flap, the listener's read waiting out its budget.
- `testMessageANestedReconnectReadReachesItsHandlerBeforeItsReconnected` - The listener's read finds the new connection gone and reconnects again, and the next server sends a message right after the client re-subscribes: the order is Connected, Disconnected, Reconnected (epoch 1), Disconnected, the message (epoch 2), Reconnected (epoch 2), then the listener's read returns. The nested Reconnected is announced from the event loop, so the delivery of what that reconnect read comes first. Pinned as it is; with the announcement made from the read, the Reconnected comes before the message.
- `testReconnectedIsNotAnnouncedForAConnectionClosedBeforeTheListenerHearsOfIt` - (closed / being closed, with a 0.2 s transport close / closed and reopened by a supervisor) A SlowLogger takes 50 ms over the Reconnected and the connection is closed meanwhile: the listener hears Connected, Disconnected, Closed (then Connected for the reopened connection), never the Reconnected, which the log records. It used to hear the Reconnected all the same: after the Closed, or just before it while the close was still under way.
- `testConnectionLostBeforeTheListenerHearsOfItIsNotAnnounced` - The new connection is lost while a SlowLogger records its Reconnected, and a read that waited for the reconnect reconnects again: the listener hears Connected, Disconnected, Reconnected - one pair - while the log records both reconnects. It used to hear the Reconnected of the dead connection and a second Disconnected/Reconnected.
- `testInitialConnectLostBeforeTheListenerHearsOfItIsAnnouncedAsConnectedOnce` - The same for a failed initial connect a reconnect completed, lost while the logger records its Connected: the listener hears only Connected, for the next connection (epoch 1); the log records Connected, Disconnected, Connected. It used to hear Connected, Disconnected, Reconnected.
- `testHeartbeatRecoversAConnectionLostWhileTheLoggerRecordsItsReconnected` - With no connection listener and a 50 ms heartbeat (maxPingsOut 1), a SlowLogger drops the new connection as it starts its 0.2 s over the Reconnected, and nothing reads any more: the heartbeat reconnects (Open, live, epoch 2) within 1.5 s. With the open logged from inside the reconnect, as it used to be, the heartbeat joined the reconnect after cancelling its own timer and the connection stayed Open on the dead socket.

### tests/Unit/RepublishAndTransformTest.php
- `testRepublishMinimal` - Asserts Republish::create(src, dest)->toArray() yields exactly ['src','dest'] with no headers_only key.
- `testRepublishHeadersOnly` - Asserts headersOnly() adds headers_only=true to the republish array.
- `testRepublishHeadersOnlyFalse` - Asserts calling headersOnly(true) then headersOnly(false) omits the headers_only key, leaving only src and dest.
- `testSubjectTransform` - Asserts SubjectTransform::create(src, dest)->toArray() yields exactly ['src','dest'].
- `testSubjectTransformWithTokenMapping` - Asserts token-mapping patterns (e.g. 'input.*.data' -> 'output.$1.processed') are preserved verbatim in the src/dest keys.

### tests/Unit/RequestsDuringDrainTest.php
Requests while a drain() delivers (#213). The drain used to unsubscribe the shared reply inbox before it delivered anything, and the connection refused every request while Draining. It now keeps the inbox through its delivery phase, sets it up for a connection's first request, takes requests through the bounded request writers while it delivers, and ends every request still waiting when that phase ends - everything delivered, or the deadline - before it releases the inbox and closes. Over `tests/Support/ReconnectingTransport.php`, `tests/Support/SubscriptionLimitServer.php` and FakeTransport for writes held up by backpressure; `tests/Support/HeldDrainParticipant.php` holds the delivery phase open where a test needs it. Each test fails on 2.24.3 except the six declared guards.
- `testACoreHandlerTheDrainsFlushRunsGetsItsRequestsReply` - Data provider (the reply inbox made before the drain / the connection's first request): a core subscription's message sent right behind the drain's UNSUB of it is delivered by the drain's flush, and its handler's request gets its reply while Draining; nothing is reported, and the drain's UNSUB of the reply inbox follows the request's PUB.
- `testACoreHandlersRequestSlowerThanTheDrainsBudgetEndsWithTheDeliveryPhase` - Data provider (a reply that never comes / one 600 ms late): a 300 ms budget, c-1 and c-2 in the drain's flush, the handler of c-1 waiting on a request with a five-second timeout: the request fails with "Connection is not open" at the deadline, c-2 is not handed over and is reported once, and the drain closes in under 0.55 s.
- `testARequestWaitingWhenTheDrainBeginsEndsWithTheDeliveryPhaseNotTheClose` - An unanswered request of another fiber, parked on the socket while a participant holds the delivery phase open, fails with "Connection is not open" as soon as the participant's hand-over is done, while the transport's one-second close is still under way. On 2.24.3 it waited for the close.
- `testTheDeadlineReportsListenerCannotStartARequest` - Guard, passes on both: a participant holding two messages holds the drain to its 300 ms deadline; the error listener that gets the report makes a request, which fails with "Connection is not open" and is not written.
- `testADisconnectDuringTheDeliveryPhaseEndsItsRequestsAtOnceAndTakesNoMore` - A request written during the delivery phase fails with "Connection is not open" as a disconnect() with a one-second close begins, a request made during that close fails the same way unwritten, and one Closed event is announced.
- `testOnlyRequestsAreTakenWhileTheDrainDelivers` - While a participant holds the delivery phase open, a request is answered while subscribe() and fetchBatch() fail with "Connection is not open"; only the reply inbox was subscribed.
- `testARequestWhoseWriteFailsWhileTheDrainDeliversIsNotRetried` - A request's PUB that fails while the drain delivers fails the request with "Connection is not open" (code 0), the socket's TransportClosedException as its previous, with no retry and no reconnect.
- `testARequestWhoseFailedWriteARecoveringDrainOvertakesIsSentOnTheNewConnection` - A request's PUB fails while Open, the reconnect's dial is held, and a drain waits for that reconnect; once it is let through, the drain takes the new connection over and the request is sent there once and answered.
- `testARequestsWriteWedgedDuringTheDrainDoesNotHoldItPastItsBudget` - Data provider (the reply inbox made before the drain, its PUB wedged / the first request, its SUB wedged): a core handler's request whose write is held up by backpressure ends with "Connection is not open" at the drain's 300 ms deadline, and the drain closes in under 1.5 s.
- `testTheReplyInboxsReleaseWedgedAtTheEndOfTheDrainDoesNotHoldItPastItsBudget` - Guard, passes on both: the drain's UNSUB of the reply inbox, held up by backpressure, does not hold the drain past its 300 ms budget.
- `testAHandlerHoldingTheEventLoopPastTheDeadlineGetsNoRequestTakenAndNoHandlerAnotherMessage` - Data provider (then making a request / and returning): a 300 ms budget, c-1 for A and b-1 for B in one chunk of the drain's flush, A's handler working 500 ms without yielding: the checks of the deadline seal the delivery phase although the timer has had no chance to, so the request fails with "Connection is not open" unwritten, b-1 is not handed over, and the report counts it once. On 2.24.3 a new pass handed b-1 over past the deadline.
- `testADrainADisconnectInterruptedReportsWhatAParticipantStillHeldAtItsDeadline` - Guard, passes on both: a drain() a disconnect() interrupts while a participant holding two messages has not ended reports the two at its 300 ms deadline.
- `testADisconnectThatInterruptsTheDrainLeavesTheReplyInboxToItsClose` - A disconnect() with a 300 ms close interrupts the delivery phase; the participant's hand-over ends during that close, and the drain writes no UNSUB of the reply inbox on the connection being closed. On 2.24.3 the drain unsubscribed the inbox as it began.
- `testADrainWithoutAConnectionEndsTheRequestsWaitingForTheReconnectAtOnce` - Over a transport that cannot stop its dial, held for a second: a request waiting for the reconnect fails with "Connection is not open" within 0.6 s, once the drain's 100 ms budget ran out without a connection, rather than when the dial ends. On 2.24.3 it waited for the dial.
- `testARequestWaitingForAReconnectADrainTakesOverIsSentWhileTheDrainDelivers` - A request made after drain(), both waiting for a held reconnect: once the dial is let through, the drain takes the new connection over first, a participant holding its delivery open, and the request is sent there and answered while Draining. On 2.24.3 it failed with "Connection is not open".
- `testASubscribeWhoseFailedWriteADrainOvertakesStaysRefused` - Guard, passes on both: a subscribe() whose SUB failed on a dead socket, the reconnect held, fails with "Connection is not open" once a drain takes over the reconnected connection, while a participant holds its delivery open, rather than resolve with a sid the drain has unsubscribed.
- `testAFailedReleaseOfTheReplyInboxIsReportedAndTheDrainStillCloses` - Guard, passes on both: the drain's UNSUB of the reply inbox failing on the socket is reported through the error listener, and the drain still closes.
- `testASetUpTheDrainOutlivedWritesNothingOnTheConnectionAClosedListenerOpens` - A 300 ms budget, the first request's SUB held up so that the drain's writes wait behind it and its deadline ends the delivery phase: the request fails with "Connection is not open", the drain's Closed listener connects again, and once the held SUB is let through, as if out before the close, nothing but CONNECT and PING is written on the new connection; one Closed event.

### tests/Unit/ScheduleTest.php
- `testAtFormatsUtcExpression` - `Schedule::at()` formats a UTC `DateTimeImmutable` as "@at 2030-01-01T00:00:00Z".
- `testAtNormalizesTimezoneToUtc` - `Schedule::at()` normalizes a Europe/Warsaw time to UTC, yielding "@at 2030-01-01T00:00:00Z".
- `testAtTimestamp` - `Schedule::atTimestamp(1893456000)` returns the equivalent "@at 2030-01-01T00:00:00Z" expression.
- `testEveryFromSeconds` - `Schedule::every(30)` produces "@every 30s".
- `testEveryFromDurationString` - `Schedule::every('1h30m')` produces "@every 1h30m".
- `testEveryRejectsNonPositiveSeconds` - `Schedule::every(0)` throws `InvalidArgumentException`.
- `testEveryRejectsEmptyString` - `Schedule::every('   ')` throws `InvalidArgumentException`.
- `testCronReturnsSixFieldExpression` - `Schedule::cron('0 0 0 * * *')` returns the valid 6-field expression unchanged.
- `testCronRejectsNonSixFieldExpression` - `Schedule::cron('0 0 * * *')` (5-field unix cron) throws `InvalidArgumentException`.
- `testPredefinedNormalizesAlias` - `Schedule::predefined()` normalizes aliases with/without leading "@" and any case ("daily"->"@daily", "@hourly"->"@hourly", "MONTHLY"->"@monthly").
- `testPredefinedRejectsUnknownAlias` - `Schedule::predefined('fortnightly')` throws `InvalidArgumentException` for an unknown alias.

### tests/Unit/ServerErrorKeepingConnectionTest.php
- `testAnErrTheServerKeepsTheConnectionOpenForFailsTheReadButLeavesItOpen` - Data provider ('maximum subscriptions exceeded', 'Permissions Violation for Publish with Reply of ...', 'Invalid Publish Subject', a bare 'Permissions Violation'; reconnect off and on): the read fails with the server's error, as before 2.10.1, and the connection stays Open on the same socket, nothing reconnects, and a publish still goes out. Since 2.10.1 these ended the connection. With reconnect on, one quick attempt is allowed, so such a regression fails at once.
- `testAnErrTheServerClosesTheConnectionAfterStillEndsIt` - Guard, data provider ('Stale Connection', 'Authorization Violation', 'Maximum Payload Violation'): an -ERR the server closes the connection after still closes it with reconnect off (#171).
- `testAnInfoThatIsNotAJsonObjectIsReportedWithoutFailingTheRead` - Data provider (a number, an array, an empty array): an async INFO that is valid JSON but no object is reported to the error listener ("INFO payload is not a JSON object") without failing the read; the connection stays Open and the server info is unchanged. A number used to fail the read with a TypeError, which 2.10.1 took for a connection failure, and an array replaced the server info with defaults.
- `testAnInitialInfoThatIsNotAJsonObjectFailsTheConnectAsBrokenJsonDoes` - Data provider (a number, an array, an empty array): such an initial INFO fails the connect with a ConnectionException "INFO payload is not a JSON object" over a JsonException, as broken JSON does. A number gave one wrapping a TypeError, and an array connected with default server info.
- `testAReadForAServingLoopReportsAnErrTheServerKeepsTheConnectionOpenFor` - readIncomingForOperation() with alwaysReport (a service's loop) reports 'maximum subscriptions exceeded' to the error listener and returns the frame it read (1 frame, bytes consumed); the connection stays Open.
- `testAReadForAServingLoopStillThrowsAnErrThatEndsTheConnection` - The same read still throws 'Stale Connection', and the connection is Closed (reconnect off).
- `testAReadForAServingLoopReportsAnErrReadAheadOfALineThatDoesNotParse` - That read also reports such an -ERR when a line that does not parse follows it in the chunk: the -ERR, then the parse error, and it returns the frame once the connection has reconnected (Open, 2 connects). It used to throw the -ERR after the reconnect.
- `testAReadForAServingLoopReportsAHandlerThatThrowsAndDeliversTheRest` - That read also reports a handler that throws ('handler a') and delivers the rest of what it read (3 frames: a:x, a:y, b:z); the connection stays Open. It used to throw the handler's failure, leaving the rest queued.
- `testAnOperationsReadReportsAHandlerThatThrowsUnlessConfiguredToFail` - (option off / on) readIncomingForOperation() without alwaysReport reports the handler's failure and delivers the rest, as a serving loop's read does (#173); with handlerErrorsFailOperations it still throws the failure and reports nothing, as it used to, but delivers the other subscription's message first and leaves only a's second message queued (#177).
- `testYourOwnReadThrowsAfterDeliveringTheOtherSubscriptionsAndAServingReadDeliversTheRemainder` - processIncoming() over a chunk of a:x (its handler throws), a:y and b:z delivers b:z before it throws 'handler a', leaving a:y queued (#177); a serving loop's read then delivers a:y although the socket has nothing more (no bytes consumed), and nothing is reported. The read used to throw with a:y and b:z both queued.
- `testYourOwnNextReadDeliversTheRemainderAndSaysItReadNothing` - After processIncoming() threw 'handler a' with a:y queued, readIncoming() delivers a:y before it reads and, with nothing more on the socket (a `tests/Support/FakeTransport.php` whose exhausted queue reads ''), returns 0 frames and no bytes consumed: its result counts what it read, not what it delivered (#186). On 2.19.0 a:y stayed queued.
- `testAnOperationsReadLeavesTheRemainderToYourNextRead` - The same remainder: readIncomingForOperation() without a subscription of its own reads nothing and leaves a:y queued, and the next processIncoming() delivers it and returns 0 (#186). On 2.19.0 your next read left a:y queued as well; an operation's read that continued the remainder, with its own rules, would deliver it first.
- `testAReadForAServingLoopThatWaitedForAnotherFibersReadDeliversWhatThatReadHasNotReached` - The same read, waiting for another fiber's processIncoming() whose delivery is held up in a's handler, which awaits (`tests/Support/HeldUpDelivery.php`), has delivered b's message, which that read had not reached, by the time it returns (without reading), while that handler is still held up; the other read then ends with its 2 frames. (Built on a throwing handler before #177.)
- `testFlushAndRttStillFailWithAnErrTheServerKeepsTheConnectionOpenFor` - Data provider (flush() / rtt()): an -ERR read before the PONG still fails the call with "Server sent error frame: 'maximum subscriptions exceeded'", nothing is reported, and the connection stays Open; only the flushes of drain() and drainSubscription() read on past it.
- `testAFatalErrAfterANonClosingOneInTheSameChunkEndsTheConnection` - One chunk with 'maximum subscriptions exceeded' then 'Stale Connection': the read fails with the fatal one, the connection Closes (reconnect off), and the rejection is reported. 2.10.2 also closed the connection, but failed the read with the rejection.
- `testANonClosingErrOutranksAnOverflowInTheSameChunk` (data sets: the overflow first, the -ERR first) - One chunk with a full subscription queue (`SlowConsumerPolicy::Error`) and 'maximum subscriptions exceeded': the read fails with the -ERR whichever came first, the overflow is reported once, and the connection stays Open. 2.10.2 threw the -ERR as well, but ended the connection with it.
- `testAReconnectWhoseReplayMeetsAFatalErrAfterARejectedSubFails` - Data provider (in one chunk, in two chunks): a replay answered by a rejected SUB and then 'Authorization Violation' fails the attempt, so the one allowed attempt ends Closed: the rejection does not hide the fatal -ERR, and the poll reads on after it reported the rejection.
- `testARejectionTheReplayReadsAheadOfAParseErrorIsReported` - A replay chunk with 'maximum subscriptions exceeded' and then a line that does not parse: that attempt fails on the parse error, the next one succeeds (Open, 3 connects), and the rejection reaches the error listener. Before, it was dropped with the attempt.
- `testTheHeartbeatsReadEndsTheConnectionOnlyOnAFatalErr` - Data provider: the heartbeat's read of a rejected SUB leaves the connection Open, and of a rejected SUB then 'Stale Connection' Closes it.
- `testAReconnectWhoseReplayedSubTheServerRejectsCompletes` - After the server rejected a SUB for exceeding the maximum subscriptions, a reconnect replays it, the server rejects it again, and the reconnect still completes (one reconnect, Open) with the rejection reported to the error listener. Before, every attempt failed until the reconnect gave up.
- `testTheClosedEventOfAHeartbeatThatGaveUpCarriesTheReason` - Data provider (maxPingsOut 0, 1 and 2): with reconnect off, the heartbeat giving up on unanswered PINGs closes the connection with one Closed event carrying the reason ("The heartbeat allows no unanswered PING (maxPingsOut is 0)" / "The server did not answer the last PING" / "... the last 2 PINGs") (#172).

### tests/Unit/ServiceTest.php

- `testDoneHandlerFiresOnceOnStop` - Asserts the onDone handler fires exactly once per stop (a second stop() does not re-fire), and that restarting re-arms it so a subsequent stop fires it again (#57).
- `testEndpointStatsHandlerMergesCustomData` - Asserts a per-endpoint stats supplier's custom data (e.g. `queue_depth` and the endpoint name) is merged into the endpoint's `data` key in the stats snapshot (#50).
- `testGroupedEndpointForwardsMetadataAndStatsHandler` - Asserts a grouped endpoint gets the group-prefixed subject (`v1.work`) and that its stats supplier output (`['ok' => true]`) appears as the endpoint `data` (#40).
- `testDrainUnsubscribesAndFlushes` - Asserts drain() emits UNSUB frames for endpoints/discovery and a PING to flush them over the wire (#51).
- `testStartRegistersSubscriptions` - Asserts start() writes discovery SUBs ($SRV.PING, $SRV.INFO.echo, $SRV.STATS.echo) and the endpoint SUB with its queue group (`svc.echo q.echo`).
- `testInfoIncludesEndpointMetadata` - Asserts the $SRV.INFO discovery response includes per-endpoint metadata (`"metadata":{"team":"core"}`) per the NATS micro spec.
- `testAddEndpointRejectsNonTokenName` - Asserts an endpoint name that is not a valid NATS token (`my endpoint!`) is rejected at registration with an InvalidArgumentException mentioning ADR-32, so an invalid name is never advertised verbatim through $SRV.INFO/$SRV.STATS.
- `testDiscoveryEncodesEmptyMetadataAsObject` - Asserts empty metadata maps serialize as JSON objects (`"metadata":{}`) and never as arrays (`[]`) in the PING, INFO, and STATS discovery responses, so ADR-32 conformant Go tooling can unmarshal them.
- `testInfoIncludesEndpointSchema` - Asserts a declared endpoint schema appears in the standard $SRV.INFO `io.nats.micro.v1.info_response` (`"schema":{"type":"object"...`) (#101).
- `testInfoOmitsSchemaWhenEndpointHasNone` - Asserts an endpoint without a declared schema emits an info_response that contains no `"schema"` key.
- `testDiscoveryReplies` - Asserts PING, INFO, and STATS discovery requests each produce a PUB to the reply inbox with the matching `io.nats.micro.v1.{ping,info,stats}_response` type.
- `testEndpointHandlesRequests` - Asserts an endpoint request is dispatched to the handler and its array result is JSON-encoded and published to the reply subject (`PUB _INBOX.req` / `{"echo":"hello"}`).
- `testEndpointResponseEncodeFailureDoesNotTearDownDispatch` - Asserts a handler whose result cannot be json_encoded yields a controlled 500 (Nats-Service-Error) for that request without aborting the dispatch loop, so a subsequent good endpoint is still served (#97).
- `testStopUnsubscribesAll` - Asserts stop() writes UNSUB for both the discovery SID (1) and the endpoint SID (13).
- `testGroupedEndpointHierarchy` - Asserts nested addGroup('svc')->addGroup('v1') prefixes the endpoint subject to `svc.v1.echo` (SUB with queue group `q`), routes the request to the handler, and reflects the subject in the stats snapshot.
- `testGroupJoinHandlesEmptySegments` - Asserts empty group prefixes / empty subjects are trimmed so subjects collapse to `echo` and `svc` rather than containing empty dot segments.
- `testSchemaDiscoveryResponse` - Asserts the service subscribes to $SRV.SCHEMA and answers a SCHEMA discovery request with an `io.nats.micro.v1.schema_response` containing the endpoint schema.
- `testStatsIncludeDetailedMetrics` - Asserts endpoint stats track num_requests (2), num_errors (1), last_error message, non-negative processing/average processing time, and that the `started` timestamp is stable across snapshots.
- `testStartedTimestampIsUtcRegardlessOfDefaultTimezone` - With the process default timezone set to Pacific/Kiritimati (+14), the stats `started` timestamp still parses as UTC within a minute of now, so its hardcoded Z suffix is truthful (ADR-32, #132).
- `testHandlerCanRespondWithCustomServiceError` - Asserts a thrown ServiceError(429,...) surfaces its code/description/body in the micro-spec error headers and body (not a generic 500) and is recorded as one error with the custom description in stats.
- `testHandlerErrorResponseDoesNotLeakExceptionMessage` - Asserts a generic handler exception returns a sanitized "Internal server error" 500 to the requester (no raw exception text), while the real message is retained server-side in `last_error`/STATS.
- `testStatsOmitsNonSpecAliasKeys` - Asserts endpoint stats use spec field names (`num_requests`, `num_errors`) and no longer expose the non-spec aliases (`requests`, `errors`).
- `testServiceRejectsInvalidName` - Asserts creating a service with a dotted name throws InvalidArgumentException mentioning "Service name".
- `testServiceRejectsNonSemverVersion` - Asserts creating a service with a non-semver version ('v1') throws InvalidArgumentException mentioning "semantic version".
- `testValidationRejectionEmitsRequestEnd` - Asserts a request rejected by the request validator still emits the full observer sequence `request_start, request_error, request_end` so opened spans are not leaked.
- `testStartRollsBackAndClearsStateOnPartialFailure` - Asserts that when a later endpoint's subscribe fails during start(), the partial state is rolled back (empty subscriptionSids) and the service is left not-started.
- `testResetClearsStats` - Asserts reset() zeroes num_requests, num_errors, processing_time, average_processing_time and nulls last_error in the stats snapshot.
- `testRequestValidatorCanRejectRequests` - Asserts a request validator returning an error string blocks the handler, publishes a VALIDATION_ERROR `io.nats.micro.v1.error` envelope with the message, and counts the request as one request and one error.
- `testRequestValidatorThatThrowsIsAnsweredLikeAHandlerThatThrows` - A request validator that throws RuntimeException('validator broke'): the handler does not run, observers see request_start, request_error (HANDLER_ERROR) and request_end, the endpoint counts 1 request and 1 error with last_error 'validator broke', and the requester gets an HPUB HANDLER_ERROR reply with code 500 that does not contain the exception text. The exception used to escape into the read: no reply, no error counted.
- `testRequestValidatorThatThrowsAServiceErrorGetsTheReplyItChose` - A validator that throws ServiceError(503, 'schema registry unavailable') gets that reply (Nats-Service-Error-Code:503 and its description), as a handler that throws one does, and the error is counted with that last_error.
- `testObserversReceiveLifecycleEvents` - Asserts observers receive request_start then request_end events carrying the correlation id (from X-Request-Id header) and the request subject.
- `testSuccessfulRequestWithHeadersAndNoObserversRepliesCorrectly` - Asserts a successful request carrying headers on a service with zero observers still publishes the correct reply and counts one request / zero errors; pins the #140 path where the observer context (header parse) is skipped entirely (no counting seam exists, so behavior is the observable).
- `testWithSchemaValidatorUsesAdapter` - Asserts withSchemaValidator(BasicJsonSchemaValidator) validates the payload against the endpoint schema and emits a VALIDATION_ERROR with a type-mismatch message (`$.id must be integer, got string`).
- `testValidationReplyToleratesNonUtf8CorrelationHeader` - Asserts a requester-supplied non-UTF-8 X-Request-Id on a schema-failing request still gets its VALIDATION_ERROR reply published, with the poisoned correlation id omitted rather than encoded, and no JsonException escaping into the shared dispatch loop (#97).
- `testServiceErrorCrLfCodeIsSanitizedAndReplyStillSent` - Asserts a ServiceError whose code contains CR/LF is collapsed onto one header line (`Nats-Service-Error-Code:400 X-Injected: 1`), so header injection is blocked, the error reply is still sent, and the header build never throws out of the dispatch loop.
- `testReplyPublishConnectionFailureIsRecordedNotEscaping` - Asserts a reply publish that fails because the connection closed while a successful handler ran is recorded on the endpoint (one request, one error, `last_error` containing "not open") and emits a trailing request_error instead of escaping into the shared dispatch loop.
- `testHandlerErrorWithFailedErrorReplyCountsSingleError` - Asserts a handler error whose error-reply publish also fails is counted as exactly one error (so num_errors never exceeds num_requests) and emits a single request_error carrying the handler's own code before the terminal request_end, with only `last_error` recording the later publish failure.
- `testErrorEnvelopeIncludesCorrelationIdFromHeaders` - Asserts a handler error's JSON envelope carries `"code":"HANDLER_ERROR"` and the `correlation_id` taken from the request's X-Request-Id header.
- `testRequestWithAnIntegerHeaderNameIsStillAnswered` - Data provider (an observer and a handler that answers / no observer and a handler that throws): a request carrying the header `1: x` next to `X-Request-Id:req-1` is answered ('echo:hello', or a HANDLER_ERROR reply with correlation_id req-1) and the observer sees req-1 as the correlation id. The int key PHP makes of the name `1` used to throw a TypeError out of the endpoint, and no reply was sent.
- `testEndpointAcceptsObjectHandlerAdapter` - Asserts an endpoint handler object implementing ServiceEndpointHandlerInterface is invoked (reply contains `obj:hello`).
- `testEndpointAcceptsClassStringHandlerAdapter` - Asserts a class-string handler is auto-instantiated and invoked (reply contains `class:hello`).
- `testEndpointRejectsInvalidObjectHandlerAdapter` - Asserts passing an object that does not implement the handler interface throws InvalidArgumentException ("Unsupported service endpoint handler").
- `testRunProcessesAndStopsOnTimeout` - Asserts run(0.03) processes an incoming request (publishes `run:hello`) and auto-stops on timeout, unsubscribing the service SIDs (`UNSUB 1`).
- `testRunSupportsExternalCancellation` - Asserts run() driven by a DeferredCancellation stops when externally cancelled and still unsubscribes both discovery (1) and endpoint (13) SIDs.
- `testEndpointDefaultsToSpecQueueGroup` - Asserts endpoints default to the micro-spec queue group `q` (`SUB svc.echo q 13`) while discovery subscriptions stay non-queued (`SUB $SRV.PING 1`).
- `testEndpointEmptyStringQueueGroupOptsOut` - Asserts an empty-string queue group produces a plain non-queued SUB (`SUB svc.echo 13`, no `SUB svc.echo q`).
- `testEndpointNullQueueGroupOptsOut` - Asserts a null queue group likewise produces a plain non-queued SUB and omits the default `q` queue group.
- `testRunPassesCancellationIntoSocketRead` - Asserts the run loop passes a real cancellation into the idle socket read so the read is torn down rather than orphaned (lastReadHadCancellation true; startedReads > 0 and equal to resolvedReads).
- `testRunLeavesConnectionReusableAfterTimeout` - Asserts that after run() times out the shared NatsConnection's `readInProgress` flag is false, leaving the connection reusable (read not orphaned).
- `testAddEndpointRejectsDuplicateSubject` - Asserts adding a second endpoint on the same subject throws InvalidArgumentException.
- `testAddEndpointRejectsEmptyName` - Asserts adding an endpoint with a blank/whitespace name throws InvalidArgumentException ("name must be non-empty and match").
- `testAddEndpointRejectsEmptySubject` - Asserts adding an endpoint with an empty subject throws InvalidArgumentException ("subject must not be empty").
- `testClassHandlerWithRequiredConstructorArgIsRejected` - Asserts a class-string handler requiring a constructor argument is rejected with InvalidArgumentException ("could not be instantiated") rather than a raw ArgumentCountError.
- `testStopToleratesClosedConnection` - Asserts stop() after the connection is disconnected completes without aborting (unsubscribe() releases local state silently on a closed connection per #116) and still clears subscriptionSids.
- `testRunStopsWhenConnectionIsUnrecoverable` - Asserts run() with no timeout returns (within a 2s bound) once the connection becomes unrecoverable (EOF, reconnect disabled) instead of busy-spinning.
- `testDiscoveryHandlerSwallowsEncodeFailure` - Asserts an INFO discovery payload that fails to JSON-encode (invalid-UTF-8 description) is swallowed inside the discovery handler so processIncoming still completes (1 frame).
- `testStartIsIdempotentWhenAlreadyStarted` - Asserts calling start() on an already-started service is a no-op, producing no additional writes/SUB frames.
- `testServiceErrorWithNullBodyIncludesCorrelationIdFromHeader` - Asserts a ServiceError thrown with a null body still emits an error payload including the correlation_id from the header plus `"code":"422"` and the `Nats-Service-Error-Code:422` header.
- `testHandlerReturningNullSendsNoReply` - Asserts a handler returning null runs but publishes no reply (no `PUB _INBOX.req`), while still incrementing num_requests to 1.
- `testDrainFiresDoneHandler` - Asserts drain() fires the onDone handler exactly once.
- `testDoneHandlerExceptionIsSwallowed` - Asserts an exception thrown by the onDone handler during stop() is swallowed (stop completes and the started flag is cleared).
- `testDiscoveryMessageWithoutReplyToIsIgnored` - Asserts a PING discovery message with no reply subject is processed (1 frame) but produces no PUB $SRV response.
- `testEndpointRequestWithNoReplyToSendsNoResponse` - Asserts a fire-and-forget endpoint request (no reply subject) still runs the handler but emits no PUB.
- `testObserverExceptionIsSwallowed` - Asserts an observer that throws does not interrupt request handling; processIncoming completes and the handler still replies (`PUB _INBOX.req` / `ok`).
- `testRunRejectsNonPositiveTimeout` - Asserts run(0.0) throws InvalidArgumentException ("timeout must be greater than zero").
- `testRunWithOnlyTimeoutUsesTimeoutCancellation` - Asserts run() with only a timeout and no external cancellation returns via the TimeoutCancellation path and stops the service (UNSUB emitted).
- `testStatsHandlerExceptionIsSwallowed` - Asserts a stats supplier that throws is swallowed: the endpoint entry is present (has num_requests) but omits the `data` key.
- `testRunBreaksImmediatelyWhenCancellationAlreadyRequested` - Asserts run() exits promptly without hanging when given an already-cancelled cancellation, still stopping the service (UNSUB emitted).
- `testStartRollbackSwallowsUnsubscribeFailureOnClosedConnection` - Asserts start() failing on a bad endpoint subject rolls back already-subscribed SIDs while swallowing the rollback unsubscribe failures (forced via failing UNSUB writes, since #116 made unsubscribe() a silent no-op on a not-open connection), leaving subscriptionSids empty and started false.
- `testDrainSwallowsUnsubscribeWriteFailure` - Asserts drain() swallows per-SID unsubscribe exceptions (forced via failing UNSUB writes on an otherwise-open connection, since #116 removed the closed-connection throw) and still clears subscriptionSids and the started flag.
- `testDrainToleratesFlushFailureOnClosedConnection` - Asserts drain() swallows a flush() failure caused by a closed connection and still clears state (started false, subscriptionSids empty).
- `testRunWithBothTimeoutAndExternalCancellationUsesCompositeCancellation` - Asserts run() given both a timeout and an external cancellation uses the CompositeCancellation branch, stopping the service when the external cancel fires first (UNSUB emitted).
- `testValidationErrorReplyPublishFailureIsContained` - Asserts a failing VALIDATION_ERROR reply publish stays contained inside the dispatch callback: the frame still completes, the handler never runs, the endpoint records one error with `last_error` "bad input", and the terminal request_end still fires (#97).
- `testStopSwallowsUnsubscribeWriteFailureAndStillFiresDone` - Asserts stop() swallows per-SID unsubscribe write failures, still clears subscriptionSids and the started flag, fires the onDone handler once, and leaves the service restartable.
- `testRunReportsASubscriptionHandlerThatThrowsAndReadsOn` - A handler of another subscription that throws while run() reads reaches the error listener ('poison handler'), the loop reads on, and on cancellation the service stops (UNSUB written) with the connection still Open. The loop's catch used to swallow it and back off.
- `testRunAnswersARequestReadBehindAMessageWhoseHandlerThrows` - A request read in the same chunk as a message whose handler throws is answered by that read ('run:hello') and the failure is reported. It used to stay queued until the server sent something else, and the service's stop() discarded it.
- `testRunDeliversTheRestOfTheFailingSubscriptionsMessages` - The failing subscription's message read behind the one that threw is delivered by the same read (['boom', 'ok']), and the failure is reported once.
- `testRunReportsAnErrAndAHandlerFailureReadTogetherAndAnswersTheRequest` - One chunk with 'maximum subscriptions exceeded', a throwing handler's message and a request: both failures are reported (in either order; the handler's is reported first, though it came second) and the request is answered. It used to stay queued behind the handler.
- `testRunReportsAHandlerThatThrowsACancelledExceptionAndServesOn` - A handler whose own bounded wait runs out throws a CancelledException while run() reads: it is reported as a CancelledException, and the request in the next chunk is answered. The loop used to take it for its own cancellation and stop, reporting nothing and leaving the request unanswered.
- `testRunReportsAHandlerThatThrowsAheadOfALineThatDoesNotParseAndServesOn` - One chunk with a throwing handler's message, a request and a line that does not parse: the handler's failure and then the parse error are reported, the request is answered, the connection reconnects (2 connects) and stays Open. The read used to throw the handler's failure after the reconnect, unreported.
- `testRunReportsAnErrTheServerKeepsTheConnectionOpenForAndServesOn` - A `-ERR 'maximum subscriptions exceeded'` read by run() reaches the error listener, the connection stays Open, and the request after it is still served. Before, the loop's catch swallowed it without a trace.
- `testRunDoesNotBackOffAfterAHandlerFails` - 25 failing messages, each in a chunk of its own, no longer slow the loop: all 25 are reported and the request behind them is answered within a 100 ms run. The loop used to back off 20 ms after each failure and reached the fifth.
- `testRunCancellationDuringReconnectBackoffStopsService` - After 'Stale Connection', with the reconnect holding its dial and waitForReconnect off, run()'s reads fail and it backs off for 20 ms. A cancel sent in the event-loop tick after the loop subscribes to its cancellation to wait for a read (which then fails, and the backoff starts) ends the loop before a timer due 10 ms after that subscribe fires, and stops the service (subscriptionSids cleared, not started). Decided by which comes first, not by elapsed time: a backoff that ignored the cancellation would let the timer fire first. Keeps the backoff covered now that handler failures no longer reach it.
- `testRunAnswersARequestReadAheadOfAFatalErrBehindAThrowingHandlerBeforeTheConnectionEnds` - Data provider (reconnect off / on): one read brings a throwing handler's message, a request and 'Stale Connection'. The handler's failure is the only report and the request is answered on the connection the server is closing (epoch 0), before it closes or reconnects. It used to stay queued: discarded with the connection, or answered on the new one.
- `testARequestAnotherFibersReadBringsBehindAThrowingHandlerIsAnsweredBeforeThatReadFails` - With handlerErrorsFailOperations, a request() in another fiber owns the read when a throwing handler's message and a service request arrive together, while run() waits for that read: the service request is answered once, on the same connection, the request() fails with 'poison handler', and nothing is reported. That read now delivers the service request before it fails (#177); before #177 run()'s read delivered it as a leftover, and before that it waited for the server's next bytes. (By default the request()'s read reports the failure and delivers the service request as well.)
- `testRunAnswersARequestTheReplayAfterAReconnectBroughtBehindAThrowingHandler` - The replay after a reconnect reads a throwing handler's message and a service request; the delivery after the reconnect reports the failure and delivers the request too, which is answered on the new connection (epoch 1) without new server bytes (#173). That delivery used to stop at the failure and leave the request to run()'s next read. Reports: the EOF, then 'poison handler'.
- `testDrainReportsAHandlerThatThrowsDuringItsFlushAndReadsOnToItsPong` - Service::drain()'s flush reads a message whose handler throws, then its PONG: the failure is reported and the PONG is read. The flush used to end with the handler's exception before its PONG, and drain() swallowed it.
- `testRunStoppedWhileAHandlerAwaitsTheSameCancellationReportsThatHandlersCancelledException` - The application's shutdown token is passed to run() and awaited inside a subscription handler; cancelling it while the handler waits stops run(), and the handler's CancelledException is reported, once. Before, it went nowhere.
- `testEndpointRejectsClassStringNotImplementingHandlerInterface` - Asserts a class-string endpoint handler that does not implement ServiceEndpointHandlerInterface is rejected at registration with a message naming both the class and the required interface, rather than being wrapped into a handler that fails at request time.

### tests/Unit/SlowConsumerErrorPolicyTest.php
SlowConsumerPolicy::Error where a subscriber that cannot keep up meets the client's own reads, over the scripted server in `tests/Support/ReconnectingTransport.php`. A reconnect (its re-subscription and its handshake), the heartbeat's read, `Service::run()`, the flushes of drain() and drainSubscription(), and every operation that reads while it waits for a result of its own report the overflow instead of failing or ending early - the operations only by default (NatsOptions::$slowConsumerErrorsFailOperations restores the old failure); the application's own read throws it once it has delivered the rest. Most tests use one of two setups: a connection with a queue limit of 3 and a handler still busy with its first message while three more wait (the reconnect and drain tests at the top), or a client with a limit of 2 whose read overflows either an "other" subscription (three messages) or a SubscriptionQueue "backlog" that nobody polls (its polling buffer is full). The tests that need something else build it themselves - for example a limit of 12 for the drain budget test, a SubscriptionQueue with a smaller buffer than its subscription, or a client with its own logger or error listener. The operations are scripted by `tests/Support/OperationsThatRead.php`, shared with HandlerFailureDuringOperationTest. Each regression test fails against the code before its fix, and each fix was reverted alone to check that its test catches it.
- `testReconnectReportsAFullQueueInsteadOfFailing` - (option off / on) The server sends on the subscription as soon as the reconnect re-subscribes it: the overflow is reported once ("Subscription queue overflow for sid N"), the first attempt succeeds (one dial, one reconnect, the SUB replayed), the subscription stays active, and the busy handler then gets m1-m4 - not the message that overflowed.
- `testFatalErrorBehindAnOverflowStillFailsTheAttempt` - The first re-subscription is answered with an overflowing message and a fatal -ERR in one read: the overflow is reported and the -ERR still fails that attempt; the second attempt succeeds. (It rejects a fix that catches the overflow around the whole read.)
- `testOverflowBeforeACorruptFrameIsReported` - The first re-subscription is answered with an overflowing message and then a corrupt control line: the corrupt line fails that attempt as before, and the overflow is reported (it used to vanish); the second attempt succeeds.
- `testDrainSubscriptionFlushReadsOnPastAnotherSubscriptionsOverflow` - (option off / on) drainSubscription()'s PING is answered by two reads: one overflowing the busy subscription's queue, then one with a message the server sent the drained subscription before its UNSUB, plus the PONG. The overflow is the only error reported, the in-flight message is delivered (it used to arrive after the subscription was removed), the subscription is removed, and the busy handler later gets m1-m4.
- `testDrainFlushReadsOnPastAnOverflow` - (option off / on) drain()'s PING is answered by two reads: one overflowing the busy subscription's queue, then one with a message the server sent another subscription before the drain's UNSUB, plus the PONG. The in-flight message is delivered (it used to be lost when the drain closed the socket), the overflow is the only error reported, the drain ends Closed with Connected, Closed, and the busy handler gets m1-m4.
- `testOperationCompletesDespiteAnotherSubscriptionsOverflow` - (request() / requestMany() / flush() / rtt() / JetStream fetchBatch() / JetStream directGetBatch() / JetStream pull consumer / Key/Value keys() / Key/Value history() / SubscriptionQueue::fetch() / SubscriptionQueue::next() / SubscriptionQueue::next() with a timeout / SubscriptionQueue::fetchAll(), each with a subscription queue overflow and with a polling buffer overflow) The read that brings the operation its result also overflows another subscription: the operation still returns its result (the reply, the fetched message, the listed key, the polled message) and the overflow is reported once. It used to fail with the overflow.
- `testOperationFailsOnAnotherSubscriptionsOverflowWhenConfiguredTo` - (the same data sets) With `slowConsumerErrorsFailOperations: true`, each fails with a SlowConsumerException naming the overflowing subscription, as before the option existed. A subscription queue's overflow is only thrown; a polling buffer's is also reported once, as it was then.
- `testACollectionThatHasReceivedPartOfItsResultStillFailsOnAnotherSubscriptionsOverflowWhenConfiguredTo` - (requestMany(max 3) / fetchBatch(3)) With `slowConsumerErrorsFailOperations: true`, the collection has received one reply or message, in a read of its own, when its next read runs into another subscription's overflow: it fails with the SlowConsumerException naming that subscription's sid, and the connection stays Open. Guard: what a collection has received is returned instead of its read's failure only when the connection is going.
- `testOverflowIsReportedOnceWhenAnotherFailureFailsTheOperationInstead` - (a fatal -ERR / a corrupt frame the connection cannot recover from, reconnecting off) With `slowConsumerErrorsFailOperations: true`, a request's read brings a message for the full queue and then the failure: the request fails with that failure, not the overflow, and the overflow is reported exactly once (it used to be reported twice: as the overflow the request was to fail with, and again when the failure took its place).
- `testOwnReadDeliversTheRestBeforeThrowingAPollingBufferOverflow` - Your processIncoming() loop holds the socket when a read brings two messages for the full queue, a message for a later subscription and another fiber's request reply: the read delivers the later message and the reply before it throws the first overflow, the second is reported, and the queue counts both drops. It used to stop at the first overflow, and the request timed out. A first request sets the reply inbox up beforehand, so that its PONG is not what the loop's one read gets.
- `testAnOverflowEscapingAHandlerIsThatHandlersFailure` - (a request's read / a request's read with handlerErrorsFailOperations / the heartbeat's read) A handler polls a SubscriptionQueue whose own subscription overflows in that poll, and lets the SlowConsumerException escape. It is that handler's failure, treated like any exception from a handler: the request whose read called the handler reports it once and gets its reply (#173), as the heartbeat reports it; with handlerErrorsFailOperations the request fails with it and it is not reported on top (it used to be taken for an overflow of the handler's subscription and reported).
- `testServiceRunReportsOverflowsEvenWhenOperationsAreConfiguredToFail` - (subscription queue / polling buffer) With `slowConsumerErrorsFailOperations: true`, Service::run()'s read brings an overflow and a request to its endpoint: the request is answered and the overflow reported once (following the option, run() swallowed it).
- `testSubscriptionQueueFetchAllKeepsWhatItCollectedWhenItFails` - fetchAll(10) takes j1 from the buffer, then reads three more messages in one read, the third overflowing its own subscription (limit 2): it fails with that overflow, and the next fetchAll(3) returns j1, j2, j3 in order (j1 used to be lost with the failed call).
- `testSubscribeQueueReportsAnEarlyOverflowAndReturnsTheQueue` - The SUB write is held up while two reads deliver three messages for the new subscription (limit 2): subscribeQueue() returns the queue, the overflow is counted (droppedCount 1) and reported once, and the queue holds j1, j2 (it used to throw out of subscribeQueue(), leaving the subscription feeding a queue nobody held).
- `testOwnReadEndingInACorruptFrameSurfacesEachOverflowOnce` - processIncoming() reads the other subscription's overflow, a message for the full queue and a corrupt control line: the subscription's overflow is thrown and the polling buffer's reported, each once (the second used to replace the first, which was neither thrown nor reported).
- `testOwnReadThrowsAFatalErrorAndReportsTheOverflowWhicheverCameFirst` - (overflow, then -ERR / -ERR, then overflow) processIncoming() reads a subscription's overflow and a fatal -ERR: it throws the -ERR's ConnectionException, not the SlowConsumerException, reports the overflow once and does not also report the -ERR (an overflow ahead of the -ERR used to be thrown in its place).
- `testOwnReadEndingInACorruptFrameItCannotRecoverFromReportsTheOverflow` - With reconnecting off, processIncoming() reads a subscription's overflow and a corrupt control line: it fails with the connection's failure, not the overflow, and the overflow it was to throw is reported once.
- `testListenerThatDrainsTheSlowSubscriptionKeepsOtherMessagesInOrder` - An error listener drains the subscription that overflowed, from inside the report, and that drain's read brings the next order: the orders arrive in wire order (x1 from the overflowing read, then x2), the request still gets its reply, and the drained subscription is gone (the report used to run before x1 was queued, and x2 overtook it).
- `testHeartbeatReadEndingInACorruptFrameReportsBothAnOverflowAndAHandlerFailure` - The heartbeat's read brings a subscription's overflow, a message whose handler throws, and a corrupt control line: both the overflow and the handler's failure are reported (the handler's failure used to hide the overflow).
- `testReconnectHandshakeReportsAnOverflowOfTheFramesBehindItsPong` - A message right behind the reconnect's handshake PONG overflows the busy subscriber's queue: it is reported and the first attempt succeeds (it used to fail the attempt - with a single attempt allowed, closing the connection).
- `testReconnectHandshakeReportsAHandlerFailureInsteadOfFailingTheAttempt` - A message left queued behind a handler that threw, and an async INFO right behind the reconnect's handshake PONG: the handshake delivers the message, the handler throws again, the failure is reported once and the first attempt succeeds (it used to fail the attempt). The drop is noticed by an operation's read, which leaves the remainder queued (#186).
- `testReconnectHandshakeFailsOnAFatalErrorAndReportsAHandlerFailure` - A fatal -ERR behind the handshake PONG while a queued message's handler throws: the -ERR fails the attempt (the second one succeeds) and the handler's failure is reported instead of standing in for the -ERR. The drop is noticed by an operation's read, which leaves the remainder queued (#186).
- `testDrainWindingDownDeliversTheBacklogPastAPollingBufferOverflow` - A drain that winds down without a connection (dials refused) delivers a later subscription's message queued behind a message for the full queue, both left queued by the application's read, which is held up in a lower sid's handler that awaits (`tests/Support/HeldUpDelivery.php`) until that later message's handler lets it go: the overflow is reported once, nothing is reported as discarded, and the held-up read ends with its 3 frames (each overflow used to end the delivery pass, and past the deadline the rest was discarded). (Built on a throwing handler before #177.)
- `testDrainKeepsToItsBudgetWhileAPollingBufferOverflows` - drain() with a 0.2 s budget whose flush brings twelve messages for a handler that blocks 50 ms each and twelve for the full queue: the slow handler gets fewer than twelve, and what is left is reported once as discarded ("drain deadline exceeded"). Each overflow used to restart the delivery, and each restart gave the slow handler one more message past the deadline.
- `testReportedOverflowReachesTheListenerDespiteAThrowingLoggerAndLogsAtErrorLevel` - A request's read reports a polling buffer's overflow: with a logger that throws on it the error listener still gets it once and the request returns its reply; with a recording logger it is logged once, at error level.
- `testKeyValueListingIsNotCutShortByThePendingBound` - (keys / history, each under Error and DropOldest) With a pending bound of 2, one read brings all three records of the listing: keys() and history() return all three (they used to come back short under DropOldest, and fail under Error).
- `testProcessIncomingThrowsAPollingBufferOverflowWithoutReportingIt` - processIncoming() delivering a message to a SubscriptionQueue whose buffer is full throws a SlowConsumerException naming the queue's subscription, does not also report it, and the queue counts the drop.
- `testHeartbeatReportsAPollingBufferOverflowAndDeliversTheRest` - (a clean read / a read ending in a corrupt frame) The heartbeat's own read brings a message for the full queue and one for another subscription: the overflow is reported once and the other message is delivered in that read (the overflow used to cut the delivery short, leaving it for the next read).
- `testHeartbeatReportsAHandlerFailureAndDeliversTheRest` - (a clean read / a read ending in a corrupt frame) The heartbeat's own read brings a message whose handler throws and one for another subscription: the failure is reported once and the other message is delivered in that read (on a clean read the failure used to vanish without a trace, and either way the other message waited for the next read).
- `testReconnectReportsAPollingBufferOverflowAndDeliversTheRestAtOnce` - Messages for the full queue and for another subscription arrive while a reconnect re-subscribes: once the reconnect is done the overflow is reported and the other message is already delivered, not left for the next read.
- `testReconnectHandshakeReportsAPollingBufferOverflowInsteadOfFailingTheAttempt` - A message for the full queue still queued from before, behind a handler that awaits in the application's read (`tests/Support/HeldUpDelivery.php`), and an async INFO right behind the reconnect's handshake PONG: the overflow is reported, the first reconnect attempt succeeds (it used to fail the attempt), and the held-up read ends with its 2 frames once its handler returns. (Built on a throwing handler before #177.)
- `testDrainFlushReadsOnPastAPollingBufferOverflow` - (drain() / drainSubscription()) The flush's first read overflows the full queue, the next brings a message still in flight for another subscription and the PONG: the overflow is reported once and the in-flight message delivered (the flush used to end at the overflow).
- `testRequestReadEndingInACorruptFrameStillGetsItsReplyPastAnOverflow` - (subscription queue / polling buffer) A request's read brings an overflow, the reply and a corrupt control line: the request returns the reply, the overflow is reported once, and the corrupt stream is recovered with a reconnect (the request used to fail with the overflow).
- `testSubscriptionQueueStillFailsOnItsOwnOverflow` - (fetch() / next() / next() with a timeout / fetchAll()) Three messages for a SubscriptionQueue's own subscription in one read (limit 2): the poll fails with a SlowConsumerException naming that subscription, and the messages that fit are still delivered by the next call.
- `testSubscriptionQueueStillFailsWhenItsOwnPollingBufferOverflows` - A SubscriptionQueue built with a smaller buffer (2) than the connection's (4) polls three of its own messages in one read, with a later subscription's message behind them: next() fails with a SlowConsumerException naming its subscription once that later message is delivered, and the next call returns j1.
- `testServiceReportsAnotherSubscriptionsOverflowAndKeepsServing` - While Service::run() reads, a request to its endpoint arrives in the same read as another subscription's overflow: the request is answered and the overflow reported (Service::run() used to swallow it without a trace).

### tests/Unit/StreamSourceTest.php
- `testMirrorMinimal` - Asserts StreamSource::mirror('ORIGIN')->toArray() yields exactly ['name' => 'ORIGIN'].
- `testMirrorWithStartSeq` - Asserts startSeq(42) on a mirror sets opt_start_seq=42 alongside the name.
- `testMirrorWithStartTime` - Asserts startTime() on a mirror sets opt_start_time to the given RFC3339 string.
- `testSourceWithFilterSubject` - Asserts StreamSource::source('ORDERS')->filterSubject('orders.>') emits name and filter_subject keys.
- `testSourceWithExternal` - Asserts external(api, deliver) emits an external sub-array with both 'api' and 'deliver' keys.
- `testExternalWithoutDeliver` - Asserts external(api) with no deliver emits an external sub-array containing only the 'api' key.
- `testFullyConfiguredSource` - Asserts a source with startSeq, filterSubject, and external(api, deliver) serializes to the full expected array with all keys including the nested external map.

### tests/Unit/SubscriptionQueueTest.php
- `testSubscribeQueueReturnsSidAndFetchesMessage` - `subscribeQueue('events')` returns a `SubscriptionQueue` with sid 1 and `fetch()` returns the message with payload 'hello' and subject 'events'.
- `testMessageDeliveredDuringSubscribeAwaitReachesTheQueue` - A message dispatched while the SUB write is still suspended (queue object not constructed yet) is buffered and reaches the returned queue instead of being silently dropped (#129).
- `testFetchReturnsNullWhenNoMessages` - `fetch()` returns null when no message is available.
- `testNextReturnsBufferedMessageImmediately` - after `processIncoming()` pre-buffers a message, `next()` returns it immediately (payload 'abc').
- `testNextReturnsNullOnTimeout` - with a 0.01s timeout and no messages, `next()` returns null.
- `testNextWithoutTimeoutRunsSingleCycleAndReturnsMessage` - with default timeout 0, `next()` runs a single processIncoming cycle and surfaces the message (payload 'xyz').
- `testNextWithoutTimeoutReturnsNullWhenEmpty` - with default timeout 0 and no messages, `next()` returns null after one empty cycle rather than blocking.
- `testFetchAllCollectsMultipleMessages` - `fetchAll()` with a 0.1s timeout collects all three messages in order ('a','b','c').
- `testFetchAllRespectsLimit` - `fetchAll(2)` collects only the first two messages ('a','b').
- `testSubscribeQueueWithQueueGroup` - `subscribeQueue('work','workers')` emits `SUB work workers 1` and `fetch()` returns 'job1'.
- `testSetTimeoutReturnsSelf` - `setTimeout(5.0)` returns the same queue instance.
- `testFetchReturnsAlreadyBufferedMessage` - after `processIncoming()` buffers a message, `fetch()` returns it directly (payload 'hi') without another read.
- `testNextWithTimeoutReturnsMessageArrivingDuringWait` - with a 0.2s timeout and no pre-buffer, `next()` breaks the bounded wait as soon as the message arrives (payload 'abc') rather than running to timeout.
- `testFetchAllWithoutTimeoutCollectsBufferedMessages` - `fetchAll()` with no timeout (null cancellation path) collects both buffered messages ('a','b').
- `testFetchAllReturnsEarlyWhenBufferedMeetsLimit` - with one pre-buffered message, `fetchAll(1)` returns early from the buffered drain alone (one message 'a').
- `testFetchDoesNotBlockOnIdleSubject` - against a transport that blocks when empty, `fetch()` returns null within a 2s bound and `lastReadHadCancellation` is true, proving the read is cancellation-bounded.
- `testNextWithDefaultTimeoutDoesNotBlockOnIdleSubject` - with default timeout 0 on a blocking transport, `next()` returns null within a 2s bound without parking the fiber.
- `testNextWithNegativeTimeoutDoesNotBlockOnIdleSubject` - with timeout -1.0 on a blocking transport, `next()` returns null within a 2s bound without blocking.
- `testFetchAllWithDefaultTimeoutDoesNotBlockOnIdleSubject` - with no setTimeout on a blocking transport, `fetchAll()` bounds its read and returns `[]` within a 2s bound rather than parking.
- `testFetchAllDoesNotBailOnTransientEmptyReadWithinTimeout` - a transient 0-frame read between two deliveries does not end `fetchAll(2)` early while timeout remains; both 'msg1' and 'msg2' are collected.
- `testEnqueueBoundsBacklogWithDropOldest` - with cap 2 and DropOldest policy, a third enqueue drops the oldest ('a'); fetchAll yields `['b','c']`.
- `testEnqueueDropsNewestWhenPolicyIsDropNewest` - with cap 2 and DropNewest policy, a third enqueue drops the newest ('c'); fetchAll yields `['a','b']`.
- `testEnqueueDropOldestIncrementsDroppedCountAndNotifiesErrorListener` - with cap 2 and DropOldest, each overflow increments `droppedCount()` monotonically (0 -> 1 -> 2) and invokes the client's errorListener with "Slow consumer on sid 99: dropped oldest message" (#134).
- `testEnqueueDropNewestIncrementsDroppedCountAndNotifiesErrorListener` - with cap 2 and DropNewest, the discarded (never-enqueued) third message increments `droppedCount()` to 1 and invokes the client's errorListener with "Slow consumer on sid 99: dropped newest message" (#134).
- `testUnsubscribeSendsUnsubForOwnSid` - `unsubscribe()` writes `UNSUB {sid}` for the queue's own sid.
- `testEnqueueThrowsOnOverflowWhenPolicyIsError` - with cap 2 and Error policy, the third enqueue throws a `SlowConsumerException` for sid 99 ("Subscription queue overflow for sid 99") and droppedCount() increments to 1; the queue does not also notify the error listener - the connection's read that delivered the message throws or reports it, so each overflow reaches the application once (#134/#159).
- `testCloseSendsUnsubForOwnSid` - `close()` (alias of unsubscribe) writes `UNSUB {sid}` for the queue's own sid.
- `testNextWithTimeoutReturnsNullWhenNoMessageArrivesBeforeDeadline` - with a 0.02s timeout on a blocking transport, the TimeoutCancellation fires inside processIncoming (CancelledException caught) and `next()` returns null.
- `testFetchAllFinalDrainCollectsConcurrentlyEnqueuedMessage` - while `fetchAll()` is suspended in processIncoming, a concurrent fiber enqueues a 'late' message; after the timeout cancels, the final drain loop collects it (one message 'late').

### tests/Unit/WaitForReconnectTest.php
Operations issued while a reconnect is in flight wait for it within one budget (`NatsOptions::$waitForReconnect`). Most tests keep a recovery owned by another fiber in flight - backing off between dials refused by `tests/Support/ReconnectingTransport.php` - and let the dials through from a timer, which fires only while the operation under test hands the event loop control.
- `testRequestDuringReconnectWaitsForItAndSucceeds` - A request issued during the reconnect waits for it, is sent once and only on the new connection, and gets its reply.
- `testRequestWithHeadersDuringReconnectWaitsForItAndSucceeds` - The same for requestWithHeaders(): the HPUB goes out once, on the new connection.
- `testRequestTimesOutWhenTheReconnectOutlastsItsTimeout` - A reconnect outlasting the request timeout fails the request after about that timeout with "Request timed out for subject svc.echo while waiting for the connection to be re-established"; the request is never sent, not even after the reconnect.
- `testRequestSharesOneTimeoutBetweenTheReconnectWaitAndTheReply` - The reconnect ends ~200 ms into a 1.2 s budget and the reply comes 1.1 s after the publish: the request times out at ~1.2 s, because one budget covers the wait and the reply; a fresh budget after the wait would have received the reply at ~1.3 s. The outcome tells the two apart, not the elapsed time.
- `testRequestJoiningASlowReplyInboxSetUpGivesUpAtItsOwnDeadline` - A request that joins another request's reply-inbox set-up, whose SUB write is held up for 300 ms, gives up at its own 100 ms deadline, while that SUB write is still stalled, with "Request timed out for subject svc.echo while waiting for the reply inbox to be set up"; the first request completes and is the only one sent.
- `testRequestSurfacesTheReconnectFailureWhenAttemptsAreExhausted` - A request waiting on a reconnect that exhausts its attempts fails with "Reconnect attempts exhausted" as soon as it gives up; the next request on the now closed connection fails at once with "Connection is not open".
- `testRequestFailsFastDuringReconnectWhenWaitingIsDisabled` - With waitForReconnect false, a request during a reconnect fails at once with "Connection is not open".
- `testRequestHonoursExternalCancellationWhileWaitingForReconnect` - The caller's own cancellation ends the wait with CancelledException, not a timeout.
- `testDisconnectEndsAWaitForReconnect` - A disconnect() during the wait ends it promptly with "Connection is not open" and leaves the connection Closed.
- `testOperationAfterDisconnectDuringAReconnectFailsAtOnce` - A request issued after disconnect() while the reconnect is still in flight (its dial cannot be stopped, so disconnect() returned at the connect timeout) fails at once with "Connection is not open" instead of waiting for that reconnect to wind down.
- `testOperationsFromAListenerInsideTheRecoveryFailFastInsteadOfDeadlocking` - request, requestMany, subscribe, flush, rtt and processIncoming awaited from a Disconnected listener (inside the recovery fiber) are each refused at once with "Connection is not open" instead of waiting on the recovery suspended in that listener, and the recovery still completes (#145).
- `testRequestManyDuringReconnectWaitsForItAndCollectsReplies` - requestMany() waits for the reconnect and collects both replies on the new connection.
- `testRequestManyTimesOutInsteadOfReturningNothingWhenTheReconnectOutlastsIt` - A reconnect outlasting requestMany()'s total timeout surfaces as TimeoutException rather than an empty collection, and nothing is sent.
- `testSubscribeDuringReconnectWaitsAndSubscribesOnceOnTheNewConnection` - subscribe() waits for the reconnect, writes exactly one SUB (on the new connection only), and the subscription delivers.
- `testSubscribeTimesOutWhenTheReconnectOutlastsTheRequestTimeout` - A reconnect outlasting requestTimeoutMs fails subscribe() with 'Subscribe to "updates" timed out waiting for the connection to be re-established' and leaves nothing behind: no SUB is written, not even by the replay once reconnected.
- `testFlushDuringReconnectWaitsForTheNewConnectionsPong` - flush() waits for the reconnect and completes on the new connection's PONG.
- `testFlushTimesOutWhenTheReconnectOutlastsTheRequestTimeout` - A reconnect outlasting requestTimeoutMs fails flush() after about that budget with "Flush timed out waiting for the connection to be re-established".
- `testFlushSharesOneTimeoutBetweenTheReconnectWaitAndThePong` - The reconnect ends ~200 ms into flush's 1.2 s budget and the server answers the flush's PING 1.1 s after it: flush() times out at ~1.2 s, before that PONG, where a fresh budget after the reconnect would have received it.
- `testFlushWriteAndPongPhasesShareOneTimeout` - Without any reconnect, a 200 ms PING write and a PONG the server sends 200 ms after it: flush() times out at ~300 ms, before the PONG (one budget), where a budget per phase would have received it.
- `testRttDuringReconnectExcludesTheWaitFromTheMeasurement` - rtt() waits ~100 ms for the reconnect but reports only the round trip: the time it reports leaves out that wait.
- `testRttTimesOutWhenTheReconnectOutlastsTheRequestTimeout` - A reconnect outlasting requestTimeoutMs fails rtt() with "RTT measurement timed out waiting for the connection to be re-established".
- `testProcessIncomingDuringReconnectWaitsThenReadsFromTheNewConnection` - processIncoming() waits for the reconnect, then reads a message the server delivers to a replayed subscription on the new connection once the reconnect is over.
- `testProcessIncomingWaitForReconnectIsBoundedByItsCancellation` - processIncoming()'s wait ends with CancelledException when its own cancellation fires while the reconnect is still failing.
- `testReadersWaitingForReconnectLeaveTheHandshakeToTheRecovery` - Three readers waiting for a reconnect never touch the new socket before it is Open: the first accepted dial completes the reconnect (no INFO/PONG stolen from the handshake), and the readers then read from the new connection (#148).
- `testAServingReadDeliveringWhatAnotherReadHasNotReachedLeavesTheHandshakeToTheRecovery` - A serving loop's read (readIncomingForOperation() with alwaysReport) delivers a message queued behind a handler that awaits in the application's processIncoming() (`tests/Support/HeldUpDelivery.php`); that message's handler awaits 50 ms while the connection drops and another fiber's read reconnects (CONNECT write held 100 ms, one attempt allowed). The reconnect succeeds on its first attempt (2 dials, Connected/Disconnected/Reconnected, only the EOF reported), the serving read then reads the new connection (#148), and the held-up read ends with its 2 frames once its handler returns. Reading at once would take the handshake's PONG ("Expected PONG after CONNECT"), and the connection would close. (Built on a throwing handler before #177.)
- `testYourReadContinuingARemainderLeavesTheHandshakeToTheRecovery` - o1's handler throws and leaves o2 queued; your next processIncoming() continues with o2 before it takes the socket, and o2's handler holds up (`tests/Support/HeldUpDelivery.php`) while the connection drops and another fiber's read reconnects, its CONNECT write held (one attempt allowed). The handler returns during the handshake and the write is let go after it: the reconnect succeeds on its first attempt (2 dials, Connected/Disconnected/Reconnected, only the EOF reported), and your read then reads o3 from the new connection (#148, #186). Decided by the order of events, with no timer. Reading at once would take the handshake's PONG, and the reconnect would give up ("Reconnect attempts exhausted"). On 2.19.0 the read took the socket at once with o2 queued.
- `testInFlightRequestWhoseReadFailsReturnsAtItsOwnDeadline` - A request whose read fails because the heartbeat started the recovery (hung server, dials refused) gives up at its own 1.2 s deadline instead of waiting for the whole recovery.
- `testInFlightRequestWhoseReadHitsACorruptChunkReturnsAtItsOwnDeadline` - The same when the request's read gets a corrupt chunk - the old socket's last bytes, read while the heartbeat's recovery closes it (dials refused): the request gives up at its own 1.2 s deadline while the recovery backs off; it then reconnects once dials are accepted (the request used to wait for the whole reconnect).
- `testSynchronousCallerDrivesAHeartbeatStartedReconnect` - The reported stall: the heartbeat notices a hung server and recovers in its own fiber; a caller that only issues requests and sleeps without touching the event loop gets its first request answered after the reconnect, instead of "Connection is not open" for good.
- `testBufferedPublishesLetASynchronousPublisherDriveTheReconnect` - A publisher that only buffers publishes (sleeping in between) drives the background reconnect to Open, and every buffered publish is flushed on the new connection, in order.
- `testBufferedPublishesDoNotYieldWhenWaitingIsDisabled` - With waitForReconnect false, buffered publishes do not yield, so the reconnect stays Connecting until something waits on the event loop; then all 30 buffered frames are flushed.
- `testDrainDuringReconnectWaitsThenFlushesBufferedPublishesAndCloses` - drain() during a reconnect waits for it: the reconnect flushes the two publishes buffered during the outage, then drain writes its UNSUB on the new connection and closes, after exactly one reconnect.
- `testDrainSharesOneTimeoutBetweenTheReconnectWaitAndItsFlush` - The reconnect ends ~200 ms into drain's 1.2 s budget and the server answers the drain's PING 1.1 s after it: the drain closes at ~1.2 s, before that PONG, its flush getting only what the wait left of the one budget; a fresh budget for the flush would have waited for it.
- `testDrainThatRunsOutOfTimeWaitingForTheReconnectStillClosesAndReportsWhatItDiscards` - A reconnect outlasting drain's 150 ms budget: drain still ends Closed after about the budget, reports the discarded buffered publish ("Drain ran out of time waiting for the reconnect: N bytes of buffered publishes were discarded") and the message stuck behind a handler that stays busy until the drain is over ("drain deadline exceeded: 1 buffered message(s) were not delivered before close"), and the stopped reconnect does not reopen the connection once dials are accepted again.
- `testDrainDuringReconnectClosesAndThrowsWhenWaitingIsDisabled` - With waitForReconnect false, drain() during a reconnect closes the connection at once and throws "Cannot drain while reconnecting: the connection was closed instead" (nats.go parity), reports the discarded buffered publish, and the reconnect does not reopen the connection.
- `testDrainFromADisconnectedListenerClosesInsteadOfDeadlocking` - drain() awaited from a Disconnected listener (inside the reconnect) closes the connection at once and throws the same error, and the reconnect - which would otherwise succeed straight away - stops before dialling.
- `testDrainWaitingForAReconnectThatGivesUpEndsClosed` - A reconnect exhausting its attempts while drain() waits leaves the connection Closed and drain() resolves, while the reader that ran the recovery gets "Reconnect attempts exhausted".
- `testDrainWaitingForAReconnectEndsWhenTheUserDisconnects` - A disconnect() during drain()'s wait cuts the reconnect's 2 s backoff short, so the drain ends well within 1 s with the connection Closed and one Closed event.
- `testSecondDrainWhileTheFirstWaitsForAReconnectFailsAtOnce` - A second drain() while the first waits for the reconnect fails at once with "Connection is not open", like any second drain, while the reconnect is still in flight; the first drains the reopened connection.
- `testDrainAfterDisconnectDuringAReconnectThrowsWithNothingToDrain` - drain() after disconnect() during a reconnect fails at once with "Connection is not open", even while the reconnect the disconnect stopped is still winding down (its dial cannot be stopped, so disconnect() returned at the connect timeout).
- `testJetStreamPublishDuringReconnectWaitsForTheAck` - A JetStream publish during a reconnect waits for it and returns the PubAck (stream ORDERS, seq 7), sent on the new connection only.
- `testJetStreamFetchDuringReconnectWaitsThenFetches` - fetchBatch() during a reconnect waits in its subscribe, then pulls and returns the message through the new connection.
- `testJetStreamFetchLosingItsConnectionMidFetchEndsByItsDeadline` - A fetch whose connection hangs mid-fetch (the heartbeat recovers in its own fiber, dials refused) ends by its own deadline with the 408 "no messages" result instead of waiting for the recovery.

- `testQueuePolledWithoutATimeoutDuringAReconnectReturnsNothing` - (fetch() / next() without a timeout / fetchAll() without a timeout) A SubscriptionQueue polled while a reconnect is backing off returns nothing (null, or []) once its short non-blocking wait is over (well within 1 s) instead of failing with "Connection is not open", and the reconnect then completes.
- `testQueuePolledWithATimeoutDuringAReconnectWaitsForIt` - (next() / fetchAll()) With a 2 s timeout, the poll waits for the reconnect, then reads on the new connection and returns the message the server sends there (j1), after exactly one reconnect.
- `testQueuePolledDuringAReconnectThatGivesUpFailsWithItsError` - A queue poll waiting for a reconnect that runs out of attempts fails with "Reconnect attempts exhausted", and the connection ends Closed.
### tests/Unit/WebSocketFrameCodecTest.php
- `testEncodeMaskedFrameRoundTrips` - Encodes a masked client binary frame and asserts the header bytes (0x82, mask bit + length 6), then decode() returns one frame with the right opcode, payload "PING\r\n", fin=true, and an emptied buffer.
- `testAcceptKeyMatchesRfcExample` - Asserts acceptKey() produces the exact Sec-WebSocket-Accept value from the RFC 6455 §1.3 sample nonce.
- `testDecodeKeepsIncompleteTrailingFrame` - Feeding all-but-last byte yields no frames and an untouched buffer; appending the final byte then decodes the full "hello" frame and clears the buffer.
- `testDecodeReturnsMultipleFrames` - Concatenates three frames (binary, ping, binary) and asserts decode() returns all three with correct payloads and the ping opcode.
- `testDecodeExtended16BitLengthFrame` - Encodes a 300-byte payload, asserts the 16-bit length marker (126) is used, and decode() returns the full payload.
- `testDecodeReportsFragmentationFlag` - Hand-crafts a non-final (FIN=0) binary frame and asserts decode() reports fin=false with payload "abc".
- `testDeflateInflateRoundTrip` - Asserts deflate() shrinks a repetitive payload (output differs and is shorter) and inflate() restores the original exactly.
